- **F. GitHub deploys through GitHub Actions: DONE 23 Oct, fixture-verified; not run live** (records 77–78). No Cloudflare GitHub app, no build token.
  - **Linking:** `POST /api/projects/:id/github-actions {repository, confirm: true}` (`worker/github-actions.ts`) commits `.github/workflows/shipcel.yml` to the default branch with the person's GitHub token (Contents API). A workflow already there is kept. When GitHub refuses (403/404, or a protected branch's 409/422, which `providerJSON` turns into 502), the link is still recorded and the answer has GitHub's new-file page with the workflow filled in (`addUrl`). For a 403 or 404 it also tells, from the installations the person can see, whether Shipcel's app isn't installed on the owner or wasn't given the repository, with GitHub's page for that (`installUrl`, record 97). `GET` returns the link and the workflow text; `DELETE` removes the link and revokes its runs' tokens, and leaves the repository alone. A project Cloudflare's builds deploy is refused; one repository links to one project. Migration 0035: `github_actions` (`project_id` PK, `repository_id` UNIQUE, `owner_id`, `repository`, `branch`, `user_id`) and `api_tokens.project_id` / `preview_only`.
  - **Each run:** `shipcel deploy` in GitHub Actions (`GITHUB_ACTIONS=true` and no Shipcel token) asks GitHub for an OIDC token with the Shipcel origin as audience and sends it to `POST /api/github-actions/token` (unauthenticated, 60 a minute per IP). `worker/github-oidc.ts` checks RS256 against GitHub's JWKS (kept an hour; an unknown key ID fetches again at most once a minute), `iss`, `aud` exactly this origin, `exp`/`iat`/`nbf` with 60 s of skew, a lifetime of at most an hour, and `jti` once (a `request_limits` row). Then only branch refs, only `push` and `workflow_dispatch`, the link by `repository_id`, the same `repository_owner_id`, and a linker who is still owner or developer. The answer is an API token for the linker that lasts an hour and reaches that project only, preview-only for a branch other than the linked one; the CLI deploys with `--project`, `--yes` and `--target` from it (`--prod`/`--publish` cleared).
  - **What a run's token reaches** (`runTokenAllows`, `worker/tokens.ts`): `GET /api/me`, `/api/connections`, `/api/connections/cloudflare/subdomain`, `/api/projects` (its project only), `/api/projects/<id>/deployments`; `POST /api/local-plan`, `/api/projects/<id>/local-plan`, `/local-deploys`, `/local-deploys/<uuid>/finish`, `/uploads`, `/uploads/<uuid>/files`, `/uploads/<uuid>/deploy`. Not listed in `GET /api/tokens`; revoked on disconnect or project deletion; deleted a day after expiry. Preview-only: no upload session before the project has its Worker, an upload deploy must be a preview of the existing Worker, and a Wrangler deploy can't be the first or a production one (`previewOnlyRun`).
  - **A Shipcel on someone's own computer:** GitHub's runners can't reach it. `/api/connections` says `githubActions: false`, the link route refuses (`localShipcel`), `git connect` stops before creating anything, and the dashboard keeps the Cloudflare-builds layout of before.
  - **Dashboard:** Deployments → **Deploy from GitHub** (`src/GitHubDeploy.tsx`): the repository, the workflow it adds, consent, **Connect repository**, then **Runs on GitHub**, **The workflow**, **Disconnect repository** (confirmed), and a refusal with **Add it on GitHub**. Cloudflare's builds fold into **Build with Cloudflare instead, for containers, queues, routes and Python packages** and ask Cloudflare nothing until opened; a project they deploy shows only them. Connections: two steps (GitHub; Cloudflare with an account), and **Cloudflare's own builds (optional)** folded, checked once opened.
  - **CLI:** `git connect` links through Actions (asks first, prints the commit and the runs, suggests `git pull`); `--cloudflare-builds` is the earlier flow; `git ls` names the link; `git disconnect` is new.
  - **GitHub App:** the manifest asks Contents and Workflows read and write, Metadata read. The owner's existing app needs them added (Owner next, step 2).
  - **Tests:** `tests/github-actions.test.ts` (37), `tests/helpers/github-oidc.ts` (a key pair, the JWKS, a token signer), CLI tests (Actions connect, ls, disconnect, refusal, local Shipcel, deploy in Actions: production, preview, missing id-token, unlinked repository, `SHIPCEL_TOKEN` first), 3 browser journeys in `connect.spec.ts`, the manifest tests. 75 mutations caught (`scratchpad/mutate-actions.mjs` 69, `scratchpad/mutate-ui.mjs` 6).

- **B. The Security panel (recommendation 1): DONE 23 Oct** (record 72). Fixture-verified only: **not yet run against a live zone.**
  - **What it shows**, for each zone of the app's custom domains (from `${root}/workers/domains`, filtered by `service === worker_name`), at `GET /api/projects/:id/security`:
    - Cloudflare's Free Managed Ruleset: an enabled rule in phase `http_request_firewall_managed` that executes `77454fe2d30c4220b5701f6fdfb893ba`.
    - Bot Fight Mode and AI crawler blocking (`/zones/:z/bot_management`: `fight_mode`, `ai_bots_protection`).
    - Under Attack mode (`/zones/:z/settings/security_level`).
    - Custom rules (`http_request_firewall_custom`) and rate limits (`http_ratelimit`).
    - Whether the app still answers on workers.dev.
  - **Changes, each confirmed, developer or owner only:**
    - `PUT …/security {managed?, bots?, aiBots?, underAttack?}`.
    - `POST …/security/rules {action: block|challenge, match: country|ip|path, values}`. The expression is built only from checked values and limited to the app's host names (`http.host in {…}`).
    - `DELETE …/security/rules/:rule`, for rules whose description carries `[app <project id>]` only.
    - `PUT|DELETE …/security/rate-limit {path, requests}`, in Free's shape: `cf.colo.id` + `ip.src`, a 10 s period and a 10 s block. The expression matches the path alone, so it covers every host name in the zone.
  - **Decisions:**
    - Turning the managed ruleset off disables Cloudflare's rule and keeps it.
    - Under Attack off sets `medium`, and only when the level is `under_attack`.
    - A phase with no entry point gets one, through `POST /zones/:z/rulesets`.
    - Cloudflare's reason for refusing a change is passed on (`errorDetail`), and a 403 names the missing permission.
    - Anything the connection can't read comes back as `null`, with the permission listed in `denied`.

  - **Outcome (23–24 Oct, record 83): the owner deployed it (`https://bittensor-rpc.takumisolutionsmv.workers.dev/`, `RPC_KEY` set), and it doesn't run.** `shipcel logs --since 1h --errors` showed "Durable Object's isolate exceeded its memory limit and was reset." again and again from 14:38 to 14:47 UTC on 23 Oct, between the cron's `POST https://light-client/heartbeat` lines. It restarts with each alarm (30 s) and cron (1 min) and goes over each time. **Cause, measured in the VM's local runtime (Wrangler 4.152.0, workerd, arm64), not on Cloudflare:** V8's GC trace (`MINIFLARE_WORKERD_V8_FLAGS="--trace-gc-verbose"`) reports "External memory": smoldot's Wasm memory plus V8's estimate for smoldot's compiled Wasm code. It doesn't drop after full GCs. The JS heap is only 5–10 MB beside it. With lazy compilation, V8's default, external memory was 12.5 MB at smoldot's start, 27 MB during warp sync, 76.7 MB once Finney's runtime was compiled (Wasm memory 42.5 MB), and **140 MB after one `state_getMetadata`** (Wasm memory 79.2 MB). With `--no-wasm-lazy-compilation`, it was 50.6 MB at start and 114.8 MB at runtime-ready (109.9 MB from a saved state). smoldot itself reports 18 MiB in use, but Wasm memory never shrinks. Restarts from a minutes-old saved state peaked at 59.7 MB of Wasm memory, against 42.5 MB from the checkpoint. Cloudflare counts "the JavaScript heap and WebAssembly allocations" against 128 MB per isolate. **Ruled out locally:** V8's optimising tier (`--no-wasm-tier-up` grew the same), connection count (a cap of 4 left the 59.6 MB peak unchanged and made smoldot redial ~4 times a second), and a second smoldot instance (no "light client stopped" in the logs). **Not confirmed on Cloudflare:** the figure itself (dashboard → Durable Objects → the namespace → Metrics → **Memory usage**, from `durableObjectsPeriodicGroups.quantiles.memoryUsageBytesP50…P999`), and whether Cloudflare compiles Wasm up front. **Conclusion:** smoldot doesn't fit in a Durable Object once it compiles and runs Finney's runtime. No setting changes that: its runtime compilation is already lazy (`CompileWithNonDeterministicValidation` → wasmi `Lazy`). **README and CLI guide corrected** (status at the top, "Memory" under Limits, Evidence); the light client is local-only until the owner decides. **Decision pending (asked 24 Oct):** smoldot in a Cloudflare Container behind this Worker, with the key, rate limit, cache and history staying in the Durable Object. Cost: the "basic" instance (1 GiB, ¼ vCPU) always on is about $7.40 a month beyond Workers Paid (memory $6.35, disk $0.69, CPU about $0.34 at smoldot's 0.01–0.02 cores). Sleeping when idle costs cents, but the first call after a sleep waits for a start and a sync. "lite" (256 MiB, 1/16 vCPU, about $2.10) is likely too small: smoldot under Node used 133–168 MB at sync on 22 Oct, before any metadata. A container deploys only from GitHub with Cloudflare's builds (`worker/local-deploys.ts` refuses it from the CLI), so it needs Cloudflare's GitHub app and a build token once. The VM has no Docker: the Node part can be tested there, the image can't.
  - **Decided and done (24 Oct, record 86): smoldot made to fit the Durable Object.** The owner: "We cant make it work with workers paid? Which was the whole point of presenting our shipcel app", then chose "Try to fit the Durable Object". **The cause, found by measuring:** V8 counts a Wasm memory in steps (32, 64, 128 MiB; a bare `WebAssembly.Memory` in a test Worker), so smoldot's 75 MiB after one metadata call counted as 128 MiB. smoldot's live data was only 18–36 MiB; four transient peaks made the rest, and Wasm memory never shrinks. **Four new smoldot patches** (`examples/bittensor-light-client/smoldot/0004`–`0007`, explained in its README):
    - **0004:** the warp-synced runtime is registered among the compiled runtimes, so a JSON-RPC runtime call no longer compiles a duplicate (16 MiB decompression and a second module).
    - **0005:** zstd decoding in 1 MiB steps, twice (count, then fill an exact buffer), instead of a 16 MiB doubling buffer plus a copy.
    - **0006:** the database save leaves out runtime code that can't fit before encoding it (7 MiB at once per save before).
    - **0007:** legacy runtime calls take the runtime of recent blocks from the runtime service instead of downloading `:code` (2.6 MiB) for every new block.
    - **Rejected after measuring:** growing a call's memory up front (no gain), capping connections (no gain; redials four times a second), and V8 flags (not ours to set on Cloudflare).
  - **Result, the VM's local runtime, live Finney (24 Oct):**
    - Wasm memory: 31 MiB from a saved state (57 before), 31.8–32.5 MiB from the checkpoint (40.5), 38–40 MiB after the metadata, neurons, dynamic info and 22 concurrent calls (75.5).
    - V8 external memory: peak 73–75 MiB (140 before), or 110.6 MiB compiled up front; from a saved state 43.3 MiB, or 80.2 MiB compiled up front.
    - Tests: `scripts/check.mjs` all identical to a full node; smoldot's suites 490 + 82 passed; the new zstd test caught 3 mutations; two fresh-clone builds byte-identical (5,533,224 bytes, sha256 `d65552dd…9d28`).
    - Rust 1.93.1 is now in the VM (`~/.cargo`, `~/.rustup`, installed with `--no-modify-path`). The work tree with the patches applied is `scratchpad/smoldot-work.HM43/smoldot` (session-only).
  - **JavaScript:** `src/index.js` logs each new high of Wasm memory from the alarm (`[light-client] Wasm memory: … MiB`). README, CLI guide and QA report updated; site rebuilt.
  - **Not yet:** run on Cloudflare (Owner next, step 8). If Cloudflare compiles Wasm up front, 110.6 MiB plus a 5–10 MiB JS heap leaves only about 10 MiB of margin.
  - **Deployed 24 Oct (record 87): it still reset**, each start 12–25 s in (log lines at 21:23–21:28 UTC, 23 Oct). Only one memory line, 2.6 MiB at the start, made it out: logs of the requests a reset cuts short are lost. **Reproduced locally in Cloudflare's order:** the alarm or cron starts the object, so the state save and the history fill ran during the sync. A restart from a saved state then reached 32.6–32.8 MiB, just past V8's 32 MiB step, so it counts as 64: 112.4 MiB compiled up front, plus about 10 MiB of heap. Cloudflare must count a little more than the local runtime. **Done:**
    - **Patch 0008** (`0008-compile-the-runtime-once-from-a-stream.patch`): a compressed runtime compiled lazily by the interpreter is decompressed as a stream, never whole: once for its two version sections, then straight into wasmi's streaming parser. It is built once, unresolved imports allowed (`HostVmPrototype::unresolved_imports` for the same warning); a strict build had failed on Finney's BLS12-381 import, so it was built twice. wasmi ignores custom sections (−1 MiB of debug names). Start: 25.1–25.2 MiB, so V8's count is 43 MiB, or 80 MiB compiled up front.
    - **0005** reformatted (its test, with rustfmt; no change to the Wasm).
    - **JavaScript:** `src/memory.js` (`MemoryTrace`) records Wasm memory in SQLite every 2 s for 3 minutes after each start. The next start logs the previous one's peak, and `/status` has `previousStart`. Save and fill wait `SETTLE_MS` (2 minutes) after a start.
    - **Tests:** 5 new in smoldot (streamed runtime same version, byte-identical `Core_version` and `Metadata_metadata`; custom sections from a stream; reader limits; unresolved imports listed); 3 trace tests in the example. smoldot 495 + 82 passed; Shipcel 880 passed.
    - **Build:** two fresh-clone builds byte-identical, 5,561,372 bytes, sha256 `47f5b214d7dff29d6762b5270421187bd814a596b3b5ae524650458bc3bb44f9`. `check.mjs`: all identical to a full node; 22 concurrent calls all succeeded.
  - **Deployed again 24 Oct (record 88): the eight-patch build still reset**, each start dying within about 4 s. Its trace: "The previous start reached 3.6 MiB of Wasm memory, 2 s in; its last sample was at 2 s", while that start logged "Successfully compiled runtime" at 3.7 s. So the object dies, or its timers stop, right at the runtime compile, with smoldot at a quarter of the limit or less. Cloudflare must count roughly 90 MiB besides smoldot's memory, most likely V8's compiled form of smoldot's 5.5 MB module (code section 3.9 MiB over 20,626 functions, with no large removable part; parachain support is 35 KiB). **Asked:** Container (recommended), one more diagnostic, or stop. **Chosen:** "One more diagnostic deploy first". **Done:** the finer trace: samples from the moment a start begins, every 250 ms for 20 s and on each network chunk (`workersPlatform` `onActivity`, `stats()`) and each smoldot log line, then every 2 s; each with bytes received, connections and smoldot's last log line. The next start logs the dead start's peak and last six samples. Tests: 37 in the example's file, 880 in all. Wasm unchanged (`47f5b214…`).
  - **The diagnostic's result (read on the Mac on 28 Oct with `shipcel logs --since 1h`; only the `[light-client]` lines were printed, since request URLs carry the key):** production version `bcd7f9d5` (deployed 24 Oct; the folder matches the repository's example, Wasm `47f5b214…`). It still resets about every 30 s: 14 resets between 09:29 and 09:36 UTC. Each start's last samples, every 250 ms: 2.50 s 3.6 MiB (0.03 MB received, 4 connections), 2.75 s 3.6, 3.00 s 4.1 (0.18 MB), 3.25 s 4.6 (0.41 MB), 3.50 s 7.8 (1.77 MB), 3.75 s **25.1 MiB** (2.72 MB, about Finney's compressed runtime). Then nothing. "Finalized block runtime ready" appears in 5 of the starts. So it dies as the runtime is decompressed and compiled, with smoldot's memory under V8's 32 MiB step. That is the outcome step 8 said rules out a Durable Object: Cloudflare counts roughly 100 MB besides it, most likely the compiled form of smoldot's module and the runtime. Each restart downloads about 2.7 MB from public Finney nodes again, about 8 GB a day.
  - **Still open:** runtime calls take smoldot to 37.6 MiB, V8's 64 MiB step: 76.5 MiB, or about 111 compiled up front. Most of it is the runtime's own memory during a call (4.2 MiB in a `Vec` that doubles, 7 MiB at once with a copy) and translated code wasmi keeps (about 3 MiB). Runtime upgrades, four in two days, will reach that step anyway, since two runtimes are held for a while. Whether the 64 MiB step fits on Cloudflare is what the next deploy measures. If not, the next options are exact growth for the runtime's memory (a patched wasmi, vendored and checksum-verified by `build.sh`) and a smaller smoldot. Measured and rejected: growing a call's memory up front.

**Newest finished (22 Oct 2026, in the VM): old block bodies are checked too** (record 68).

- **The check:** `chain_getBlock` at an old block needs a header that hashes to the block's hash, and extrinsics that rebuild the header's extrinsics root (`proofs.extrinsicsRoot`, `trieRoot` in `src/proof.js`; `#block` in `src/history.js`). Justifications aren't checked, so they come back as `null`.
- **Found:** Finney builds that root as **state version 0** (Core API below 5), over the extrinsics exactly as JSON-RPC gives them, while its state is version 1. Version 1 is accepted too, for later.
- **Tests:** 34 in the example's file, with `tests/fixtures/finney-blocks.json` holding blocks #9,000,000 and #9,339,090. 18 more mutations, all caught.
- **Run locally against the archive node:** identical to its blocks.
- **Gate in the VM:**
  - TypeScript, Prettier and the three build checks.
  - `npm test`: 798 tests in 34 files, twice in a row.
  - The browser suite wasn't rerun, since Cloudcel's dashboard didn't change.

**Earlier (22 Oct 2026, in the VM): the light client has a verified archive and a per-block cache** (record 67). It was run against live Finney and Opentensor's public archive node in the local runtime; nothing was deployed. The details are in the slice "Bittensor light client example" (`src/history.js`, `src/proof.js`, `src/blake2b.js`, the router), the example's README ("Old blocks", "Answers kept") and the QA report (22 October, first entry).

- **What it does:**
  - **The record:** every finalized block (number, hash, parent, state root) goes into the Durable Object's SQLite, and each alarm backfills `HISTORY_DAYS` (7) from the archive node, 1,000 blocks per alarm, each header checked against its child's parent hash. `chain_getBlockHash` answers from it; smoldot answers only for its best block.
  - **Old blocks:** a block more than 200 behind the best, or one smoldot never imported, goes to the archive node (`ARCHIVE_URL`; `off` for none). Storage and headers there are checked by proof or hash. Everything else comes back marked `unverified`, and what doesn't check out is an error.
  - **The cache:** calls about one block are pinned (latest → best), shared while running and kept (4 MB, LRU, 512 KB per answer, no errors).
- **Why not leave old blocks to smoldot:** smoldot's p2p fetches of old blocks work only with an archive peer connected, and fail after 4 to 9 s otherwise.
- **Tests:** 31 in the example's file (13 new), with real fixtures from block #9,000,000 in `tests/fixtures/finney-9000000.json`. 44 mutations, all caught.
- **Still to measure on Cloudflare:** the cache's memory beside smoldot's 81 MB, and that Opentensor's node keeps serving us.
- **Gate in the VM:**
  - TypeScript, Prettier and the three build checks; the site was rebuilt for one changed sentence in the CLI guide.
  - `npm test`: 795 tests in 34 files, twice in a row.
  - The browser suite wasn't rerun, since Cloudcel's dashboard and Worker didn't change.

**Earlier (22 Oct 2026, in the VM): the reconnect bug is fixed, and the light client has a key and a rate limit** (record 62). Nothing was deployed and nothing ran against Cloudflare. The evidence is fixtures and the local runtime (QA report, 22 October, first entry).

- **Reconnect.** The code: `worker/auth.ts` (`exchange`, `refreshFailed`, `grantToken`) and `worker/providers.ts` (`providerJSON`'s `oauth` option, `OAuthError`, `oauthError`).
  - **What ends a connection:** only the provider's refusal of the refresh token itself. That's `invalid_grant`, or GitHub's `bad_refresh_token`, which GitHub sends with a 200. The grant is marked `reconnect_required`, its tokens are wiped, and the answer is 409: "Cloudflare no longer accepts this connection (invalid_grant). Connect Cloudflare again."
  - **What keeps it:** anything else. The refresh lock is released, so the next call tries again.
    - No answer, a 5xx, a 429, a garbled answer, or a 400 without an OAuth error: 503 ("…The connection is kept: try again in a minute.").
    - `invalid_client`, or another refusal of Cloudcel's own sign-in app: 502, naming the code.
  - **No 401 any more.** The CLI reads 401 as "Your Cloudcel token is no longer valid" (`packages/cli/src/core.ts`, `failure`). A 200 without tokens or an OAuth error now answers 502; it was 401.
  - **Tests:** written first (`tests/security.test.ts`, "keeps the connection when a refresh fails…"); 11 mutations caught.
  - **It doesn't restore the owner's grant,** whose tokens were wiped on 15 Oct. (a) is still needed once.
- **The light client is WebSocket only now** (record 66). JSON-RPC over HTTP gets 405 with the WebSocket's address. The Worker answers `/` itself (the page, the description, the 405), so none of these reaches the Durable Object. The page asks for its status over its WebSocket (`lightclient_status`), and `scripts/check.mjs` checks over WebSocket. Run in workerd against live Finney (QA report, 22 October, first entry).
- **The light client's key and rate limit:** the slice "Bittensor light client example" (`src/access.js`) and the example's README ("Keep it to yourself").
- **Gate in the VM:**
  - TypeScript, Prettier, and the three build checks. The public site was rebuilt for one new sentence in the CLI guide.
  - `npm test`: 782 tests in 34 files, three runs in a row. `npm run test:browser`: 65 journeys.
  - One early full run had one failing test that wasn't captured; 16 full runs since passed. Probably a flake, but unidentified.
- **Seen locally, not caused by this change:** workerd logs "Uncaught Error: Network connection lost." for each WebSocket a Node client closes before leaving. A bare Worker that accepts WebSockets the same way logged the same 8 errors for the same client. On Cloudflare these may appear in Workers Logs. Answering a client's close with `server.close()` might stop them; untested.

**Earlier (22 Oct 2026, on the Mac): a Bittensor light client as Cloudcel's first demo, `examples/bittensor-light-client`** (the owner: "Lets do this / I need a real light client for bittensor"). Built and run locally in workerd against live Finney peers. **Not deployed yet:** the owner approved the deploy on 22 Oct. It is waiting for them to reconnect Cloudflare: the workspace's grant is `reconnect_required` (see "What's next", first item). It needs Workers Paid. How it works: smoldot 3.7.0 runs in one Durable Object, compiled to WebAssembly with three small patches (two to follow Finney, one for a security advisory in its zstd decoder), and reaches peers over TCP (`cloudflare:sockets`). It warp-syncs from the checkpoint in Subtensor's spec (#8,867,448), follows GRANDPA finality, and answers JSON-RPC over HTTP and WebSocket. Locally, its storage reads and Bittensor runtime calls matched a full node byte for byte at the same block. Full details are in the slice "Bittensor light client example" below and the QA report (22 October).

- **The one non-obvious fact:** the spec checkpoint's GRANDPA set is 5, but smoldot needs **6**. The checkpoint block enacts a change without delay, and its own justification verifies with set 5, later ones with set 6. With 5, every warp-sync proof failed with `BadSignature`.
- **To deploy** (approved by the owner on 22 Oct; once Cloudflare is reconnected): start local Cloudcel (`preview_start` `cloudcel-dev`, or `npm run dev`), then `cp -R examples/bittensor-light-client ~/Downloads/bittensor-light-client`, `cd` there, `node /Users/zayaan_1/Downloads/Cloudcel/packages/cli/dist/cloudcel.js deploy --prod`, and run `node scripts/check.mjs https://bittensor-light-client.takumisolutionsmv.workers.dev`.
- **What to measure live:**
  - Memory against the 128 MB limit. `/status` reports Wasm memory, which was 42–78 MB locally.
  - CPU. Workers Paid gives a Durable Object 30 s, renewed by each request; the cron sends one every minute, and smoldot used about 0.01 of a core locally.
  - Whether outbound TCP to peers works from Cloudflare, and the WebSocket boot node on port 30333.
  - Two documented restrictions workerd didn't enforce locally. Cloudflare's TCP sockets page says sockets "cannot be created in global scope or shared across requests"; the light client opens them in one request and uses them in later ones, which worked inside a Durable Object locally. And each open socket counts toward Workers' simultaneous-connection limit, which may cap smoldot's peers (1–7 locally).
  - The Durable Object's duration bill: about 330,000 of the 400,000 included GB-seconds a month. The same page says each open socket keeps the object in memory for up to 15 minutes.

**Checked on the Mac itself (22 Oct 2026).** This session ran in the Claude app on the owner's Mac (darwin, `/Users/zayaan_1/Downloads/Cloudcel`), not in the VM, so it can reach the owner's Cloudcel at `http://127.0.0.1:5173` whenever `npm run dev` runs there. **Earlier (14 Oct 2026): the budget guard.** After a $10,000 bill from a Durable Object alarm that kept setting itself again, the owner asked: "We need automatic alerts, safeguards, budget limits set up to ensure this never happen to our clients … what do you propose?" Decided with the owner: over a budget, both **alert only** and **pause** are offered, **alert only by default**; the default budget is **$0 beyond what Cloudflare includes**; Cloudcel asks for Cloudflare's **Billing Read** permission with the rest of the sign-in ("ask once"). Built: every five minutes Cloudcel reads each deployed project's usage from Cloudflare's GraphQL analytics, estimates its cost this month beyond what Cloudflare includes, alerts at 50/80/100% and on a rate that would go over (dashboard, a badge, and the project's webhook whatever events it chose), and, if an owner chose it, pauses the app: production switches to a stand-in version that keeps every Durable Object class (so data stays), ends alarms, answers 503 and puts off queue messages, the cron schedules are removed, production changes are refused until an owner resumes it, and a version deployed outside Cloudcel meanwhile is paused again. An owner can also **Pause now**. Budgets, pause and resume are dashboard-only for owners; `cloudcel budget [check]` reads. `npm run dev` now runs Cloudcel's minute schedule every minute too, so the guard (and the other scheduled work) runs on a local Cloudcel. Migration **0032**. Implemented, fixture-verified (27 server tests against a fake Cloudflare, CLI, token and browser tests, 60 mutations caught), the stand-in run in the real Workers runtime (a runaway alarm loop stopped, data kept); **not run live**. Details in the newest slice, "What's next" item 0, and `docs/guides/free-only-and-billing.md`.

**Earlier (13 Oct 2026, second session): level with Vercel's CLI for ease of use and features.** At the owner's request ("Continue from handoff, Ensure we are on parity with vercel in terms of ease of use and features"), after reading Vercel's current CLI reference (69 commands, vercel.com/docs/cli, last updated 7 Oct 2026): (1) **`cloudcel` deploys apps with code and bindings from the computer**: Next.js on OpenNext, SvelteKit, Astro with its adapter, Hono, any Worker with a `wrangler.jsonc`, with D1, KV and R2. Wrangler runs on the person's computer and, for that one deploy, reaches Cloudflare through Cloudcel, which checks every call; nothing is set up in Cloudflare and no Cloudflare token reaches the computer. Before this, `cloudcel` sent those apps to GitHub deployments, which need Cloudflare's GitHub app and a build token. (2) **A new app's first deploy goes to production**, as Vercel's does (`--target preview|production` says otherwise). (3) **Everyday commands**: `dev`, `env pull`/`update`, `pull`, `alias`, `crons`, `rolling-release`, `metrics`, `curl`, `httpstat`, `api`, `mcp`, `agent init`, `upgrade`, `switch`, `git ls`. (4) **More presets**: Docusaurus, VitePress, VuePress, Eleventy, Parcel, Hexo, Gridsome, Ember, Stencil, UmiJS, Preact CLI, and in the CLI Hugo, Jekyll, Zola and MkDocs. CLI **0.4.0**, migrations **0030 and 0031**. Implemented, fixture-verified (the real pinned Wrangler runs through Cloudcel against a fake Cloudflare) and mutation-checked; **not run live** (this session ran in the VM again). A security review of the new path followed (no critical finding, one high, two medium, eight low): all fixed except six smaller points that are recorded in the slice and in `docs/qa-report.md`; one of the fixes had a bug of its own that the new tests caught before it left the VM. Then the owner asked whether `cloudcel deploy --prod` creates the `wrangler.jsonc` itself: that path (a framework app with no Wrangler configuration) was run on real `create-next-app`, `create-react-router` and `sv create` apps in the VM, which found six problems, all fixed (see "A folder without a Wrangler configuration" in the slice). React Router and SvelteKit now go from a bare folder to a deploy with one question; **a new Next.js app is stopped with the two changes it needs (Next.js 16.3.8, Cache Components off), because Cloudflare's adapter can't run 16.4 yet.** **On 13 Oct the owner ran `npm run db:migrate` on the Mac (0029, 0030 and 0031 applied), started the server, and, after one attempt in the wrong folder, ran `cloudcel deploy --prod` in a new React Router app's folder: the CLI set the app up, built it and deployed it live to `https://rr-test.takumisolutionsmv.workers.dev/` (checked from outside: the page, its assets and the app's own 404). This is the first live run of the app path, start to finish. Later that day the owner also ran a preview and a second production deploy of it, and `cloudcel ls` listed the three versions correctly. Live so far: a new app with files and code, a preview, a second production deploy. **Then, asked for by the owner ("We need this also. We need complete one"), live the same evening: `examples/hello-database` (D1 and KV created through Cloudcel, a Durable Object, a variable, a cron schedule that ran, and a second deploy that reused both resources) and a Next.js 16.3.8 app from `create-next-app` (OpenNext 1.20.11). On 14 Oct SvelteKit (`svelte-test`) deployed live too; its second deploy failed at the build's own type check, which the CLI now handles (the types are made again before every build), and the next deploy went through live with the fix ("Types … are up to date", version `58a13480`). **First, now (22 Oct 2026): put the Bittensor light client on Cloudflare.** The owner approved the deploy ("Deploy it now (Recommended)"). It stopped before anything reached Cloudflare:

- `cloudcel whoami` says "Cloudflare: not connected" for Xayaan's workspace.
- In the Mac's local database, the workspace's last Cloudflare grant (`fc38beff…`, made 9 Oct 15:47 UTC) is `reconnect_required`, with no active grant.
- Its access token was last refreshed on 15 Oct (it expired 15:38 UTC), and the Cloudflare sign-in app hasn't changed since 9 Oct. So a token refresh after that failed and Cloudcel dropped the grant (`worker/auth.ts`, `grantToken`).

In order:

- (a) **Owner:** reconnect Cloudflare once. With Cloudcel running, open `http://127.0.0.1:5173/app/#cli-setup` (or run `node /Users/zayaan_1/Downloads/Cloudcel/packages/cli/dist/cloudcel.js login`), connect Cloudflare and approve on Cloudflare's page. The agent must not do this approval itself. `cloudcel whoami` should then say connected.
  - **First try, 22 Oct (record 63): refused by Cloudflare.** The page said "This is a private application and can only be authorized by members of the developer's account", then "Application authorization failed".
  - **Why:** "Cloudcel local" is a private OAuth client. Cloudcel's local setup creates it that way, and its owning account is in `provider_apps.account_id`. Cloudflare's docs: "Private clients can only be authorized by members of the parent Cloudflare account." The browser was signed in to Cloudflare as a user outside the account that owns the client. That's almost certainly "Takumisolutionsmv@gmail.com's Account", whose 9 Oct grant worked; not checked, since the agent can't read the Mac's database.
  - **Fix given:** check the signed-in email at dash.cloudflare.com/profile. Log in as the user who belongs to that account, then start Connect Cloudflare again from Cloudcel.
  - **Alternatives:** invite the other login to that account (Manage Account → Members), or set up a new sign-in app in the other login's account. With a new app, deploys would go to that account instead.
- (b) **Deploy, on the Mac.** A Claude session on the Mac can do this. A session in the VM can't reach the owner's Cloudcel, so there the owner runs these in the Mac's Terminal (record 63). The commands were given in record 63.
  1. Start Cloudcel if needed (`npm run dev`, or `preview_start` `cloudcel-dev` from a Mac session; then use `127.0.0.1`).
  2. Copy the example over the older copy, from the Cloudcel folder: `cd /Users/zayaan_1/Downloads/Cloudcel`, then `cp -R examples/bittensor-light-client /Users/zayaan_1/Downloads/`. The copy made on 22 Oct predates the key, the rate limit, WebSocket-only, the archive and the cache.
  3. `cd /Users/zayaan_1/Downloads/bittensor-light-client`, then `node /Users/zayaan_1/Downloads/Cloudcel/packages/cli/dist/cloudcel.js deploy --prod`.
  4. Run `node scripts/check.mjs https://bittensor-light-client.takumisolutionsmv.workers.dev` while it's still open, and watch `/status`.
  5. **Owner: give it a key** (they'll be the only one using it, record 62). In that folder: `openssl rand -hex 24`, then `node …/cloudcel.js env add RPC_KEY` and paste the key. The agent shouldn't see the key. From then on the address is `…workers.dev/<key>`; the owner runs `check.mjs` with it, and its output holds no key.
  6. **Measure, then record** in the example's README, the QA report's "Live evidence" and this file:
     - From `cloudcel logs`, `cloudcel metrics` and `/status`: memory against 128 MB (`wasmMemoryBytes`, plus up to 4 MB of cache), CPU, and log volume.
     - The archive on Cloudflare: `/status`'s `history` growing about 1,000 blocks per alarm until it reaches 7 days back; an old-block read, checked; the cache's hits.
     - Whether the TCP sockets to peers work from Cloudflare.
- (c) ~~`grantToken` dropped a connection on any failed refresh~~ **Fixed on 22 Oct** (newest checkpoint paragraph). Only `invalid_grant` (GitHub: `bad_refresh_token`) ends a connection now; anything else keeps it, and the next call tries again. The fix can't bring back the owner's grant, whose tokens were already wiped, so (a) is still needed once. Still unknown: whether Cloudflare's refresh tokens expire when unused for days. If they do, the refusal now says so plainly: "Cloudflare no longer accepts this connection (invalid_grant). Connect Cloudflare again."

- (d) ~~Patch `0003`, `ruzstd` 0.9.1 (RUSTSEC-2026-0344)~~ **Done on 22 Oct**: rebuilt reproducibly (sha256 `5591b0fc…622e`), and `scripts/check.mjs` passes locally against live Finney.
- (e) **Later, for the light client:**
  - **Clients:** try the Bittensor Python SDK and polkadot.js against it before saying they work. Only raw JSON-RPC, legacy and `chainHead_v1`, has been checked.
  - ~~**Abuse:** a rate limit or an API key for the public address~~ **Done on 22 Oct**, both (newest checkpoint paragraph); not on Cloudflare yet.
  - **WebSocket closes:** the "Network connection lost" errors (newest checkpoint paragraph). Try answering the client's close with `server.close()`, in the local runtime first.
  - **Upstream:** offer both patches to smoldot.
  - **Metadata:** keep it in storage across restarts, so a restart doesn't compute it again (the 78 MB high-water mark).
  - ~~**Old blocks:** a fallback to full nodes for archive queries, clearly labelled as unverified~~ **Done on 22 Oct, beyond that** (record 67): storage and headers checked by proof or hash, the rest marked `unverified`.
  - ~~**Old blocks, next:** check `chain_getBlock` bodies against the extrinsics root, the way storage is checked~~ **Done on 22 Oct** (record 68). Runtime calls at old blocks can't be checked without running the runtime ourselves.
  - **The site:** show it as Cloudcel's first demo once it has run live.
  - **The checkpoint:** a forced GRANDPA change while the object is down for longer than its saved state covers would need a newer checkpoint (README, "Updating the checkpoint").

The first attempt that day went wrong for a reason worth remembering: the owner created `my-app` with `npx create-react-router@latest my-app` _inside the Cloudcel folder_ and ran the deploy from the Cloudcel folder, so the CLI reviewed Cloudcel's own `wrangler.jsonc`; the fix was `mv` to `/Users/zayaan_1/Downloads/rr-test` and `cd` there. Two reasons for the move: Cloudcel's dev server watches its own folder and reloads when files in a nested app change (setup changes several), and **the owner already has a project named `my-app`** (their landing page, `Xayaan/Royal-Protocol-Landing`), which the CLI would offer to link a folder of that name to; answer **n** to "Link to existing project?" for a test app. Always give the owner a `cd` line and a folder outside the repository. In general: in any app's folder run `node /Users/zayaan_1/Downloads/Cloudcel/packages/cli/dist/cloudcel.js deploy --prod` (plain `cloudcel` does the same for a new app: its first deploy is production). No `wrangler.jsonc` is needed beforehand for a framework app: the CLI asks once ("Add them now?"), runs Wrangler's setup, which writes it and adds the adapter, then builds and deploys. Good first candidates: `npx create-react-router@latest my-app` (went through cleanly in the VM), or a Worker from `npm create cloudflare@latest my-api -- --type hello-world`, which already has its configuration. **A new `create-next-app` project is stopped after setup** with two changes to make (`npm install next@16.3.8`; remove `cacheComponents` and `partialPrefetching` from `next.config.ts`), then `cloudcel` again deploys it. SvelteKit 3 needs Node.js 22.17 or newer (the Mac has 22.13). It shows what it detected, asks before Cloudflare creates a database, builds, and prints "Deploying … with Wrangler 4.148.0…" followed by Wrangler's own output and the production address. `npx` downloads Wrangler 4.148.0 the first time (it is always that version now, even when the project has a newer one).
4. **Owner — put `my-app` online from the computer:** in a clone of `Xayaan/Royal-Protocol-Landing`: `… cloudcel.js link --project my-app`, then `… cloudcel.js`. Gatsby 3 on Node 22 may need `NODE_OPTIONS=--openssl-legacy-provider`.

## Owner's live setup (each fact dated, the latest from 29 Oct 2026; no secrets)

- **Where the agent ran on 29 Oct, evening (records 114–115):** a QEMU Ubuntu 26.04 VM (aarch64, `zayaan-QEMU-Virtual-Machine`, 6 cores, 7 GB), not the Parallels one: the Mac's home is mounted over SMB (CIFS, `file_mode=0755`) at `/mnt/mac`, so the repository is `/mnt/mac/Downloads/Cloudcel`. No sudo, no Docker, no Chrome; system Node 22.22.1 with npm 9.2.0; the old VM's `~/.local/cloudcel-toolchain` and `~/cloudcel-verify` don't exist here. Two traps: every file reads as mode 0755, so `git status` lists about 370 mode-only changes (use `git -c core.fileMode=false status`, stage explicit paths); and a file created read-only can't be renamed on the share (EACCES), which is how git writes loose objects, so `git add` fails there. Commits from the VM go through a VM-local object store (`GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY=~/.local/shipcel-git-objects` with the repository's objects as an alternate), then the new objects are packed and copied into `.git/objects/pack/` and checked with `git cat-file -e` and `git fsck --connectivity-only` (record 115). `git ls-remote origin` fails (no GitHub credentials): pushes happen on the Mac.

41. **"R2 file storage. Free-only projects refuse it by design. But R2 is included in free plan?"** Answered from Cloudflare's own pages (read 14 Oct 2026: R2 pricing, updated 1 Oct; R2 get started, updated 21 Apr): R2 has a monthly free allowance (10 GB-month Standard storage, 1M Class A, 10M Class B operations, free egress), but it is a subscription of its own added through a checkout, and usage beyond the allowance is billed monthly rather than stopped, unlike Workers, D1 and KV on Workers Free. So Free-only, which promises nothing can bill, still leaves it out; to use or test R2, the owner turns Free-only off for that project in its Billing panel (owner only, in the dashboard) and adds the R2 subscription in Cloudflare. Reworded the refusal to name the Billing panel (it said "the project's configuration") and explained the difference in `docs/guides/free-only-and-billing.md`. The design is unchanged; it is the owner's to change.

53. **"Im wondering if i can start run a fully serverless bittensor lite node using cloudcel as first demo ? " I'm fed up with existing bittensor RPC providers so I have built my own. It is fully serverless and costs basically nothing to run. " Someone did this already?"** (22 Oct). Answered, after web searches (the agent's words, condensed):
    - **"Not a lite node, but a serverless Bittensor RPC, yes."** The quoted post wasn't found (probably X or Discord); the owner was asked for the link.
    - **Found instead:** managed providers OnFinality, GetBlock and Nodies; Blockmachine, a free keyless endpoint that says its gateway checks every response with Merkle proofs; Umi-BitSign (github.com/Umi-BitSign/umi), a Bittensor subnet that embeds smoldot and talks to Finney's peers directly. No public Bittensor RPC on Cloudflare Workers.
    - **Why not a lite node:** Bittensor's lite node is a full Subtensor node that skips history (guides.learnbittensor.org, "subtensor node requirements": about 16 GB of RAM, about 128 GB of fast disk, always running, peers connecting in on port 30333). Workers have 128 MB and can't accept incoming connections, and the largest Container has 12 GiB and 20 GB of disk (on the paid plan).
    - **Two options, both serverless:**
      1. An RPC gateway: a Worker and a Durable Object in front of several public full nodes, with failover, a cache for what never changes, shared subscriptions and a 15-minute health check. It fits the free plan's 100,000 requests a day but still trusts others' full nodes.
      2. A real light client (experimental): smoldot in a Durable Object, reaching Finney's peers over outbound connections and checking blocks itself. "One always-on Durable Object uses about 10,800 of the 13,000 GB-seconds a day the free plan includes", with the caveat "Unproven: whether smoldot fits in 128 MB and Cloudflare's CPU limits."
    - **Recommended:** the gateway as the first demo, since it shows a Worker, a Durable Object, a cache and a schedule from one `cloudcel deploy --prod`. The light client was offered as an experiment, alongside or after.
54. **The owner quoted option 2 ("1. A real light client (experimental) …"), then: "Lets do this / I need a real light client for bittensor"** (22 Oct). The plan given, in three steps: (1) a feasibility experiment, locally in Cloudflare's runtime first, then on the owner's account; (2) the light client app, JSON-RPC over HTTPS and WebSocket from the Durable Object; (3) the Cloudcel demo, one `cloudcel deploy --prod`. Two approvals, asked before downloading:
    - **"Yes, both (Recommended)":** smoldot 3.7.0 from registry.npmjs.org (about 6.8 MB unpacked; GPL-3.0-or-later with the Classpath exception, "so an app using it needn't be GPL"), and Finney's `raw_spec_finney.json` (12.1 MB) from github.com/RaoFoundation/subtensor (Subtensor's repository, moved from opentensor), only for its boot nodes and checkpoint. Both went into the session scratchpad.
    - **"Yes, download and build (Recommended)",** asked as: "Upstream smoldot can't follow Finney (a forced validator change, then the Aura/BABE mix). The fix is two small patches to smoldot, the same ones the Umi subnet runs, and rebuilding its WebAssembly with the Rust already on your Mac." It covered smoldot's source at tag `npm-smoldot-v3.7.0` (commit `cd98d7fa`) from github.com/paritytech/smoldot (said to be 20–40 MB; the clone was 80 MB), its crates from crates.io (150–300 MB into `~/.cargo`), and 1–2 GB of build output in the scratchpad (25 GB free then). "The same ones the Umi subnet runs" means the same two changes, which Umi makes to smoldot 2.2.0 and smoldot-light 1.3.2; ours are written for 3.7.0.

    Built `examples/bittensor-light-client` (newest checkpoint paragraph, slice "Bittensor light client example"). It works against live Finney in the local runtime.

61. **"It needs the Workers Paid plan ($5 a month). Nothing records whether your account has it; on the free plan it would deploy and then fail. It cant work without workers paid plan?"** (22 Oct). The agent rechecked Cloudflare's pages; the slice "Bittensor light client example" has what they say.
    - **Answer:** right, as far as Cloudflare documents it, and the blocker is CPU, not cost. Durable Objects get their plan's Workers limits, and Workers Free allows 10 ms of CPU per invocation.
    - **Why that's too little:** smoldot averaged about 10 ms of CPU every second locally. Even counting each 30-second alarm as a fresh 10 ms, Free gives about 10 ms for about 300 ms of work.
    - **Everything else fits Free.**
    - **Not certain:** the page's own table lists 30 s with no plan column, and no one has tried it on a Free account. On Free, going over a limit fails; it doesn't bill.
    - **Options given:**
      - Workers Paid at $5 a month: inside what's included at the expected load, with the rate limit added before sharing.
      - The RPC gateway from entry 53: it fits Free, but it trusts its providers.
      - If the account is on Free, the deploy itself is a free test.
62. **"yes fix the reconnect bug and add the rate limit / Also tell me how much it would cost me on cloudflare tttttttttttto keep it running? I will be only using the RPC"** (22 Oct, in the VM).
    - **Done:** "What's next" (c), and (e)'s abuse item (newest checkpoint paragraph).
    - **Added beyond the ask:** a key, an optional one, since the owner will be the only user.
    - **Told about cost:** $5 a month (Workers Paid), and nothing more for one person's use.
      - The object takes about 330,000 of the 400,000 included GB-seconds and about 130,000 of the 1 million included Durable Object requests. That leaves about 870,000 HTTP requests or about 17 million WebSocket messages a month.
      - Beyond that: $0.15 per million Durable Object requests, plus $0.30 per million Worker requests past 10 million (HTTP only).
      - Examples: 10 requests a second all month, about $13.50 over HTTP or about $5.06 over WebSocket.
      - Already on Workers Paid: about $0 extra, unless other Durable Objects use the rest of the included time ($12.50 per million GB-seconds beyond).
      - Unknown until it runs on Cloudflare: Workers Logs volume (20 million events included; the pricing changes on 1 Dec 2026), and whether it fits Cloudflare's limits at all.

The page read: "This is a private application and can only be authorized by members of the developer's account" and "Application authorization failed". The diagnosis and the fix given are under "What's next" (a).
    - **Corrected:** the agent had said "I deploy the updated light client". This VM session can't reach the Cloudcel on the Mac, so the deploy runs in the Mac's Terminal. The commands are in the answer: copy the example over the old copy, `cd` there, `cloudcel.js deploy --prod`.

      - Opening the app needs Cloudflare's public visibility. It's permanent, and it needs the client URL's domain verified with a DNS TXT record (Cloudflare's "Create your OAuth client", read 22 Oct).
      - Unknown: whether a public client may keep loopback redirect URLs.
64. **"Check https://blockmachine.io/pricing Will our bittensor light-client be 1000x times cheaper than them? Compare costs and tell me"** (22 Oct). The agent read Blockmachine's pricing, its method catalog for Bittensor and its RPC overview that day.
    - **Blockmachine's prices:**
      - Keyless: free, 60 RU a minute per IP, no archive.
      - With a key: Free (10K RU), Standard $9 (5M RU), Pro $25 (20M), Business $125 (100M), Business Plus $499 (500M), Enterprise $999 (1B). Extra use costs $10 to $1 per million RU.
      - Every Bittensor method shown costs 1 RU, `state_call` and the subscriptions included. Whether each notification is charged isn't stated.
      - Its overview says "verifiable RPC network" without saying how. Its method docs say the gateway forwards to a backend node and returns its answer "as it is".
    - **Answer: no, not 1000x on a bill.** Monthly bills, Blockmachine / ours over WebSocket / ours over HTTP with one call per request:
      - Up to 60 calls a minute: $0 keyless / $5 / about $5.26. Ours costs more here.
      - 5M calls: $9 / $5 / about $5.62.
      - 26M calls (10 a second): about $43 (Pro plus extra) / about $5.06 / about $13.58, so roughly 3–8x cheaper.
      - 260M calls: about $445 / about $6.82 / about $123, in theory only, since one light client can't answer that fast.
    - **Where 1000x shows up:** only per extra million calls, $0.0075 over WebSocket against $1–$10. That's the margin, not a bill.
    - **Other differences given:**
      - Ours checks every answer itself; theirs is trusted.
      - Theirs has archive data; ours only knows blocks from its start.
      - Theirs has many nodes; ours is one object, with storage reads around 0.5 s each locally and 4 runtime calls at a time.
      - Ours isn't proven on Cloudflare yet.
    - **Suggested claim:** a flat about $5 a month for your own verified Bittensor RPC, or "about 8x cheaper at 10 calls a second", not "1000x".
65. **"Should we host cloudcel on a VPS or should we host this on a CPU as well?"** (22 Oct). The agent read "CPU" as Cloudflare. The recommendation: Cloudflare, not a VPS.
    - **Why:**
      - Cloudcel already is a Cloudflare app: one Worker, static files, D1 and a cron every minute (`wrangler.jsonc`). On a VPS it would run in Cloudflare's development runtime, or need porting.
      - It holds customers' Cloudflare and GitHub tokens. On a VPS, the OS, patches, backups and the encryption key would all be the owner's to secure.
      - About $5 a month on Workers Paid.
      - A public Cloudflare sign-in app needs a domain the owner verifies with a DNS record, which workers.dev can't take.
    - **Suggested:** a Cloudflare account of its own for Cloudcel.
    - **A VPS (or a Container) only for a later long-running piece,** such as builds run by Cloudcel. Today builds run on the person's computer or in their own Cloudflare.
    - **Before hosting:**

66. **"The bittensor RPC lite node has websocket enabled right? For our demo we should enable websocket version only not HTTP so we can make it free so it doesnt cost us alot. Also it should be fast like blockchmachine … Anyway around the archive node like our lite node? Cheap serverless version?"** (22 Oct).
    - **Done:** WebSocket only (newest checkpoint paragraph).
    - **Told about cost:** it isn't free, since the $5 of Workers Paid stays. About 17 million messages a month fit inside it. For a public demo, set the budget guard to pause, since every connection is still a request.
    - **Told about speed:** what the light client holds answers at once: heads, subscriptions, runtime version, cached metadata.
      - Storage reads need a proof from a peer, about 0.5 s each locally, and runtime calls 0.4–1.5 s. A full node reads its own disk.
      - Proposed: a cache per block hash, placing the object near Finney's peers, and measuring on Cloudflare first.
    - **Told about archive data:** a real archive node can't be serverless.
      - Proposed instead: the light client records each block hash it verifies. For an old block, it asks Opentensor's public archive node for the value and its proof (`state_getReadProof`) and checks it against that block's verified state root. That gives verified storage reads from the day it started.
      - Runtime calls at old blocks, and blocks before its start: passed through, marked unverified.
      - Checked that day: the archive node gave a 4-node, 937-byte proof for block #9,000,000.
      - Estimate given: a few days of work, about $0 a month. Not built; offered after the first deploy.

67. **"build the verified archive and the per-block cache now"** (22 Oct, in the VM; the session hit the owner's usage limit midway, and the owner said to continue). Built and run locally (newest checkpoint paragraph).
    - **Told:** what it does and its local timings: old reads about 0.4 s checked, then 8 ms cached; latest reads 0.5 s, then 2 ms.
    - **Limits given:**
      - Unchecked calls are marked.
      - It depends on Opentensor's free archive node.
      - Not measured on Cloudflare.
      - The cost stays $5: SQLite about 1.5 MB a day, and about 2,000 archive requests once.
    - **Next step:** the same as before: reconnect Cloudflare under the right login, then deploy from the Mac.

68. **"check block bodies against the extrinsics root too / we have archive node implemented into it now ?"** (22 Oct). Built and run locally (newest checkpoint paragraph).
    - **Answered the second question:** no, it isn't an archive node. The light client keeps a small record of verified block hashes and state roots, not the chain's history. It reads old blocks from someone else's archive node (Opentensor's public one), checking what it can, and depends on that node's being up (`ARCHIVE_URL` to switch). An archive node of our own would need terabytes and a server, not serverless.

70. **"Check new features on cloudflare, see if any free feature we should implement, we are competing with netlify and vercel, so we need to be the complete replacement for them, that offers much cheaper than them. Also is WAF and cloudflare DDOS protections etc enabled for all customer apps they will deploy with us?"** (22 Oct). The agent read Cloudflare's docs (workers.dev routing, DDoS, WAF, managed rules, custom rules), the changelog, the Birthday Week 2026 wrap-up, and secondary coverage of Traces and Vinext. Nothing was built.
    - **Answer on protection:**
      - **DDoS:** on for every app, automatically. Cloudflare's docs: DDoS protection goes "to all customers on all plans and services", "unmetered and unlimited", covering layers 3, 4 and 7.
      - **WAF:** a zone setting, so it reaches only apps on the customer's own domain, never workers.dev. Cloudflare recommends a custom domain for production.
      - **What Free gets:** the Free Managed Ruleset (whether it's on by default isn't stated), 5 custom rules and 1 rate-limiting rule.
      - **Cloudcel today:** it configures none of this, and its sign-in app has no firewall permissions.
    - **Recommended, in this order:**
      1. A Security panel for apps with custom domains: the Free Managed Ruleset, Bot Fight Mode, Under Attack mode, presets for the 5 custom rules and 1 rate-limit rule, and a push toward custom domains. It needs new sign-in permissions, so connections would have to be set up again.
      2. Allow Workers' `ratelimit` binding in deploys (`worker/wrangler-policy.ts`).
      3. The new observability: Traces (open beta), Issues (error grouping to webhooks), and log export as Cloudcel's log drains. Pricing is conflicting in secondary sources, so check the official page first.
      4. Vinext 1.0 as a Next.js path beside OpenNext: Cloudflare says over 99% of Next.js tests pass, Cache Components excepted.
      5. Domain search and purchase through Cloudflare Registrar's new APIs (at cost).
      6. Forms with Turnstile and D1, as Netlify Forms.
      7. Image transformations, as Vercel's and Netlify's image optimization.
      8. Queues and Workflows for background jobs.
      - Free status for 6–8 is from earlier knowledge, to recheck before building.
    - **New this autumn, besides those:** Threat Signals (free), Account Abuse Protection, KV Instant, AI Search GA, Basin, K2, EmDash. Pricing wasn't stated for most.
    - **Asked:** which to build first. Answered in record 71.

    - **Why not GitHub's "authorize during installation":** it sends no state or PKCE, which Shipcel's sign-in requires, and GitHub's docs say to use the web flow for that.

Shipcel had been sending owners down the Pages path (Pages → Connect to Git), which may link only Pages.

Only 404 code 12000 counts as unreadable (`missingCodes`); any other refusal shows Cloudflare's own message (`errorDetail`). 81. **"https://bittensor-rpc.takumisolutionsmv.workers.dev/ Done but it looks stuck also shipcel env add RPC_KEY is stuck as well / can we change domain from takumisolutionsmv to shipcel.workers.dev?"** (23 Oct, with a screenshot of `shipcel env add RPC_KEY` showing an empty line).
    - **`env add` wasn't stuck: its prompt was erased, and what was typed would have shown.** Reproduced in a pseudo-terminal in the VM: `askSecret` (`packages/cli/src/main.ts`) wrote "Value of RPC_KEY: ", then readline's first redraw (`ESC[1G ESC[0J`) cleared the line, and the mute override (`_writeToOutput`) no longer takes effect with `readline/promises` on Node 22 and 24, so the typed value was echoed. **Fixed:** `packages/cli/src/secret.ts` (`readSecret`: raw mode, nothing echoed, Enter or Ctrl+D finishes, Backspace, escape sequences left out, Ctrl+C exits 130), used by `askSecret`; the question now says "(hidden as you type)". Checked in a real pseudo-terminal (the question stays, nothing echoed) and by `tests/cli-secret.test.ts` (3 tests, 8 mutations caught). The CLI is rebuilt (`public/cli/*.tgz`); shipcel.com serves the fix after the next `npm run production:setup`, and `npm install -g https://shipcel.com/cli/shipcel.tgz` installs it. Until then a piped value works: `openssl rand -hex 24 | tee /dev/tty | shipcel env add RPC_KEY`.
    - **The light client fails on Cloudflare** (checked from the VM, read-only, 23 Oct): the page (`/`, served by the Worker) answers 200, but `/status` answers 500 "error code: 1101" (an exception reaching the Worker) after 36 s and again after 11.6 s, and a WebSocket closes with 1006 after 5.8 s. Every request to the Durable Object waits for `#ensureRunning()` (smoldot's start and `addChain`), so whatever stops it there stops everything. The local runs (22 Oct) never met Cloudflare's per-object limits: 128 MB of memory (smoldot's Wasm memory alone was 42–78 MB locally, before the JavaScript heap), 30 s of CPU per request, 6 simultaneous outbound connections. Not diagnosed yet: the owner's `shipcel logs --since 1h --errors` (or `npx wrangler tail bittensor-rpc`) has the exception.
    - **Domain:** a workers.dev subdomain belongs to the whole Cloudflare account. `shipcel.workers.dev` looked unclaimed on 23 Oct (names under it don't resolve, as for an unregistered one, while `no-such-app.takumisolutionsmv.workers.dev` answers 404); only Cloudflare can confirm it. Changing it (Cloudflare dashboard → Workers & Pages → the account's subdomain → Change) moves every Worker in the account (`hello-cloudcel`, `hello-database`, `next-test`, `rr-test`, `svelte-test`, `bittensor-rpc`) to `*.shipcel.workers.dev`, and their old addresses stop working. Shipcel stores each app's address when it deploys (`deployment_jobs.live_url`), so its pages show the old one until the app deploys again. The alternative suggested for the endpoint: a custom domain on shipcel.com (`shipcel domains add rpc.shipcel.com` in the app's folder), which moves nothing else.

85. **"How do i create an email like info@shipcel.com using cloudflare since i created the domain there"** (24 Oct). Answered from Cloudflare's Email Routing docs (read 24 Oct):
    1. Compute → Email Service → Email Routing → **Onboard Domain**, which adds MX, SPF and DKIM records at the root and doesn't touch the website.
    2. **Destination Addresses**: add the existing inbox, then verify it from the email Cloudflare sends.
    3. **Routing Rules** → **Create routing rule**: pattern `info`, action **Send to an email**, the verified inbox, **Save**.
    4. Test from another account.

    Email Routing receives and forwards only. To reply as info@shipcel.com: Gmail's "Send mail as" through `smtp.gmail.com` with an app password, with `include:_spf.google.com` added to the same SPF record, or a mail host (Google Workspace, Zoho Mail, iCloud+). Nothing was changed in the account.

Netlify Free 300 credits, Personal $9 (1,000), Pro $20 (3,000); 20 credits per GB, 2 per 10,000 requests, 15 per deploy, 10 per GB-hour; packs 500 for $5 (Personal), 1,500 for $10 (Pro). Cloudflare: 100,000 requests a day free, Workers Paid $5 with 10 M requests and 30 M CPU-ms, then $0.30 and $0.02 per million; "no additional charges for data transfer"; "requests to static assets are free and unlimited".
      - **The ladder** (2 MB a page view, about 10 requests a view, 20 ms of CPU per API call): a personal site of 20,000 views: $0 on both (Vercel only if non-commercial; Netlify $9, since 40 GB is 800 credits). A small business site, 50,000 views and 150,000 API calls: $0 against Vercel Pro $20 and Netlify Pro $20. A busy app, 1 M views, 8 M requests, 2 TB, 3 M API calls: $5.60 (Paid $5 plus 30 M extra CPU-ms at $0.02) against Vercel about $40 ($20 plus the $20 tier; about $6 of functions inside the credit) and Netlify about $290 (about 43,000 credits: Pro's 3,000 plus 27 packs). A 2 GB database: +$0 to $5 (Workers Paid, D1 5 GB) against +$25 on Vercel (Supabase Pro, now in the Vercel page's sources) and +$0 on Netlify (Netlify Database).

101.  **"Now get bittensor RPC working, on our free plan … fix it and ensure it works super fast and free so it is a good showcase project for shipcel / after that check our pages, declutter, make it easy to read, and simple with all facts/features/everything clear"** (28 Oct, about 13:15 UTC), then, to the question below, **"Skip bittensor RPC"**.
      - **The RPC:** told that the resets aren't a bug of ours: Cloudflare counts roughly 100 MB for smoldot's compiled 5.5 MB module beside its own memory, so it dies at the runtime compile inside the 128 MB every isolate gets on every plan, and a Durable Object on the free plan has 10 ms of CPU per request where verifying a block takes more.       - **The pages, measured first:** the front page was 1,842 words with 30 headings, and its last two sections (three tier cards, then "Why it costs less" with a figure and three cards) repeated /pricing/ and /why/; /pricing/ listed every Cloudflare free limit twice (in a card, then in the table); the Alpha notice on the front page, the features page and /why/ still said Shipcel "has not yet been verified against live GitHub and Cloudflare accounts", which stopped being true with records 83 (CLI deploys since 9 Oct) and 99 (GitHub).

103.  **The reviewer's report on today's diff, and the fixes** (28 Oct, 15:30–16:40 UTC; the TypeScript reviewer agent from record 102, 25 minutes, 91 tool uses, every finding checked against the code before acting).
      - **Accepted and fixed, tests first (10 new or changed tests, 907 in all):**
        - _High:_ `/ingest/*` read a body with no `Content-Length` whole before checking its size; a chunked 64 MB stream was buffered. Now Hono's `bodyLimit` (1 MB) guards the route as `worker/index.ts` does elsewhere; the test streams 4 MB and sees the read stop after about 1 MB.
        - _Medium:_ the proxy forwarded any path and method to PostHog, so another project's site-app JavaScript could have been served from shipcel.com's origin under `script-src 'self'`. Now only PostHog's loader's paths pass: `/static/….js[.map]`, this project's `/array/<key>/config[.js]` (GET/HEAD), and `/e/`, `/i/v0/e/`, `/batch/`, `/flags/`, `/decide/` (POST); an encoded spelling of `/ingest` (`/%69ngest/`), which Hono matched but which mangled the upstream host, answers 404.
        - _Medium:_ `shipcel` with a token but no address (CI with `SHIPCEL_TOKEN` and no `SHIPCEL_URL`) would have sent a self-hosted token to shipcel.com. A token now requires the address ("This token needs the address of its Shipcel too…"); an empty `SHIPCEL_URL` counts as unset; `shipcel login` alone still goes to shipcel.com. The CLI guide's and README's CI examples carry `SHIPCEL_URL` again.
        - _Medium:_ connecting a linked repository again asked nothing and the upsert overwrote the link's `user_id`, `created_at` and `branch`, so a teammate's rerun took over whose token the runs use. Now it asks (or `--yes`), and a link with the same repository is kept as made; the test reruns as a developer teammate after GitHub renames the default branch.
        - _Low:_ request headers are an allowlist (Accept, Accept-Language, Content-Type, Origin, Referer, User-Agent, plus `X-Forwarded-For` from `CF-Connecting-IP`), so `Proxy-Authorization`, `X-Real-IP`, `Forwarded`, `True-Client-IP` and Cloudflare Access headers never leave; response headers too (Content-Type, Content-Length, Cache-Control, ETag, Last-Modified, plus `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`), so PostHog's HSTS with `includeSubDomains` and its reflected CORS no longer reach shipcel.com. `POSTHOG_REGION` is read case-insensitively; `/apps` no longer counts as the dashboard; the cache's `match` and `put` failures are isolated; `providerApp` is guarded as in `connections.ts`.
        - _Design:_ the dashboard no longer loads PostHog's script at all (337 KB, unpinned, running with first-party privileges on pages that show tokens). `/analytics.js` gives it a sender of Shipcel's own: a `$pageview` at load and `src/posthog.ts`'s named events, posted to `/ingest/e/` with `distinct_id: "$posthog_cookieless"` and `$cookieless_mode: true`, the way PostHog's own script marks cookieless events (checked in its source), never the address's fragment. The privacy page says so.
      - **Not taken:** a CLI-side check of the install link (the server now passes only github.com addresses); the reviewer's unreachable `providerApp` failure got the guard without a test, like `connections.ts`.
      - **The reviewer found clean:** `/analytics.js` injection, the proxy's host control, cookie and credential stripping, cache poisoning, redirects, the key-unset path, secrets in URLs (fragments aren't captured), the installation check's error handling, TypeScript hygiene.
      - **Open from the review:** whether Cloudflare's runtime raised error 1102 for the chunked case (not reproducible locally); whether the "not installed" branch is reached for private repositories, where `GET /repos/…` may 404 first.

108.  **"Sttart with those and complete it"** (28 Oct, after the roadmap approval in record 107). The agent started with the first item, Postgres and MySQL through Hyperdrive, and built it end to end in this session (the agent's model changed to Claude Fable 5.1 during it; a context compaction happened mid-slice, so the owner's words here are the ones quoted in records 106–107 and above, not reconstructed).
      - **Built.** `worker/hyperdrive.ts` (migration `0036_hyperdrive.sql`): `GET/POST /api/projects/:id/hyperdrive` and `DELETE …/:configId`. A connection string (`postgres://`, `postgresql://` or `mysql://`, with user, password, host and database; default ports 5432/3306) becomes a Hyperdrive configuration in the customer's account (`POST /accounts/:id/hyperdrive/configs`, named `<project>-<binding>`); the password goes to Cloudflare and nowhere else; Shipcel stores the binding, scheme, host and database name, and answers with the `"hyperdrive": [{ "binding", "id" }]` snippet. A sign-in whose recorded scopes lack Hyperdrive gets a 409 that points at Connections. The upload policy (`worker/wrangler-policy.ts`) accepts a `hyperdrive` binding only for an id Shipcel made for that project (`hyperdriveCreated`, merged into the created list in `worker/wrangler-api.ts`). The repository review (`worker/repository-plan.ts`) accepts a top-level `hyperdrive` setting (`binding`, `id`, optional `localConnectionString`; five at most) as a resource of type `hyperdrive` that a deploy binds and never creates; GitHub deploys (`worker/deployments.ts`) refuse an id that isn't the project's before queuing and record the observed binding after the build; local deploys (`worker/local-deploys.ts`) keep it out of "resources to create" and check the id in the review; previews (`worker/previews.ts`, `previewsBlockIssues`) bind the same configurations as production, by the same ids, and the `previews` snippet and recorded `config_json` carry them. Dashboard: `src/Hyperdrive.tsx` under Manage → Database, shown before the first deploy too (the kit needs the id first). CLI 0.4.2: `shipcel hyperdrive ls | add <BINDING> | rm <BINDING>`; the connection string is read from a hidden prompt or standard input, never the command line. A **Postgres API** starter kit (`postgres` in `shared/catalog.ts`, `worker/kits.ts`): Hono plus `postgres` 3.4.9 on `env.DB.connectionString`, `nodejs_compat`, `GET /api/now`; its `wrangler.json` carries the project's configuration id when one exists, else the placeholder `HYPERDRIVE_ID`, which the kit's `check-config.mjs` refuses. Connections now ask Cloudflare for **Hyperdrive Write** (`shared/provider-setup.ts`; Cloudflare lists "Hyperdrive Write"/"Hyperdrive Edit" among account permissions, and OAuth scope names mirror permission names); `npm run production:setup -- --cloudflare-permissions` patches an existing OAuth client (`PATCH /accounts/:id/oauth_clients/:client_id`) and saves `CLOUDFLARE_OAUTH_SCOPES`, since a plain re-run asks nothing. Docs: the databases guide's "Postgres and MySQL through Hyperdrive", the CLI guide, the kits guide, the pricing row (free: 100,000 queries a day; Workers Paid: unlimited; the database bills itself), README, SETUP, ROADMAP (item 1 struck through), `docs/runtime-matrix.md`.

110.  **Roadmap item 2, from the owner's list: "2. Sign-in by email: magic links and passwords, sent through Cloudflare's Email Service from the customer's own domain (Workers Paid; a domain on Cloudflare). No third-party provider." / "Do this" / "we are on workers paid plan already"** (28 Oct; built on 29 Oct, right after the design pass, under "all this done?").
      - **Cloudflare's Email Service, as read on 29 Oct:** the Worker binding is `"send_email": [{ "name": "EMAIL" }]` and `env.EMAIL.send({ from, to, subject, text, html, … })` → `{ messageId }` (https://developers.cloudflare.com/email-service/api/send-emails/workers-api/; up to 50 recipients, 5 MiB); the sender's domain is onboarded in the dashboard (Compute → Email Service → Email Sending → Onboard Domain; it adds the MX, SPF, DKIM and DMARC records; the domain must use Cloudflare DNS; no API or Wrangler command for onboarding is documented, https://developers.cloudflare.com/email-service/get-started/send-emails/); pricing: not available on Workers Free; Workers Paid 3,000 a month included, then $0.35 per 1,000 (https://developers.cloudflare.com/email-service/platform/pricing/).
      - **Built (the service, `services/backend`):** `src/email.ts` with `POST /email/start` (a plain form from the hosted page, same-origin: the address is normalised, a one-time token is stored hashed in the new `email_links` table for 15 minutes, five links per address per ten minutes, then `env.EMAIL.send()` from `{ email: settings.emailFrom, name: appName }`), `GET /email/finish?token=…` (the link finishes the sign-in request it was asked for, only in the browser that asked, like every other method; used once; the address counts as verified and joins the account GitHub or Google verified with the same address, else a new account with an `email` identity; a session cookie is set), `POST /password/login` (JSON from the page's script; PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 at 600,000 rounds, derived even for unknown addresses so timing tells nothing; a wrong password and an unknown address answer the same 401), `POST /password/set` (the signed-in session's account; 10 to 200 characters) and `GET /password/set` (a page apps link to). New settings `email_from` and `password` (`readSettings`: `emailFrom`, `password`), tables `email_links` (indexed on expiry, cleaned with the others) and `passwords`, an `EMAIL?` binding on `Env`, the sign-in page's email form (shown only with a sender address; with passwords on, a password field signs in at once through the page script, and an empty one asks for a link; the form works without scripts), a "Check your email" page, a password page with its own script asset.
      - **Built (Shipcel's side):** `worker/backend.ts` saves `emailFrom` (validated) and `password` into the service's settings and binds every service Worker to `send_email` as `EMAIL` (the binding is harmless without an onboarded domain; the service checks the setting); `src/Backend.tsx` has the sender-address field with the onboarding note and the passwords checkbox. Docs: a new guide, `docs/guides/sign-in-and-realtime.md` (there was none for sign-in), registered in `site/docs.ts`; `notYet`, the features card, the pricing bullet and a pricing row ("Email sending": not offered free; 3,000 a month, then $0.35 per 1,000); README; ROADMAP item 2 struck through.

All pass; tsc, Prettier, the site and docs tests pass; the full gate's numbers are in the next record line once it finishes.
