111.  **"Also this 1. OAuth for the MCP server, so chat apps that only sign in with OAuth can connect."** (the owner's list of 28 Oct; built on 29 Oct after the email slice).
      - **Built:** `worker/oauth-server.ts` (migration `0037_oauth_server.sql`: `oauth_clients`, `oauth_requests`), mounted at the root for `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` (also `…/mcp`), `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server`, `POST /oauth/register`, `GET /oauth/authorize` and `POST /oauth/token`, and under `/api` for the dashboard's `GET /oauth/:id`, `POST /oauth/:id/approve` and `…/deny`. Shipcel is the authorization server for its own `/mcp`: a client registers itself (RFC 7591; `client_name` and one to ten HTTPS or loopback `redirect_uris`; `mcp_…` ids; 30 registrations a minute), the authorization request (`response_type=code`, PKCE S256 only, an optional `resource` that must be `/mcp`) is checked and recorded, and the browser is sent to `/app/#oauth/<id>`, where `src/OAuthApprove.tsx` shows who is asking and asks a signed-in developer or owner to allow or deny (signed out, it goes through GitHub and back: `returnHash` in `worker/auth.ts` accepts `oauth:<id>`). Approving records the code (hashed, 60 seconds) and the approver's workspace; the token endpoint checks the verifier, client and return address, spends the code before anything is made, then mints an API token named `<client> (MCP)` for 90 days through `insertToken`, so it shows under API & CLI and is revoked there. No refresh tokens: the app reconnects after 90 days. `/mcp`'s 401 now carries `resource_metadata="…/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource"` (RFC 9728), which is how an OAuth-capable client finds all this. The AI agents page and guide say how chat apps connect; `notYet` no longer says OAuth can't; ROADMAP item 3 struck through.
      - **Tests first:** `tests/mcp-oauth.test.ts`: the metadata and the 401 hint; registration (HTTPS or loopback only, 400 otherwise); the whole flow with the dashboard's side (401 without a session; the shown client; approve; the exchange once, a wrong verifier refused; the token listing tools at `/mcp`; the token named `Claude (MCP)`; deny sends `error=access_denied` and mints nothing); and four refused authorization requests. 927 unit tests, tsc, Prettier and the build pass; the journeys: 69 passed (2.7 m), after a dev server started before the new module existed was restarted and migration 0037 applied to the local database. On the dev server, registration and the authorization redirect to `/app/#oauth/<id>` were probed end to end, and the approval page renders its signed-out state.
      - **Not run live:** connect a real Claude.ai or ChatGPT connector to `https://shipcel.com/mcp` after the deploy and watch the approval page; that is the owner's browser. Found on the dev server, not in the unit tests: the static-assets setting `run_worker_first` in `wrangler.jsonc` decides which paths reach the Worker at all, so `/.well-known/*` and `/oauth/*` were added to it (without that, production would have answered the metadata with the 404 page).
      - **Live on 29 Oct, after the owner's "Done" and "Ran this also":** the deploy went through (version `42c91c66`; "All there already"), so shipcel.com now runs the whole working tree: `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` answers JSON, `/mcp`'s 401 carries the `resource_metadata` hint, the CLI tarball is 0.4.2, and `POST /oauth/register` answered 201, so migration 0037 is applied in production (that probe left one client row named "Probe" on production; it can do nothing without an approval). The `--cloudflare-permissions` run in the deploy tab ended at "That doesn’t look like a Cloudflare API token", and the live Hyperdrive create as 0xConsole answers 409 "This Cloudflare sign-in has no Hyperdrive permission", so the permission step is still open: a token of 40 letters, digits, `_`, `.` or `-` with OAuth Client Write, then connect Cloudflare again in Connections.
      - **Roadmap item 4, researched on 29 Oct, not started:** "Previews for Next.js (OpenNext's incremental cache in R2 and KV) and for pull requests from forks." (a) Next.js previews stay refused for the reason `previewRefusal` gives: OpenNext's `WORKER_SELF_REFERENCE` is a service binding, and service bindings from a Preview reach production; Cloudflare's way around that is `ctx.exports` (the `enable_ctx_exports` compatibility flag), which `@opennextjs/cloudflare` 1.20.14 has not adopted (its changelog has no entry for it; 1.14.4 even adds the binding to the generated config). So that half waits on OpenNext. (b) What can be built: the Next.js kit's incremental cache from https://opennext.js.org/cloudflare/caching: `r2IncrementalCache` with `withRegionalCache` (binding `NEXT_INC_CACHE_R2_BUCKET`, so an R2 draft binding, hence Free-only off), `doQueue` (`NEXT_CACHE_DO_QUEUE`, class `DOQueueHandler`) and `doShardedTagCache` (`NEXT_TAG_CACHE_DO_SHARDED`, class `DOShardedTagCache`), both SQLite Durable Objects with migrations; KV is listed but not recommended (eventually consistent); the static-assets cache for SSG-only apps that stay Free-only. The Next.js import adapter would need `durable_objects` and `migrations` among its `extraKeys` (today `services` and `images`) and the kit its `open-next.config.ts` and bindings. (c) Pull requests from forks: Cloudflare's builds configuration page says nothing about forks (only the production branch and preview builds for other branches); Shipcel's own GitHub Actions path can't give a fork's pull request the OIDC token without `pull_request_target`, which runs the fork's code with the repository's permissions. Honest position for now: previews stay for trusted branches and same-repository pull requests. The site's remaining linter lines are the stamp and two findings its disk mode cannot see through the linked stylesheet (`:focus-visible` and `overflow-x: clip`, both present in `public/site.css`).

115.  **"Commit whats live and start on Item 4, item 5 and items 6-11"** (29 Oct, evening, in the QEMU VM).
      - **Committed:** `6ddb269` "Overview as a working surface, one Free card, the log viewer's level badge": the 39 changed files of record 113 (sources, journeys, the site's built pages, screenshots and the new test), staged by path with `core.fileMode=false`, so no file's mode changed (`git diff --cached --summary` had no mode change; the new test is 100644), authored as 0xConsole like the commits before it. The first `git add` failed ("unable to write file .git/objects/cb/…: Permission denied"): git writes each loose object to a read-only temp file and renames it, and the share refuses that rename (EACCES, reproduced with a 0444 file in Python). So the commit was made with a VM-local object store and its 52 objects were packed into `pack-b2ce5565…` in the Mac's `.git/objects/pack/`; every object reads from the repository alone and `git fsck --connectivity-only` passes. **Not pushed:** the owner pushes from the Mac.
      - **Roadmap items 4–11:** in progress, in order; each slice's record follows here as it lands.
      - **The VM's write trap, found while building item 4:** besides `COMMIT_EDITMSG`, two source files rewritten shorter on the share (`worker/wrangler-api.ts`, `packages/cli/src/opennext.ts`) kept their old length with trailing NULs (3 and 177 bytes); a plain rewrite test didn't reproduce it, so it is intermittent. Fixed by stripping them; since then every write to the share goes through a helper that writes, truncates to the exact length, syncs and reads back after the attribute cache expires (`shed.py` in the session scratchpad), and the copy into `~/cloudcel-verify` refuses to run while any changed file holds a NUL. The verify copy and the toolchain (Node 24.22.2, npm 11.19.1, checksum verified) are new in this VM; `.claude/launch.json`'s `cloudcel-vm` runs its dev server.
      - **Item 4, OpenNext's cache for Next.js (built, fixture-verified; the kit built and run locally; not run against Cloudflare):** upstream first: `@opennextjs/cloudflare` 1.20.14 (28 Oct) is still the newest and still binds `WORKER_SELF_REFERENCE`, so Next.js branch previews stay refused (`previewRefusal`). Built instead: (1) the review (`openNextCacheReview` in `worker/repository-plan.ts`) replaces the old refusal of every cache override: `r2IncrementalCache` (needs the R2 binding `NEXT_INC_CACHE_R2_BUCKET`), `kvIncrementalCache` (`NEXT_INC_CACHE_KV`), `staticAssetsIncrementalCache`, `doQueue` (`NEXT_CACHE_DO_QUEUE` → `DOQueueHandler`), `doShardedTagCache` (`NEXT_TAG_CACHE_DO_SHARDED` → `DOShardedTagCache`), `kvNextTagCache`, `memoryQueue`/`queueCache` are accepted with their bindings and named when missing; the D1 tag cache (its table is made at deploy), cache purge (needs a zone and API token) and unknown caches are refused. The plan says which cache (`openNextCache`). From Cloudflare's builds the static-assets cache is copied into `.open-next/assets/cdn-cgi/_next_cache` by the build command; an R2 cache there fills on first request (warning). (2) Filling R2: OpenNext's own `populateCache remote` starts a remote dev Worker, which Shipcel's proxy can't vouch for, so the CLI does it through the deploy session: `packages/cli/src/opennext.ts` walks `.open-next/cache` exactly as OpenNext's `getCacheAssets` does and computes `computeCacheKey` (`incremental-cache/<buildId>/<sha256>.cache|fetch`); after Wrangler deploys production and before the session closes, it PUTs each entry (4 at a time, waits on 429) to a new proxy route in `worker/wrangler-api.ts`: `PUT /accounts/:id/r2/buckets/<bucket>/objects/<key>` passes only for a Next.js production session that is live, only to `<worker>-next-inc-cache-r2-bucket` reviewed for the binding and created by one of this project's deploys, only keys of OpenNext's shape, 25 MB an entry, 100 a minute and 500 a deploy (`NEXT_CACHE_ENTRIES`, so the workspace's 1,200 Cloudflare calls per five minutes stay mostly free); a preview fills nothing. The session's open answer carries `cache: { bucket, prefix }`. A static-assets cache is copied into the files by the CLI before Wrangler runs. (3) The Next.js kit: Free-only off → R2 + `withRegionalCache` (long-lived) + `doQueue` + `doShardedTagCache` with their bindings and migration; Free-only → `staticAssetsIncrementalCache` and nothing billable; a project's `FILES` bucket now joins the cache's instead of replacing it. (4) Fork pull requests: no preview, by design, said in the Previews panel and the previews guide (a fork's code would build with the repository's token); the safe design if the owner wants them later: a `pull_request` build job with no token, a `workflow_run` deploy job that never runs the fork's code, an owner's approval per commit in Shipcel, and a preview with no bindings or secrets. Docs: starter kits, previews, CLI, runtime matrix, the site's not-yet and Vercel pages, ROADMAP item 4.
      - **Item 4's evidence:** tests first, red, then green: `tests/deployments.test.ts` (4 cases replace the old refusal test), `tests/local-deploys.test.ts` (the proxy route: a live production deploy fills its bucket at OpenNext's keys; other buckets, other keys, `..`-free build ids, previews, a bucket Shipcel didn't make and a non-Next.js app are refused before Cloudflare is called), `tests/cli.test.ts` (Free-only refuses the R2 cache; the fill after production, nothing for a preview; the static copy), `tests/projects.test.ts` (both kit variants pass the folder review). 14 of 14 mutations caught. `npm run verify:kits -- nextjs` passed in the VM (install, OpenNext 1.20.13 build, native plan, dry run, `wrangler dev` answering / and /api/health) with the R2/Durable Object variant, whose `.open-next/worker.js` exports `DOQueueHandler`, `DOShardedTagCache` and `BucketCachePurge`; on a second build of the exported kit the CLI's entries and keys matched OpenNext 1.20.13's own `getCacheAssets` + `computeCacheKey` exactly (3 of 3; the real layout is `<buildId>/route-cache/APP_PAGE/<hash>/$/index.cache`). Gate: 938 unit tests, tsc, the site build within budgets, Prettier clean except the two `.impeccable/critique` snapshots, which were unformatted at `HEAD` already and are left alone. **Not run live:** a PUT through Cloudflare's R2 REST API (the storage browser uses the same endpoint), and a deploy of the kit.
      - **Item 5, a container kit and the first services (built, fixture-verified, kits checked in the VM without Docker; nothing run on Cloudflare):** Cloudflare's docs read on 29 Oct: Containers pull public Docker Hub, ECR and Google Artifact Registry images with the default policy (not GHCR; updated 30 Sep 2026); instance types `lite` 1/16 vCPU 256 MiB 2 GB … `standard-4`; Workers Paid includes 375 vCPU-minutes, 25 GiB-hours and 200 GB-hours a month, then $0.000020 a vCPU-s, $0.0000025 a GiB-s, $0.00000007 a GB-s (updated 5 Oct 2026); `@cloudflare/containers` 0.3.7 is the newest. **Money first:** the budget guard didn't count containers at all, so before offering services it reads Cloudflare's billing dataset `containersUsageAdaptiveGroups` (`cpuTimeSec`, `allocatedMemory` and `allocatedDisk` in byte-seconds, by `applicationId`; https://developers.cloudflare.com/analytics/graphql-api/tutorials/querying-container-metrics/) as three meters (`containers.cpu`, `.memory`, `.disk`; no free allowance, so any use proves Workers Paid). Shipcel's Cloudflare sign-in has no Containers permission to map an application to its Worker, so every project whose production adapter is `container` counts the whole account's container usage (alerts early, never late); egress isn't estimated. **Built:** the `docker` runtime is a kit now (catalog stage `kit`; the catalog's `Stage` type keeps `import`): `AppContainer` from `./Dockerfile` (Node 24 alpine, `container/server.mjs` on 8080), `lite`, at most 2, sleeping after 10 minutes, the Worker answering `/api/health` itself. Two services, new catalog entries (family Services): **Umami** (`docker.io/umamisoftware/umami:3.4.1`, port 3000, `basic`, one instance, sleep 30 min; `DATABASE_URL`, `APP_SECRET`, telemetry off) and **n8n** (`docker.io/n8nio/n8n:2.46.2`, port 5678, `basic`, one, sleep 1 h; the Worker turns `DATABASE_URL` into n8n's `DB_POSTGRESDB_*` settings and starts it with `WEBHOOK_URL`, `N8N_HOST` and the forwarded protocol of the request's own address). Until their two secrets are set each answers 503 and starts nothing. READMEs give the steps, the risk of the first visit (Umami's `admin`/`umami`, n8n's owner account) and the cost (a `basic` instance awake all month: about $6.50 beyond what Workers Paid includes, plus CPU). `usesContainers()` in `shared/catalog.ts` replaces every `framework === 'docker'` (the review's container adapter, Dockerfile reads, Free-only refusal and toggle, site presets, the dashboard's hints). The CLI: `shipcel init <container kit>` and `git connect` on a repository with containers ask before creating the project with Free-only off (`--yes` in scripts), and `git connect` deploys containers with Cloudflare's builds by itself (the GitHub Actions path runs the CLI, which can't make images); the local refusal no longer points at the wrong path. `verify:kits` knows container kits: no bindings, Free-only off, the Dockerfiles the configuration names read like `inspectRepository`, and "needs Docker" reported instead of failing. Not offered, with the reasons in the docs: Plausible (ClickHouse), Ghost (MySQL 8 and an uploads adapter). Docs: starter kits, billing guide, runtime matrix, README, ROADMAP item 5, the features and pricing copy (thirteen kits).
      - **Item 5's evidence:** tests first: `tests/budgets.test.ts` (the container meters priced at Cloudflare's rates, the dataset read and converted, attribution only to container projects), `tests/projects.test.ts` (the three kits; their generated Workers run against a stand-in `@cloudflare/containers` that reports what each container would start with: ports, sleep, `envVars`, n8n's decoded database settings and webhook address, the 503 without secrets, the health route; Free-only refused), `tests/cli.test.ts` (init's consent, git connect's consent and its Cloudflare builds with no workflow committed). 11 of 11 mutations caught. 947 unit tests, tsc. `npm run verify:kits -- umami` and `-- n8n`: install, build, review, and a Wrangler 4.154 deploy dry run that lists `qa-svc-umami-umami (docker.io/umamisoftware/umami:3.4.1)` and the n8n image, without Docker; `-- docker`: install, build and review, its dry run and local run need Docker (this VM has none). **Not run:** any container on Cloudflare, the image pulls, Umami and n8n against a real Postgres, the container usage dataset against a real account. Noticed: Wrangler 4.155.0 is out (Shipcel pins 4.154.0); not changed.
      - **Item 6, WordPress as an experimental kit (built, fixture-verified; never run: no Docker, PHP or MySQL in the VM):** the official image is `wordpress:7.1.4-php8.4-apache` (Docker Hub, 29 Oct). Hyperdrive serves Workers, not containers, so MySQL (8, or MariaDB 10.6+) is reached directly over TLS. `@cloudflare/containers` 0.3.7's `static outboundByHost` hands the container's HTTP calls to named hosts to the Worker, with its bindings, which is how uploads and mail work with no credential to create. **Built:** catalog `wordpress` is a kit now (labelled experimental everywhere); `wrangler.json`: the `WordPress` container from `./Dockerfile` (`basic`, one), its Durable Object and migration, an R2 draft `UPLOADS`, `send_email` `EMAIL`. The Dockerfile copies `wp-content/{mu-plugins,themes,plugins}` into the image. `src/worker.js`: `DATABASE_URL` → `WORDPRESS_DB_*`; the eight keys and salts as HMAC-SHA-256 of `WP_SECRET` (stable across restarts); `WORDPRESS_CONFIG_EXTRA` with `WP_HOME`/`WP_SITEURL` from the request's origin, HTTPS from `X-Forwarded-Proto`, `MYSQLI_CLIENT_SSL`, `DISALLOW_FILE_MODS`, no auto-updates; `uploads.shipcel.internal` (PUT/GET/DELETE into R2, keys with empty, `.` or `..` parts refused) and `mail.shipcel.internal` (`EMAIL.send` from `WP_MAIL_FROM`, 503 when unset); `/wp-content/uploads/*` served from R2 without waking the container; 503 until `DATABASE_URL` and `WP_SECRET` are set. `wp-content/mu-plugins/shipcel.php`: uploads and every generated size (and a scaled image's original) PUT to the Worker, deletions mirrored, a missing original fetched back before WordPress opens it, `pre_wp_mail` through the Worker (no attachments). The review accepts `send_email` entries of only a name (with the Email Service price warning), as does the upload policy for deploys from a computer; the budget guard's note says emails aren't estimated. Docs: the kits guide's WordPress section (route 3 is now the experimental kit), the runtime matrix row and checklist status, the not-yet list, ROADMAP item 6, the kit count (fourteen).
      - **Item 6's evidence:** `tests/projects.test.ts`: the kit's configuration and review; its Worker run with stand-ins for `@cloudflare/containers`, R2 and the Email Service (the database settings with the port defaulted and the password decoded, eight distinct 64-hex keys equal across two starts, `WP_HOME`, TLS, file mods off; uploads stored with their type, read back, served to visitors from R2, deleted; bad keys refused; mail sent from the owner's address, 503 without one; 503 without secrets); a malformed `send_email` refused. `tests/local-deploys.test.ts`: the upload policy's `send_email`. 7 of 7 mutations caught. 951 unit tests. `npm run verify:kits -- wordpress`: install, build, review, guard; image build, dry run and local run need Docker. The PHP parses with php-parser 3 (PHP 8 grammar, in the scratchpad, not a project dependency). **Unproven:** whether the container's requests to `*.shipcel.internal` resolve and reach `outboundByHost` (Cloudflare documents the handlers, not DNS for invented hosts), the PHP at runtime, WordPress against a real MySQL over TLS, and every item of the proof checklist.
      - **Item 7, GitLab, Bitbucket and Gitea (built, fixture-verified; not run from a real pipeline; finished in record 117):** researched: GitLab CI ID tokens (RS256, `iss` https://gitlab.com, `project_id`, `project_path`, `namespace_id`, `ref`, `ref_type`, `ref_protected`, `pipeline_source`; JWKS https://gitlab.com/oauth/discovery/keys); Bitbucket Pipelines' OIDC token is `BITBUCKET_STEP_OIDC_TOKEN` with custom audiences, but its claim names aren't in Atlassian's text; Gitea Actions' OIDC isn't confirmed released (go-gitea #33681, PR #33945). **Built, tests green:** migration `0038_git_sources.sql` (`git_sources`; `api_tokens.deploy`; `cli_logins.token_project`); `worker/github-oidc.ts` refactored into `signedClaims` + `checkedToken`, shared by GitHub and GitLab (GitHub's tests still pass); `worker/git-sources.ts`: `GET/PUT/DELETE /api/projects/:id/git-source` (one source per project, one project per repository, refused when the project deploys from GitHub) and unauthenticated `POST /api/git-sources/token` (GitLab.com jobs: the project and namespace ids pinned by the first job; production only from the protected production branch; pushes and web pipelines only; single use); deploy tokens through the approval page (`POST /api/cli/login` with `token.projectId`, checked against the approver's workspace, listed in `GET /api/tokens` with their project). `tests/git-sources.test.ts`: 4 tests pass. **Built, not yet tested:** the CLI's GitLab job sign-in (`SHIPCEL_ID_TOKEN`, `ciRun`), `git connect` for other hosts (`remoteRepository` in `packages/cli/src/git.ts`, `otherHostConnect`, `ciFile` writing `.gitlab-ci.yml`, `bitbucket-pipelines.yml` or `.gitea/workflows/shipcel.yml`), `tokens add --project`, the help texts. **Left:** (1) one failing test, `tests/cli.test.ts` "names the repository itself, or says what it needs": it pins the old `--repo` refusal, which now also names host/path; update its expected text (it may span lines). (2) CLI tests for the new paths, then mutation checks. (3) Docs (a guide for other Git hosts, the CLI guide, ROADMAP item 7), a dashboard view of the linked source. 953 of 954 unit tests pass; tsc clean.
      - **Items 8–11, the browser journeys and the final gate:** record 117.
116.  **"Did you update roadmap.md?"**, and while that turn ran, **"Against Coolify / remove this section too"** (30 Oct, in the QEMU VM).
      - Answered yes: items 4–6 were struck through in ROADMAP.md, each with what was built and what hasn't run live. Removed its "Against Coolify" section; its introduction now reads "This is what comes next, in order." Offered to change README.md's status line, which still pointed to that section; no answer came, and it changed with item 11 (record 117).
117.  **"items 8 to 11 (backups to R2, Telegram and email notifications with shared variables, row-level security and data APIs, the CLI on npm with a Coolify page), the browser test suite, and the final full test run. / Start on this and any remaining item, complete it test well end to end, ensure our code doesn't look like AI slop and is clean"** (30 Oct, in the QEMU VM; the session's usage ran out twice, and the owner said to continue).
      - **Item 7 finished** as record 115 describes it: its tests green, 11 of 11 mutations caught, `docs/guides/other-git-hosts.md`.
      - **Item 8, scheduled backups to R2 (built, fixture-verified; not run against a real account):** migration `0039_backups.sql`; `worker/backups.ts` keeps a schedule per project (daily or weekly, at an hour UTC, the newest 1 to 90 kept) and a bucket `<worker>-backups` that Shipcel makes (an existing bucket of that name is refused: Shipcel writes only into buckets it made). The minute cron starts due copies with D1's polling export (`POST /accounts/:id/d1/database/:id/export`, `current_bookmark`), moves each a step a minute, streams the signed dump into R2 (`PUT …/r2/buckets/:bucket/objects/:key`, at most 300 MB, the API's upload limit), deletes copies beyond the newest, gives up on an export after two hours and says why a copy failed. Keys carry the second and part of the copy's id. Free-only projects are refused (R2 can bill). Manage → Database → Scheduled copies (`src/Backups.tsx`): set, back up now, stop, and the list with downloads. Docs: the databases guide (restoring with `wrangler d1 execute`), the features page, ROADMAP.
      - **Item 9 (built, fixture-verified; not run against Telegram or a real sender):** migrations `0040_notification_channels.sql` (Telegram and email beside the webhook, one each per project; the outbox `webhook_deliveries` gains `channel`) and `0041_shared_variables.sql`. Telegram uses the customer's own bot: Shipcel checks the token (`getMe`), finds the chat from the bot's latest update (`getUpdates`; a chat id when the bot has a webhook of its own), says hello there and keeps the token sealed. Email comes from this Shipcel's sender through Cloudflare's Email Service (`NOTIFY_EMAIL`, `NOTIFY_FROM`; not set on shipcel.com), to an address only after someone with that inbox presses Confirm on the page its link opens (`worker/notification-pages.ts`: one button, a same-origin form post, a strict CSP; one confirmation per project every ten minutes), and every email has a stop link. Budget events go to every channel. Shared variables (`worker/shared-variables.ts`, a Shared variables page in the dashboard) set one secret on up to 20 of a workspace's projects, each write an operation in that project's history through the same `target()` and `change()` as `shipcel env add`, with values never stored; a project that missed a new value shows as out of date, and a project's secrets list marks the shared ones.
      - **Item 10 (built; tested in workerd with D1 and Durable Objects; not run on Cloudflare):** `services/backend/src/data.ts` serves `/data/<table>` over the app's own D1 database, bound to the sign-in service as `DATA`, for the tables the owner opens, each read and changed by everyone, signed-in users, each row's owner (its owner column holds the user's `sub`) or no one. Filters as PostgREST writes them, `select`, `order`, `limit` (at most 200) and `offset`; a new row takes the user's id as owner, a change never moves a row to someone else, and changing or deleting needs a filter; a broken constraint answers 409 with D1's reason. Every change goes out on realtime as `data:insert`, `data:update` or `data:delete` with `{ table, rows }` (a count past 32 KB): on `public:data:<table>`, on `signed-in:data:<table>` (a channel kind sign-in tokens may now join) or on the owner's `user:<id>`. Shipcel's side: migration `0042_backend_data.sql`, `GET` and `PUT /projects/:id/backend/data` (tables and columns read through D1's API; unknown tables or owner columns, and Cloudflare's or SQLite's own tables, refused), the service uploaded again only when its database changes, and Manage → Sign-in & realtime → Data API (`src/DataApi.tsx`). REST only, no GraphQL.
      - **Item 11:** the Coolify comparison at `/compare/coolify/`, from Coolify's pricing page, installation docs, GitHub README, and backup, notification and variable docs, read on 30 Oct (self-hosted free; Cloud $5 a month with 2 servers you bring, then $3 a server; at least 2 cores, 2 GB and 10 GB; Apache-2.0; 360+ one-click services). The CLI: npm metadata (repository, homepage, keywords, `publishConfig`), an npm README that leads with `npm install -g shipcel`, and `.github/workflows/publish-cli.yml` (run by hand; provenance and trusted publishing; the CLI's tests and `cli:build --check` first). `npm pack --dry-run` packs three files, 78.2 kB. **Not published:** it waits on the owner (Owner next, 5). README's status line now links the Coolify page instead of the removed roadmap section.
      - **Found and fixed on the way:** removing a project now also removes its Git source, backup schedule and list of copies, notification channels and shared-variable links (before, a deleted project's schedule would have kept copying its database, since its deployment jobs stay); two copies started in one minute shared an R2 key; a failed prune marked a stored copy failed (now it's logged and the copy stays done); a data API change could have handed a row to its editor (found reading the code, before its test); shared-variable removals came back in random order; and because items 5–6 made Docker and WordPress kits, the App catalog and Getting started read "GitHub imports … and for ." The unused "import" and "research" stages are gone from the catalog, the dashboard and the CLI, and the two journeys that tested them now test the WordPress kit and a Docker import.
      - **Evidence:** tests first in each slice. 987 unit tests in 49 files pass. Mutations: 80 of 80 caught once the gaps they showed were closed (backups 23, a deleted project's Git source 1 and the Git-source fixes 3, notifications 13 and the review's batch 4, shared variables 11, the data API in the service 17, rebuilding its bundle each time, and on Shipcel's side 8). Browser journeys: all 73 pass in the VM (`CI=1 npx playwright test`, 3.1 minutes, after the review's fixes too), four of them new (scheduled copies, Telegram and email, shared variables, data API) and two rewritten. tsc clean; Prettier clean except the two `.impeccable/critique/` files, unformatted at HEAD; `cli:build --check` and `backend:build --check` current; `npm audit` 0 vulnerabilities; `npm run verify:kits -- hono` passed; the new panels checked in screenshots at 1280 and 375 px.
      - **Review:** one read-only reviewer (a subagent) found 3 high, 8 medium and 4 low issues in the new code; fixed, each with a test and a mutation check:
        - **High.** A data API change answered with rows its author couldn't read (read `owner`, write `signed-in`): now no more people may change a table than read it, checked when saved and in the service, and a table no one reads only takes new rows. Email confirm and stop links were refused when opened from webmail, as cross-site navigations: those two GET pages are exempt, and their forms still post same-origin. The publish workflow checked a bundle the repository doesn't hold (`dist/` is ignored): it now builds it, packs only `dist/shipcel.js` (a stale `dist/cloudcel.js` sat beside it), and publishes from a second job, the only one that can request the OIDC identity.
        - **Medium.** A scheduled copy that couldn't start left no trace: the schedule now says why and tries again an hour later. Stopping forgot the bucket's name, so starting again was refused: stopping now pauses the schedule. Two minutes' runs could store one copy twice: a `storing` claim lets one run store it, and an upload that stalls is given up after twenty minutes. Free-only and the budget guard's pause didn't stop the cron: they do, and Free-only can't come back on while backups run. Removing and re-adding an address got round the confirmation wait: a workspace sends five confirmations an hour. The "taken" message named another workspace's project, and anyone could link a repository first: the message no longer names it, and a GitLab job must name its project (`--project`, which the CI file has), so a link someone else made deploys nothing of it. API tokens could open tables to the data API: that's dashboard-only now.
        - **Low.** `constructor` and other inherited names were taken for tables and operators (`Object.hasOwn` now); two D1-binding parsers and two remote parsers are one each; the data API handler is split; a D1 outage no longer reads as a disabled account; copy and comments corrected.
        - **Left, noted for later:** the data API has no rate limit and no cap on the rows one change reaches, so reads of a public table count against the app's D1 quota; `worker/backend.ts` is about 1,000 lines (851 before) and its data routes could move to a file of their own; the dashboard lists a database's columns with one query per table (at most 40).

119.  **"Update wranger everywhere and check for other updates needed too / Create LLM.txt so agents can start, link it to in main page, and also readme. / Add the videos in frontpage to readme as well host it in r2 / Also the app catalog should be extensible easily so developers can contribute, state how to contribute and guidelines in readme and a seperate .md how to create own extensions"** (31 Oct, in the QEMU VM; the usage limit paused it once).

With it `@cloudflare/vite-plugin` 1.66.0 (its own Wrangler moves from 4.152.0, which has advisories, to 4.154.0), `@cloudflare/workers-types`, `@types/node`, Hono 4.13.14 and lucide-react 1.65.0; `npm audit` 0. Kits: Astro 7.4.4 with adapter 14.4.3, SvelteKit 3.1.1 with adapter 8.0.2 and Svelte 5.57.3, Next.js 16.4.3 with OpenNext 1.20.15, Hono 4.13.14, n8n 2.46.3, and their example GitHub workflows on checkout, setup-node and upload-artifact v7 with Node 24. `publish-cli.yml` on upload-artifact v7 and download-artifact v8 (their breaking changes: Node 24 runners, and downloads by id, where this workflow downloads by name). **Not taken:** postgres 4.0.0 (released 29 Oct, a rewrite of its connection and socket layer): the Postgres kit stays on 3.4.9 until 4.0 has run against Hyperdrive. `PYTHON_WRANGLER_VERSION` stays 4.154.0, the one Python packages were verified with on the Mac, so a new Python kit reports Wrangler 4.156.0 as untested until the Mac's run (Owner next, 7).       - **The App catalog, one file per kit:** `kits/<id>.ts` holds a kit's catalog entry (`meta`) and what its export contains (`kit`); `kits/index.ts` lists both, and TypeScript refuses a kit on one list only; `kits/common.ts` holds what kits share and `kits/types.ts` documents every field. `worker/kits.ts` went from 885 lines to 198, the part every kit shares; `shared/catalog.ts` reads the catalog from `kits/`, and its hard-coded container and binding lists became `containers` and `bindings` on each kit. A script moved the code, copying each template by line range: for the 224 combinations validation allows, every kit's export is byte-for-byte what it was (the 12 that differ are Postgres with a database or storage, which validation refuses, and only in key order). `tests/kits.test.ts` holds the contract (listed once under the file's name, the catalog's fields, exact versions, the scripts and files every export has); an unlisted file and a `^` version each fail it. `npm run verify:kits` takes its kits from the catalog, and which build an image from their configuration, so a new kit needs no edit there. The dashboard bundle carries no kit templates (searched); the CLI's grew 4 KB, two services' README prose, after hoisting the values that kept three kits' objects in it.
      - **Docs:** `docs/guides/add-a-starter-kit.md` (on the site under Run Shipcel: the kit file, the steps, guidelines, the checks), README's **Contributing** section with the guidelines, `kits/` and `media/` in README's folder list, and a link from the starter-kits guide. Found on the way: `/docs/starter-kits/` showed `[the runtime matrix](../runtime-matrix.md#…)` as raw text, since that file isn't on the site; the sentence no longer links, and a new test fails on any Markdown link the renderer leaves unconverted (it catches that one).
      - **llms.txt:** `site/llms.ts` writes `/llms.txt` (llmstxt.org): what Shipcel is, install, sign in (`SHIPCEL_TOKEN` without a browser), deploy, the MCP server, then every guide with its first sentence. Every guide is also published as Markdown at `/docs/<slug>.md`, which those links point to. Linked from the front page's agent card, every page's footer, and README. Tested.
      - **The tour in R2:** moved from `public/media/` to `media/`, so it is no longer a Worker asset. `npm run media:publish` (`scripts/publish-media.mjs`) creates the bucket `shipcel-media`, uploads both files with their types and a day's caching, connects `media.shipcel.com` (it asks once for shipcel.com's zone ID), and reads them back, the video with a range as players ask. The front page and README link to `https://media.shipcel.com/tour.webm` and `tour.webp`, and the CSP allows that host for images and media. The names are stable because whether R2's custom domains ignore a query string isn't documented. GitHub doesn't play a video from another host in a README, so README shows the poster, which opens the video. The browser journeys answer media.shipcel.com from `media/`, so they need neither the network nor the upload. **Not run:** the upload (the VM has no Cloudflare sign-in; it ran from the Mac on 31 Oct, record 120). Until `npm run media:publish` runs, the live front page's poster and video would fail, so it comes before the deploy (Owner next, 5). Cost: 5.5 MB in R2, inside its free 10 GB; reads and egress are free.
      - **Evidence:** 993 unit tests in 50 files; tsc clean; Prettier clean across the repository, now that `.prettierignore` skips `.impeccable/` (snapshots the review tool writes and reads back; the two committed on 28 Oct had kept `npm run format:check`, which CI runs, failing); the CLI, service and site bundles current; `npm audit` 0. `verify:frameworks` with Wrangler 4.156.0, one fixture at a time in the VM: astro, html, nextjs, sveltekit and vite-react passed; the sveltekit-autoconfig probe fails as recorded on the Mac. `verify:kits` one kit at a time: react, nextjs, vue, hono, postgres, astro, sveltekit, static and worker passed in full; umami and n8n passed all but the local run, which needs Docker; docker and wordpress passed install, build, the import plan and the configuration guard, their dry runs and local runs needing Docker; Python not run (no pipx in the VM). The recorded evidence files are untouched, since a partial run would have replaced them. Browser journeys: all 74 pass in the VM (`CI=1 npx playwright test`, 3.2 minutes), after one count was updated (19 docs pages with the new guide); the site journeys answer media.shipcel.com from `media/`. Nothing committed; not deployed; the R2 upload not run (it ran on 31 Oct, record 120).
120.  **"Its stuck here"** (31 Oct, in the QEMU VM), with the output of `npm run media:publish` on the Mac, which stopped after "4. Reading them back".
      - **Found:** the upload had worked: the bucket was already there, both files uploaded, and media.shipcel.com connected. Through Cloudflare's edge, from the VM, `tour.webm` answers a range with 206 `video/webm` and `content-range: bytes 0-1/5434706`, and `tour.webp` with 200 `image/webp` and 44,204 bytes, both `cache-control: public, max-age=86400`. shipcel.com's nameservers, 1.1.1.1 and 8.8.8.8 all give the domain's addresses, but the Mac's resolver (192.168.64.1 from the VM) still answered NXDOMAIN, which may be kept for up to 1,800 s (shipcel.com's SOA). Something asked for media.shipcel.com before the domain existed, possibly the read-back itself straight after connecting it. The read-back asked that resolver and retried without printing anything, 20 tries 15 s apart for each file. Reproduced in the VM with a stand-in for Wrangler: nothing after "4. Reading them back" for 75 s.
      - **Fixed** in `scripts/publish-media.mjs`: the read-back takes the address from shipcel.com's own nameservers, so no resolver's cache holds it back and it can't leave a "no such host" in one either; each failed try is printed with its error and the wait before the next; each request gives up after 20 s. Until the Mac's resolver drops its answer, the Mac's own browser won't show the tour; other networks see it.
      - **Evidence:** with the stand-in, both files read back in about a second (206 and 200, exit 0) while the VM's resolver still said NXDOMAIN; with a host that doesn't exist (3 tries, 1 s apart) each try printed ENOTFOUND and it exited 1; through the system resolver instead (the old lookup) each try printed ENETUNREACH and it exited 1. `node --check` and Prettier pass; only `npm run media:publish` runs the script. Not yet run on the Mac. Nothing committed; not deployed.

Node 22.13.0, the Mac's on 22 Oct, is kept in `node-v22.13.0`; rollback is removing the link and renaming that folder back. Playwright's headless Chromium is build 1248 (Playwright 1.64.1). The same goes for `package-lock.json` after an `npm install` there. - A new migration needs `npm run db:migrate` in the copy too: otherwise the browser journeys' cleanup fails silently (500s), their projects pile up, and the Free plan's 100-project limit then fails unrelated journeys (23 Oct 2026). Remove left-over `qa-` projects through the local API (`DELETE /api/projects/:id` with `X-Shipcel-Request: 1`), not by opening `.wrangler/state`. - Never `pkill -f` a pattern that also appears in the command being run: it kills the agent's own shell (exit 144). Start servers with `setsid … &`, keep `$!`, and `kill -- -$PID`. - The VM has network access, so a feature that drives an outside tool can be run on real apps, not only fixtures: scaffold them in the session scratchpad with the frameworks' own tools (`create-next-app`, `create-react-router`, `sv create`), run Wrangler from the copy's `node_modules` with an environment that can't reach Cloudflare (`CI=true`, an invalid `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, an empty `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`), and run the built app with `wrangler dev` to see whether it answers. No live checks against the owner's accounts are possible from the VM (their Cloudcel runs on the Mac at 127.0.0.1); say so, and give the owner the commands to run on the Mac. Don't commit or push unless the owner asks (their standing rule in `HANDOFF.md`). See [[vercel-simple-terminal-first]].

**`gateguard-hook-facts.md`** (reference) — _The ECC GateGuard hook in this environment denies some tool calls once until facts are stated; what triggers it and how to get through in one retry_ A GateGuard "Fact-Forcing Gate" PreToolUse hook is active in the Cloudcel sessions (seen 12–13 Oct 2026). It denies a call once and passes the identical retry after the facts are stated in the message. - **First Bash command of a session, and the first Write of every new file** (scratchpad files, migrations and memory files included): state the file and lines that will call it, that no existing file serves the purpose, the data schema it reads or writes, and the user's instruction verbatim. Stating them before the first attempt did not avoid the denial; expect one denial per new file, so don't create files that an inline edit would do. - **Bash commands that look destructive**: `rm -rf`, `pkill`, `rsync --delete`, and also SQL `DELETE` inside a heredoc of test code (13 Oct 2026). State the files or data touched, a one-line rollback, and the user's instruction verbatim. Cheaper: avoid the trigger (`mktemp -d` instead of removing, rsync without `--delete`, expire a row instead of deleting it in a test). - **The first Edit of every existing file in a session** (added 22 Oct 2026: 13 denials in one session). The facts asked depend on the file: for source, the callers or importers found by a search run this turn (Grep, or `grep` whose path contains the file) and the public functions affected; for tests, the behaviour and module under test and the existing test files; for config, which process reads it and that no secret is written; for docs, what references the section and what the change corrects or adds; for every file, the user's instruction verbatim. In a parallel batch of edits to one file only the first is denied and the rest apply, so retry just that one; batch a file's edits. - **Background servers:** stop them with TaskStop (load it with ToolSearch), not `kill`/`pkill`. - Don't gate a patch behind `grep … &&`: when grep finds nothing the patch silently doesn't run. Assert inside the patch instead (each replacement must match exactly once). See `vm-toolchain-and-verify-copy.md`.

**`commands-for-the-owners-mac.md`** (feedback) — _How to give Cloudcel's owner commands to run on their Mac, learned from a live run that went to the wrong folder_ When giving the owner commands to run on the Mac, give every step as its own command, including the `cd`, and put test apps outside the Cloudcel folder under a name that is not one of their projects. **Why:** on 13 Oct 2026 the instructions said "create a React Router app … then, inside `my-app`:" with no `cd` line. The owner ran `npx create-react-router@latest my-app` inside `/Users/zayaan_1/Downloads/Cloudcel` and then ran `cloudcel deploy --prod` from the Cloudcel folder, so the CLI reviewed Cloudcel's own `wrangler.jsonc` and refused it with a confusing message. Two more problems with those instructions: Cloudcel's dev server watches its own folder and reloads when a nested app's files change, and `my-app` is the name of the owner's existing project for their landing page (`Xayaan/Royal-Protocol-Landing`), which the CLI offers to link a folder of that name to. **How to apply:** say where the commands run: the Mac's own Terminal app, not this session's shell, which is the Linux VM (on 15 Oct 2026 the owner typed `npm run dev` into the session after being given Mac commands, and it failed there; see `vm-toolchain-and-verify-copy.md`). Write `cd <absolute path>` as a command before anything that depends on the folder; create scratch apps in the home folder (for example `~/rr-test`), never inside the repository; check `HANDOFF.md` ("Owner's live setup") for existing project names before suggesting one; say what each question the CLI asks will be and what to answer. The owner pastes terminal output back without comment: read which folder each command ran in from the prompt. See `vm-toolchain-and-verify-copy.md` and `vercel-simple-terminal-first.md`.

## Completed slices (newest first)

### Bittensor light client example (22 Oct 2026) — implemented, run locally against live Finney; not deployed

**What.** `examples/bittensor-light-client` is a Bittensor (Finney) light client on Cloudflare, deployable with `cloudcel deploy --prod` from a copy outside the repo. **It needs Workers Paid.** Cloudflare's Durable Object limits page says Durable Objects follow Workers' per-invocation CPU limits, which on Workers Free are 10 ms, and verifying blocks takes more. The agent's first answer (session record 53) said only that one always-on Durable Object uses about 10,800 of the 13,000 GB-seconds a day the free plan includes, and flagged the CPU limits as unproven. The owner was first told plainly that Workers Paid is needed in the deploy question (record 55). **Rechecked on 22 Oct (record 61).** The limits page opens with "Durable Objects are a special kind of Worker, so Workers Limits apply according to your Workers plan", and Workers' limits and pricing pages give Workers Free "10 milliseconds of CPU time per invocation". The same page's own tables list "CPU per request: 30 seconds (default)" with no Free or Paid column, which is easy to misread as applying to Free too. No page says it does, and it hasn't been tried on a Free account. Everything else fits Workers Free: Durable Objects with SQLite storage only (this one uses it), about 10,800 of the 13,000 GB-seconds a day, and about 4,300 of the 100,000 Durable Object requests a day from the alarm and the cron. Going over a daily limit fails until 00:00 UTC; it doesn't bill.

**Why a light client.** A Subtensor "lite node" is a full node with warp sync (16 GB RAM, about 128 GB of NVMe disk, an inbound p2p port), so it can't run serverless. smoldot can. It verifies the chain itself; it isn't a proxy to an RPC provider.

**Files.**

- `src/index.js`: the Worker and the `LightClient` Durable Object. One object, named `finney`. Since 22 Oct (record 66), JSON-RPC is over WebSocket only. The Worker answers `/` itself (the page, the description, 405 for HTTP JSON-RPC). The WebSocket also takes `lightclient_status`, what `/status` reports.
  - It starts smoldot with `src/smoldot.wasm` (imported as a compiled module: Workers forbids compiling Wasm at run time, and smoldot's npm build would) and `src/finney.json`. It starts from the saved state (`ctx.storage` key `database`) or from `src/finney-checkpoint.json`; if the saved state is refused, it falls back to the checkpoint.
  - An alarm every 30 s keeps the object in memory, and every 10 minutes it saves `chainHead_unstable_finalizedDatabase` (max 1,000,000 bytes, so without the 2.5 MiB runtime).
  - A cron every minute sends `/heartbeat` a request. Cloudflare documents that a Durable Object's 30 s of CPU is renewed by each incoming HTTP request or WebSocket message, and doesn't say whether an alarm renews it, so the documented renewal is the cron's request. It also wakes the object if it was evicted or its alarm was lost. (The cron was every 5 minutes until the owner's question in record 57; the deploy question still said 5 minutes.)
  - Routes (each under `/<key>` when `RPC_KEY` is set):
    - WebSocket upgrade on any path: JSON-RPC with subscriptions, batches of 1–100, and `lightclient_status`.
    - `POST /`: 405 from the Worker, with the WebSocket's address. Until 22 Oct this was JSON-RPC over HTTP.
    - `GET /status`: JSON.
    - `GET /` with `Accept: text/html`: `src/page.js`, from the Worker. A live page of verified finalized and best blocks, all over its WebSocket.
    - Any other `GET /`: JSON describing the endpoint, from the Worker.
  - `captureMemory()` wraps `WebAssembly.instantiate` once, so `/status` can report smoldot's Wasm memory.
  - Logs drop smoldot's legacy-API warnings and peers' `protocol-error` lines.
- `src/platform.js`: smoldot's platform for Workers.
  - TCP through the injected `connect` (`cloudflare:sockets`; IPv6 hostnames bracketed, `allowHalfOpen`).
  - WebSocket peers through `fetch` with `Upgrade: websocket`. No WebRTC.
  - Rules: nothing throws into smoldot or calls it back synchronously, since `connect()` runs inside its Wasm and a JS exception there is reported as a crash. No callback runs after a reset from either side.
- `src/history.js` (22 Oct, record 67): the verified history and old blocks.
  - **`History`:**
    - `imported` / `finalized`: fed by the object's own `chain_subscribeAllHeads` and `chain_subscribeFinalizedHeads`.
    - `route`: `smoldot` for blocks it imported within 200 of the best, else `archive`.
    - `answer`: checked storage (`state_getStorage(At)`, `state_getStorageHash(At)`, `state_queryStorageAt`, via `state_getReadProof` against the recorded or hash-checked state root) and `chain_getHeader`; anything else passed through with `unverified`.
    - `blockHash`: from the record, verified; older, unverified.
    - `fill`: gaps newest first, then back `historyBlocks`, in batches of 50; stops at the first header that doesn't link.
    - At most 8 archive calls at once.
  - **`sqlStore`:** the `blocks` table (number, hash, parent_hash, state_root).
  - **`archiveNode`:** JSON-RPC over HTTP, with batches, an 8 MB answer limit and a 30 s timeout.
- `src/proof.js` (22 Oct): hex and SCALE compact, `headerHash` (BLAKE2b of the SCALE header), and `readProof(root, key, proof)` for Substrate's base-16 trie, state versions 0 and 1 (hashed values). `ProofError` covers anything incomplete or malformed. `src/blake2b.js` is RFC 7693 BLAKE2b-256, matched against Python's `hashlib`.
- `src/router.js` (22 Oct): `AT` (each block-pinned method's block parameter); the per-block cache (`#shared`, `#keep`, `cacheStats`); pinning (`#pin`); the archive route and fallback (`#fetch`); `#blockHash`.
- `src/access.js` (22 Oct): who may use it, and how much.
  - `unlock`: with the `RPC_KEY` secret set, every address starts with `/<key>`. The Worker compares SHA-256 digests and takes the key off; anything else is 401 before the Durable Object. The Worker also refuses `/heartbeat` from outside; the cron calls the object directly.
  - `RateLimit`: per caller (`clientOf`: the IP, or the IPv6 /64 from `CF-Connecting-IP`), 20 calls a second and 200 at once, a batch counting each call (`RATE`). It answers 429 with `Retry-After`, or -32005 on a WebSocket. There are also 8 WebSockets per caller (`MAX_SOCKETS_PER_CALLER`). Kept in memory, forgotten by the alarm once whole again, 10,000 callers at most.
  - **Why in the object:** Cloudflare's rate-limit binding would stop floods before the object, but Cloudcel's deploy policy (`worker/wrangler-policy.ts`) refuses `ratelimit` bindings. The key does that job instead.
  - The page and `scripts/check.mjs` keep every address under the one they were given.
- `src/router.js`: one smoldot chain shared by all clients.
  - Each request goes in under the router's own ID (`r<n>`). Notifications are routed by `params.subscription` to their owner, and only the owner may unsubscribe. A closed connection's subscriptions are closed, including one confirmed after it left.
  - Subscriptions need a WebSocket; 64 per connection.
  - `PRIVATE` methods are refused unless `trusted`: `sudo_*`, `system_add/removeReservedPeer` and `chainHead_unstable_finalizedDatabase`. They would let anyone point the client at any address.
  - At most 4 runtime calls at once (`state_call`, `state_getMetadata`, `payment_*`, `system_accountNextIndex`, `system_dryRun`); 256 may wait.
  - smoldot's two documented-retryable errors get 3 attempts (300 and 600 ms apart).
  - Metadata is cached per runtime version (`specName/specVersion/implVersion` plus the call), shared by concurrent asks, 4 kept, and failures aren't kept.
  - A request times out after 30 s.
  - smoldot subscription IDs are 32 random bytes (`light-base/src/json_rpc_service/background.rs`).
- `src/finney.json`: the light chain spec. No genesis storage (the state root only), no `protocolId`, no code substitute, no telemetry, no `lightSyncState`. Made by `scripts/finney-chain-spec.py` from Subtensor's `raw_spec_finney.json` (12,144,055 bytes, sha256 `f280b687…f105`). The genesis header comes from a full node and must hash to Finney's genesis hash `0x2f05…6c03`.
- `src/finney-checkpoint.json`: smoldot's database for the spec's `grandpaWarpSyncCheckpoint`. Made by `scripts/finney-checkpoint.py`, which takes nothing from the network: the header hashes to `0x5119…0200` (#8,867,448), and the authorities come from its digest (20 Aura keys; FRNK ScheduledChange, 20 keys, delay 0). Aura slot 12,000 ms, GRANDPA set 6, and 30 known peers with public addresses (taken from a smoldot database saved during the session).
  - Both generated JSON files are in `.prettierignore`, and both scripts reproduce them byte for byte.
- `scripts/check.mjs <address> [full node]`: checks a running light client against a full node, byte for byte, at its finalized block. It compares canonically, setting aside the full node's `systemVersion` field, which smoldot doesn't return. It also checks the operator-method refusal and two finalized heads over WebSocket.
- `scripts/grandpa-set-check.mjs <rpc> <block>…`: prints which GRANDPA set IDs verify a block's stored justification. Use it when the checkpoint changes.
- `smoldot/`: patches `0001` (choose Aura or BABE from the finalized header's pre-runtime digest, when the runtime has both APIs and the block isn't genesis), `0002` (start from a database whose genesis hash matches, when the spec has no `lightSyncState`) and `0003` (`ruzstd` 0.8.2 to 0.9.1, for RUSTSEC-2026-0344; finding 5). Also `build.sh` (clones paritytech/smoldot tag `npm-smoldot-v3.7.0`, checks commit `cd98d7fa…`, applies the patches, `cargo build --locked … --profile min-size-release` for `wasm32-unknown-unknown`, local paths remapped), the GPL text and a README.
  - The first build embedded 284 local paths (`/Users/zayaan_1/.cargo`, the scratch folder); remapping removed them.
  - Two clean builds were byte-identical, with patches 0001 and 0002: 5,529,984 bytes, sha256 `8fc2373f…a609`. With 0003 too, two more: 5,530,407 bytes, sha256 `5591b0fc6a77bfc010c0bbe14dcd95daec9f7d5bdc3fd5ed4b6bc74052a8622e`, rustc 1.93.1. The committed file is the second.
- `package.json`: `smoldot` pinned at 3.7.0, since its JS must match the Wasm; the lockfile is included.
- `wrangler.jsonc`: the `alias` `smoldot-client` points at `node_modules/smoldot/dist/mjs/internals/client.js`, because the package exports only its Node, Deno and browser bindings and the browser one forbids TCP. Cloudcel's local deploy accepts `alias` (a local build key), `durable_objects`, `migrations`, `triggers` and `observability: { enabled }`.
- `tests/bittensor-light-client.test.ts`: 14 unit tests for the router and the platform with a fake chain and fake sockets. All 15 mutations were caught (the script was in the session scratchpad: each guarded line removed or altered in turn). On 22 Oct, 4 more for `src/access.js`, written just after it, not before; all 17 of its mutations were caught. The wiring in `src/index.js` has no unit tests, since it needs the Workers runtime. It was run in workerd against live Finney instead (QA report, 22 October, first entry). Later on 22 Oct (record 67), 13 more covered the hash, the proofs, the history and the cache, using real data from block #9,000,000 in `tests/fixtures/finney-9000000.json`; 31 in all, and 44 more mutations caught.
- `docs/guides/cli.md` mentions the example; `docs/qa-report.md` has the evidence (22 October).

**Findings on the way.**

1. **Upstream smoldot can't follow Finney.** A warp sync from the genesis stops at a forced GRANDPA change. It refuses a database without `lightSyncState` ("Either a checkpoint or the genesis storage must be provided"). And Finney's runtime advertises AuraApi and BabeApi together (`MultipleConsensusAlgorithms`). Hence the two patches.
2. **GRANDPA set 6, not the spec's 5.** Proved with Finney's own justifications through Opentensor's archive node (`archive.chain.opentensor.ai`; the entrypoint is pruned): #8,867,448's verifies with set 5, #8,867,840's and #8,868,352's with set 6. The runtime's `Grandpa::CurrentSetId` is 5 at #8,867,447 and 6 from #8,867,448. 10 of the 20 GRANDPA keys rotate at the checkpoint.
3. **Peers turn proof requests away under load.** 8 of 24 concurrent metadata calls failed ("Error trying to access the storage required for the runtime call"; smoldot documents it as retryable). Hence the 4-call limit, the retries and the metadata cache. A call proof includes the 2.5 MiB `:code`.
4. Subtensor's runtime imports BLS12-381 host functions smoldot lacks; smoldot warns once at start.

5. **A security advisory in smoldot's zstd decoder: RUSTSEC-2026-0344** (`ruzstd` before 0.9.1, issued 13 Oct 2026, CVSS 7.5). A crafted input under 128 KB makes the decoder allocate gigabytes, a denial of service only. smoldot 3.7.0 locks `ruzstd` 0.8.2 and uses it only to decompress runtime code (`lib/src/executor/host/zstd.rs`), which arrives with storage proofs. Exploiting it would need a Finney validator to sign a fake best block whose state carries such code; that would crash the object out of memory, and it would restart. Umi-BitSign pins 0.9.1 in its vendored smoldot. **Fixed the same day** by patch `0003` (0.9.1, no code change). smoldot's 4 zstd tests passed with it, and so did `scripts/check.mjs` against live Finney, after a runtime upgrade (below).

**Prior art and sources (read 22 Oct 2026).**

- **Umi-BitSign** (github.com/Umi-BitSign/umi, Apache-2.0; its vendored smoldot stays GPL). `rust/grandpa-finality-observer` embeds smoldot against Finney's boot nodes from a hash-pinned spec, starting at the same checkpoint. Its `vendor/PATCHES.md` describes the same two changes: an engine from the pre-runtime digest, failing closed when ambiguous; and bootstrapping from a genesis-matched database. It makes them to smoldot 2.2.0 and smoldot-light 1.3.2. Ours were written for 3.7.0, not copied.
- **Providers the owner was told about:** OnFinality, GetBlock, Nodies, and Blockmachine (free, keyless, says its gateway checks responses with Merkle proofs). No Bittensor RPC on Cloudflare Workers was found, and no smoldot on Workers.
- **Cloudflare, TCP sockets page:**
  - Outbound `connect()` only; inbound only from another Worker.
  - Cloudflare's IP ranges, `localhost`, private addresses and port 25 are blocked.
  - Sockets "cannot be created in global scope or shared across requests".
  - Each open socket counts toward the simultaneous-connection limit, and in a Durable Object it keeps the object in memory for up to 15 minutes.
- **Cloudflare, Durable Object limits page:**
  - CPU: 30 s by default on Workers Paid, up to 5 minutes with `limits.cpu_ms`.
  - "Each incoming HTTP request or WebSocket message resets the remaining available CPU time to 30 seconds"; alarms aren't mentioned.
  - Durable Objects follow Workers' per-invocation CPU limits (10 ms on Workers Free).
  - Workers Free has SQLite-backed objects only, 5 GB in all.
- **Cloudflare, Containers limits page:** at most standard-4: 4 vCPU, 12 GiB, 20 GB of disk.
- **Bittensor's node requirements:** 4 cores, 16 GB of RAM, about 128 GB (lite) or 3.5 TB or more (archive), NVMe.

**Experiments before the example** (Node 22, in the scratchpad):

- Upstream smoldot from the genesis connected fine (10 or more peers, the WebSocket boot node) but stopped at the forced GRANDPA change. With the full 12 MB spec, Node's RSS was about 200 MB, hence the light spec with only the genesis state root.
- Upstream refused a database without `lightSyncState`, and refused the chain for the Aura/BABE mix.
- With both patches, the chain started from the checkpoint, but every warp-sync proof failed with `BadSignature` until the set ID was corrected to 6. Then it reached the finalized block, verified the runtime (spec 481, 2.5 MiB `:code`, built in 78 ms) and followed finality.

**Session scratch, not kept** (`/private/tmp/claude-504/…/scratchpad`, gone after the session; rebuild from these notes if needed):

- `tools/node-checkpoint.mjs`: runs smoldot in Node from a spec and a database, with a custom Wasm, and prints the finalized block every 15 s.
- `tools/extract-wasm.mjs`: inflates the npm package's Wasm.
- `tools/node-sync.mjs`, `tools/node-warp-debug.mjs` and `tools/decode-checkpoint.py`.
- `tools/grandpa-set-probe.mjs`: the storage reads of `Grandpa::CurrentSetId` through the archive node; its justification check became `scripts/grandpa-set-check.mjs`.
- `tools/ws-check.mjs` and `tools/compare-calls.mjs`: became `scripts/check.mjs`.
- `tools/load-check.mjs`: N concurrent `get_neurons_lite` calls with Wasm memory before and after.
- `tools/mutate-light-client.py`: the 15 mutations below.
- The downloaded spec and the smoldot clone with both patches.

**The 15 mutations** (each caught by `tests/bittensor-light-client.test.ts`). Each is a code change; the tests caught every one:

1. Any connection may close any subscription.
2. A subscription confirmed after its connection closed is kept.
3. No retries.
4. Every error retried.
5. Five runtime calls at once.
6. Metadata kept across runtime versions.
7. A failed metadata answer kept.
8. Operator methods open to callers.
9. Subscriptions over HTTP.
10. Answers keep the router's ID.
11. Notifications sent to every subscriber.
12. A failed `connect` calls smoldot back synchronously.
13. Callbacks after a reset.
14. IPv6 hostnames unbracketed.
15. A refused WebSocket upgrade unreported.

**Evidence (local only).** Wrangler 4.148.0's local runtime, live Finney peers and Opentensor's full node:

- **Sync:** warp sync within 5 s to #9,334,180, then finality block by block, every 12.5 s. Restarting from the saved state caught up within 30 s.
- **Reads:** identical to the full node for `System.Number`, `Timestamp.Now`, the runtime version, `get_subnet_hyperparams`, `get_neurons_lite(1)` (47 KB, 1–3.4 s), `get_all_dynamic_info` (53 KB) and the metadata (364 KB). `get_delegates` failed after 19 s, and an old `chain_getBlockHash` returns `null`.
- **Load:** after the fix, 24 concurrent metadata requests and 12 concurrent neuron queries all succeeded. Wasm memory was 42 MB after sync, 58 MB from saved state, and 78 MB after metadata, stable under load. smoldot reports about 0.01 CPU cores.
- **WebSocket:** legacy and `chainHead_v1` subscriptions worked, and a stolen unsubscribe returned `false`.
- **Page:** checked in the browser pane, including at 375 px with no sideways scroll.
- **Node:** under Node, the patched Wasm reached the same finalized block (RSS 133–168 MB, Node included).

**Not established:** anything on Cloudflare itself. Also, a public address has no rate limit: anyone who finds it uses the owner's allowance. And the licence of the example's own code is the owner's call (the Wasm and the patches are GPL-3.0-or-later WITH Classpath-exception-2.0).

### Front page: pictures, demo and tour video (15 Oct 2026) — implemented, fixture-verified

At the owner's request (session record item 49). What was wrong: no pictures, diagram or video; a 60-word opening; the same claims (Git deploys, previews, "free, no markup") repeated in four sections; the free plan as 12 + 9 lines in full; jargon (Workers, D1, R2, KV, GB-seconds) without plain words; analytics and the budget guard absent.

- **Structure** (`site/home.ts`, which `site/render.ts` `landing()` calls; `freePlanCards` moved there and `/pricing/` imports it; `list` moved to `site/markup.ts`): hero with the deploy demo → How it works (diagram + "No infrastructure code", `noCode` reworded with bold leads) → Start your way (icons and a glimpse of each way: a prompt, `git push` → preview address, `$ cloudcel --prod`, a `dist/` drop zone) → See it in action (`#tour`, the video) → Everything you expect (eight features; `platformFeatures` now has `icon`, shorter text, and "Analytics and logs" and "Built for teams" added) → No surprise bills (budget meter at 50/80/100%, the projected-overrun alert, Free-only, alert-or-pause, the free plan stopping) → What the free plan includes (six figures, the stop at daily limits, both plans in a `<details>`) → tiers → Why it costs less (a typical stack of five plans and bills against one account; three reasons) → the call to action. The opening now says "Put your websites and apps on Cloudflare, in your own account, with no infrastructure code…"; the second button is **Watch the tour**. The Alpha notice is unchanged.
- **The deploy demo**: the five output lines are the CLI's own wording for a first deploy of a Vite app (`demoOutput` and `demoProduction`, exported; the "Project name" and "Customize settings?" prompts are left out), and a test checks the wording is still in `packages/cli/src/cli.ts`. A 16-second CSS loop (typing, then line by line, the live app, the traffic bars); things appear with `visibility` and movement, never half-transparent text (a test reads the keyframes), so an accessibility scan can't catch a half-faded line; every piece's resting style is the end of the story, so reduced motion and screenshots show the whole picture. The moving stage is `aria-hidden`; its caption says what happens. Pause: `.demo:has(#demo-pause:checked)`.
- **The tour video**: `npm run site:media` (`playwright.media.config.ts` → `scripts/site-media.spec.ts`, not part of `test:browser`) needs the dev server; it creates a project `storefront` (deleted afterwards), answers every Cloudflare-backed route with sample data (builds and a streaming build log, versions, analytics for 24 h and 7 days, status codes, logs, visitors, the budget), and records 1280×800 WebM with a pointer, captions, and a title and end card (the card also hides the dashboard loading in development). It writes `public/media/tour.webm` and the poster `tour.webp` (the analytics view, converted to WebP in the browser). Times are UTC. Re-record after the dashboard's look changes. On the page: `controls preload="none"`, no autoplay, `aria-describedby` the list of its four steps, "recorded with sample data". Chrome, Edge and Firefox play WebM; older Safari shows the poster and the steps.
- **CSS**: `public/home.css` (hand-written, Prettier-formatted, loaded only by `/` through `layout({ styles })`). Icons are inline SVG drawn for the page (`icons` in `site/home.ts`), stroked in the text's colour.
- **Tests**: `tests/site.test.ts` adds: every `src`/`poster` is a file in `public/`; the demo's lines and their wording in the CLI, the pause and reduced-motion rules, no half-transparent keyframes; the video waits to be played, has its poster and steps, and stays under 8 MB. `tests/browser/site.spec.ts` adds a journey: the demo plays, pauses and resumes; with reduced motion nothing moves and every part shows; **Watch the tour** goes to `#tour`; the video is paused with its poster and decodes at 1280×800. Mutation check (`scratchpad/mutate-home.py`): 11 of 11 caught. `tsconfig.json` now includes `scripts/`.
- **Not done**: no dark theme for the public site (it had none); the tour shows "Local workspace" because it is recorded on a local Cloudcel; a video file of 5.4 MB lives in the repository (move it to R2 or Stream when hosting is decided).
