Facts gathered before building (15 Oct 2026), so they needn't be looked up again:

- **Cloudflare's GraphQL schema, from a third-party introspection dump** (pages.johnspurlock.com/graphql-schema-docs/cloudflare.html, saved as text in the session scratchpad `cf-schema/schema.txt`; `scratchpad/schema_fields.py` and `schema_check.py` print a type's fields). Every field the budget guard queries exists: `workersInvocationsAdaptive` (sum `requests`, dims `scriptName`, `date`; filters `date_geq`/`date_leq`), `durableObjectsInvocationsAdaptiveGroups` (sum `requests`, dims `namespaceId`, `date`), `durableObjectsPeriodicGroups` (sum `rowsRead`, `rowsWritten`, `activeTime` µs, **`duration` GB-s**, `storageReadUnits`, `storageWriteUnits`, `storageDeletes`), `d1AnalyticsAdaptiveGroups` (sum `rowsRead`, `rowsWritten`, dims `databaseId`, `date`), `kvOperationsAdaptiveGroups` (sum `requests`, dims `namespaceId`, `actionType`, `date`), `r2OperationsAdaptiveGroups` (sum `requests`, dims `bucketName`, `actionType`, `date`). So the guard's queries are schema-correct (still not run live).
- **`workersInvocationsAdaptive` quantiles are in microseconds**: `cpuTimeP25…P999`, `wallTimeP50/P99…`, `requestDurationP50…`, `durationP50…` (GB-s), `memoryUsageBytesP…`, `responseBodySizeP…`; sums `requests`, `errors`, `subrequests`, `wallTime`, `cpuTimeUs`, `duration`, `requestDuration`, `responseBodySize`, `clientDisconnects`; dimensions `date`, `datetimeHour`, `datetimeSixHours`, `status` (invocation outcome), `cacheStatus`, `coloCode`, `scriptVersion`… **There is no HTTP status code dimension.** **Bug found:** Manage → Analytics shows `cpuTimeP50`/`P99` as milliseconds, so it reads 1,000× too high (to fix in item 2; the CLI's `cloudcel metrics` has the same bug).
- **Web Analytics (RUM)**: `GET /accounts/{id}/rum/site_info/list` needs only Account Settings Read (the owner's grant has `account-settings.read`); each site has `site_tag`, `site_token`, `snippet`, `auto_install`, `ruleset { zone_name, zone_tag, enabled }`, `rules [{ host, paths, inclusive, is_paused }]`. Creating a site (`POST …/rum/site_info`) needs Account Settings Write, which Cloudcel doesn't ask for. GraphQL: `rumPageloadEventsAdaptiveGroups` (`count` = page views, `sum { visits }`; dims `requestPath`, `requestHost`, `refererHost`, `countryName`, `deviceType`, `userAgentBrowser`, `userAgentOS`, `date`, `datetimeHour`, `bot`; filter `siteTag`, `datetime_geq`/`datetime_leq`) and `rumWebVitalsEventsAdaptiveGroups` (sum `lcpGood/NeedsImprovement/Poor/Total`, same for `inp`, `cls`, `fcp`, `ttfb`; quantiles `largestContentfulPaintP75`, `interactionToNextPaintP75`, `cumulativeLayoutShiftP75`, `firstContentfulPaintP75`, `timeToFirstByteP75`, in microseconds except CLS; negative means not available). Data is sampled.
- **Build logs**: `GET /accounts/{id}/builds/builds/{build_uuid}/logs?cursor=` → `result { cursor, lines: [[timestamp, message]…], truncated }`; Workers CI Read or Write (the owner's grant has `workers-ci.write`).

### Budget guard: usage alerts, budgets and an optional pause — implemented, fixture-verified, the pause's stand-in run in the real runtime; not run live

At the owner's request after a $10,000 bill (session record item 42). Decisions: alert only by default, pause as an owner's choice; $0 beyond what Cloudflare includes by default; Billing Read asked for with the rest of the Cloudflare sign-in.

- **Files.** `worker/budget-usage.ts` (meters with Cloudflare's prices of 14 Oct 2026: included per month, $ per million, Workers Free's daily limit; `readUsage` = one GraphQL query per product, `date_geq`/`date_leq`, `limit: 10000`, a refused product recorded in `missing` with Cloudflare's message; `estimate(plan, rows, mine)` = the account's charge per meter beyond what's included, shared by the project's use, `accountCents` for the whole account, R2 charged on any plan, nothing else on Workers Free unless a day went past a free limit by more than 25% (sampling), which also proves Workers Paid; `detectPlan` from `GET /accounts/:id/subscriptions`). `worker/budgets.ts` (the guard: `scheduledBudgets`, `checkAccount`, `record`, `alerts`, `enforce`, `pauseNow`, `journal`, `failCheck`, `planFor`, `pausedForBudget`/`refuseWhilePaused`). `worker/budget-pause.ts` (`pausedModule(classes)`, `pauseWorker`, `resumeWorker`, `servingVersions`). `worker/budget-routes.ts` (routes). `src/Budget.tsx` (Overview → Billing → Budget guard) and a `BudgetBadge` in `src/App.tsx`. `cloudcel budget [check] [--json]` in `packages/cli/src/commands.ts`. Migration `0032_budgets.sql`: `project_budgets` (no row = $0, alert), `budget_checks` (one row per project: month, plan, estimate/projected/account cents, meters and missing JSON, the highest level alerted this month, `state` ok/over/paused, the pause's `paused_from_json`/`paused_schedules_json`/`paused_version`, `checked_at`/`read_at`/`error`/`error_alerted_at`), `account_plans` (24-hour cache; `source` subscriptions, usage, no-permission or error).
- **Meters.** Worker requests (`workersInvocationsAdaptive`), Durable Object requests and alarms (`durableObjectsInvocationsAdaptiveGroups`), rows read/written and running time from `activeTime` in µs × 0.125 GB (`durableObjectsPeriodicGroups`, two queries), D1 rows (`d1AnalyticsAdaptiveGroups`), KV by `actionType` (`kvOperationsAdaptiveGroups`), R2 Class A/B by `actionType` (`r2OperationsAdaptiveGroups`). Resources are matched to a project by the Worker's name, its Durable Object namespaces (`/workers/durable_objects/namespaces`, `script`), and its D1/KV/R2 bindings (`/workers/scripts/:name/settings`). Not estimated: stored data, Workers CPU time, key-value Durable Object storage operations.
- **Schedule.** Every minute, the production projects (latest `deployment_jobs` row with a `worker_tag`) whose reading is 300 s old, 25 at a time, oldest first, grouped by workspace and account (usage read once per account). The rate since the last reading gives `projected_cents` to the end of the month (UTC).
- **Alerts** (`notify`, events `budget.alert`, `budget.paused`, `budget.resumed` in `BUDGET_EVENTS`, delivered to the project's webhook whatever its chosen events): each new level (50, 80, 100; with a $0 budget any cost is 100) once a month; a projected overrun at most every 6 h below 100; a failed pause once; usage unreadable for an hour (or never read) at most once a day. Saving a budget resets the alerted level to where the estimate stands against the new budget.
- **Pause.** Only the Worker Cloudcel deployed (name and tag checked against `/workers/scripts`), journaled in `project_operations` as `budget.pause`/`budget.resume` (`user_id` `budget-guard` for the guard's own). Uploads a version (`POST …/versions`, `main_module: paused.js`, `compatibility_date: 2025-09-01`, `bindings: []`, `keep_bindings: ['secret_text','secret_key']`, message "Paused by Cloudcel: over budget") exporting each Durable Object class of the Worker (namespaces whose `script` is the Worker, plus its own bindings' `class_name`; names must be identifiers) with `fetch` → 503 and `alarm() {}`, and a default export with `fetch` → 503, `scheduled() {}`, `queue(batch)` → `retryAll({ delaySeconds: 43200 })`; deploys it at 100% (`?force=false`); `PUT …/schedules []`. Resume deploys the stored versions and schedules (refused while the estimate is over a budget that pauses). While paused, `refuseWhilePaused` stops redeploy, rollback, traffic, secrets (set and remove), schedules, direct-upload production deploys and the GitHub pipeline's start, and a local deploy's review lists it for production (previews allowed). Each reading of a paused project checks production still serves only the stand-in; if not, it pauses again (over a budget that pauses) or marks it running.
- **Routes.** `GET /api/projects/:id/budget` (any member) → `{ budget, deployed, webhook, plan, notEstimated, labels, check }`; `PUT …/budget` `{ monthlyCents 0..10,000,000, onExceed: alert|pause }` (owner); `POST …/budget/check` (developer; 3 a minute per workspace); `POST …/budget/pause` and `…/resume` `{ requestId, confirm: true }` (owner). API tokens can't `PUT`, pause or resume (`tokenForbidden`). `GET /api/projects` adds `budget: 'over' | 'paused' | null`.
- **Local Cloudcel.** `vite.config.ts` has a plugin that requests `/cdn-cgi/handler/scheduled?cron=*+*+*+*+*` every minute while `npm run dev` runs (`CLOUDCEL_LOCAL_CRON=off` turns it off; Playwright's server sets it). Before this, a local Cloudcel ran its scheduled work only through the dashboard's refresh calls.
- **Billing Read.** Added to the wanted Cloudflare scopes (optional). Without it the plan is unknown and estimated as Workers Paid, which still shows $0 on Workers Free unless R2 is used; a grant whose recorded scopes lack it isn't asked for the plan.
- **Evidence and limits:** see `docs/qa-report.md` (14 October, the budget guard). Nothing of it has run live: the GraphQL field names (some known only from third parties), the stand-in's upload and deployment, and the subscriptions' names are unverified against a real account. Layer 4 of the proposal (review warnings for risky patterns, notes for coding agents) is not built.

### Level with Vercel's CLI: apps with code and bindings from the computer, first deploy to production, everyday commands, more presets — implemented, fixture-verified with the real Wrangler; not run live

At the owner's request (13 Oct 2026: "Ensure we are on parity with vercel in terms of ease of use and features"). Vercel's CLI was read first (vercel.com/docs/cli, 69 commands, updated 7 Oct 2026; /docs/cli/deploy; /docs/frameworks/more-frameworks). CLI **0.4.0** (165 KB bundle), migrations **0030 and 0031**.

- **Why apps couldn't deploy from the computer before, and how they do now.** A Worker with code and bindings is deployed by Wrangler, and Wrangler needs Cloudflare's API with a credential. The workspace's OAuth connection is sealed in Cloudcel and must not leave it. So for one deploy Cloudcel opens a session and **Wrangler talks to Cloudflare through Cloudcel**: the CLI runs Wrangler with `CLOUDFLARE_API_BASE_URL=<cloudcel>/api/wrangler/<session>/client/v4`, `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN=<the session's own token>` and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`; Cloudcel checks each call and only then sends it on with the OAuth token. The command is the one the GitHub pipeline's trigger runs (`npx wrangler@4.148.0 deploy --no-autoconfig --x-provision --x-auto-create --name <worker>`; `versions upload` for a preview). The calls allowed are the ones Wrangler 4.148 makes, recorded first by running it against a logging stand-in: 13 endpoints for a new app, three more for a redeploy (`POST …/versions`, `POST …/deployments`, `PATCH …/script-settings`: Wrangler uses these instead of `PUT` once the Worker exists and no Durable Object migration is pending).
- **Review, the same one as GitHub's** (`worker/local-deploys.ts`; `repositoryPlan(files, framework, { local: true })` in `worker/repository-plan.ts`): the CLI sends the root files the GitHub inspection reads (`shared/local-deploy.ts` `localPlanFiles`; `package-lock.json` reduced to the two versions the review checks) to `POST /api/local-plan` (before a project exists; it also names the runtime, `detectFramework`) and `POST /api/projects/:id/local-plan` (with the project: Free-only, the Worker's name, whether the name is free, what Cloudflare would create now). Local mode drops what only Cloudflare's builds need (npm and a committed `package-lock.json`, no workspaces, paths inside the repository), builds with the project's own package manager, accepts build-only Wrangler settings (`minify`, `no_bundle`, `rules`, `define`, `build`, `upload_source_maps`, `limits`…) and a service binding of the Worker to itself, and names the settings it doesn't deploy. Without the framework's Cloudflare adapter installed, the folder's own configuration decides (a static export is a plain assets Worker). Refused from a computer: containers (Cloudflare's builds make their images), Python Workers with packages (pywrangler unverified here), `wrangler.toml` and programmatic configurations, routes, queues, existing resource IDs, bindings to other Workers.
- **Sessions** (`POST /api/projects/:id/local-deploys`; migrations 0030 and 0031 `local_deploys`): reviews again; 409 with the issues; 400 with `create` unless `confirmResources` when Cloudflare would create something; refuses a project that deploys from GitHub; a new Worker's name must be free in Cloudcel's records and in Cloudflare (an empty Worker counts as the project's own only when its earlier upload or deploy started it); the account needs a workers.dev subdomain; an existing Worker must still have its recorded tag. One deploy of a project at a time: another open session with a call in the last two minutes answers 409, an older one is closed and taken over. Journaled as `local.deploy` / `local.preview` (the operation's `updated_at` moves with Wrangler's calls). The session's token (32 random bytes, stored as SHA-256, one hour) is returned once. A session also ends when the sign-in that opened it ends (`local_deploys.opened_with`: an API token revoked or expired, a browser session signed out or run out). Opening a deploy closes the workspace's sessions whose hour is up, each **together with its operation** and its sealed tokens (closing only the session leaves the operation "running", and the unique index on running operations then refuses the project's next deploy); `unfinished()` says what such a deploy left: nothing, the resources it created, a version production doesn't serve yet, or a version that was deployed and never recorded. What a deploy may create is fixed when it opens (`create_json`). **A new app's first deploy is production whatever was asked**, because a preview needs the Worker to exist.
- **Wrangler's calls** (`worker/wrangler-api.ts`, mounted before the sign-in middleware; `/api/wrangler/*` skips Cloudcel's own authentication and is sized by `wranglerGate`, which reads nothing: a request other than `GET`/`HEAD` must declare its `Content-Length`, 411 otherwise and for a chunked body, 413 above 100 MB): the token must hash to the session's, the session be open and unexpired, the person still an owner or developer, the sign-in that opened it still good, the Cloudflare grant and account unchanged, deployments switched on; then 240 calls a minute per session and 600 per workspace (429), and a size for each part of a deploy (100 MB for a batch of files, which is streamed; 32 MB for code; 8 MB for the list of files; 64 KB otherwise; 2,000 parts in an upload). Allowed: reads of the session's own Worker (`services`, `workers`, `secrets` names, `script-settings`, `subdomain`, `schedules`); `deployments` reduced to which versions serve (no author, no notes); no reads of a version (its details carry variable values); `settings` with bindings reduced to name and type (no variable values, no resource IDs); the account's `workers/subdomain`; the scripts list filtered to this Worker; **empty lists** for D1, KV and R2 (Wrangler lists them only to offer a choice; with `--x-auto-create` it creates `<worker>-<binding>` without asking, so nothing of the account is listed); `GET /user…`, `/accounts`, `/memberships` answered locally (Wrangler asks only to explain an error). Writes: the assets upload session and file upload, where **Cloudflare's upload and completion tokens stay in Cloudcel, sealed**, and Wrangler gets the session's own token in their place (it presents it with the files; the metadata's `assets.jwt` is swapped back before the upload); creating D1/KV/R2 only for reviewed bindings under the exact name Wrangler gives them, sending Cloudflare only the name; the script upload and version upload after `uploadProblems` (below); and for production sessions only `POST …/deployments` (exactly the version this session uploaded, at 100%), `PATCH …/script-settings` (tags, and observability as on or off, sampling and persistence, never `destinations`; no Logpush, no tail consumers), `POST …/subdomain` and `PUT …/schedules` (five valid cron expressions at most). Anything else answers 403. Every answer is in Cloudflare's envelope, failures inside Cloudcel included (`wranglerApi.onError`), with a message starting `Cloudcel:`, which Wrangler prints. **A new app's Worker is touched by nothing before the session's own upload has created it** (version uploads, deployments, settings, the address and schedules answer 409 until then), that upload is refused if a Worker of that name appeared since the review, and for an app that exists an upload is refused when the Worker's tag is no longer the reviewed one. Cloudflare's upload tokens are taken out of an answer also when Cloudflare refuses or answers unreadably; an upload goes on with only Wrangler's own three flags.
- **What an upload may contain** (`worker/wrangler-policy.ts` `uploadProblems`, over the metadata fields Wrangler 4.148's `createWorkerUploadForm` writes): ES modules only; bindings limited to variables (`plain_text`, `json`), `assets`, `ai`, `browser`, `images`, `version_metadata`, `inherit`, Durable Objects of this Worker (no `script_name`), a service binding to this Worker itself, and D1/KV/R2 whose ID is one created for this app in this session (or by an earlier stopped session of the same project and Worker name, re-checked in Cloudflare); migrations that only create SQLite classes, rename or delete; no containers, exports, Logpush, tail consumers, placement other than Smart, or unknown fields.
- **Small departures from what Wrangler would do, each on purpose:** Workers Logs stay on unless the configuration mentions `observability` (Wrangler switches them off on every redeploy of a configuration without it, and `cloudcel logs` reads them); previews stay enabled with the workers.dev address unless `preview_urls` says otherwise; a resource a stopped deploy created is handed back instead of failing on its name (Wrangler never adopts by name, so that deploy could not have been repeated).
- **Recording:** the first production upload inserts the project's `deployment_jobs` row (adapter `direct-upload`, new column `local_adapter` = the reviewed adapter, `resources_json` = the reviewed resources, so Free-only's "uses R2" check sees them); the subdomain call sets `live_url`; `POST …/local-deploys/:id/finish` reads what the calls left, adds a `direct_uploads` history row (so `cloudcel ls`, `open --app` and the dashboard show the version and its preview address), closes the session (clearing the sealed tokens), completes the operation and notifies `upload.deployed`. A deploy that uploaded nothing answers 409 ("stopped before anything changed", or naming what it had created); one that uploaded a version without sending production to it says so and how to promote it; `failed: true` after an upload leaves the operation `needs_attention`. Because the Worker is recorded like an uploaded one, Manage (secrets, domains, versions, rollback, storage and database browsers, schedules, analytics, logs) works unchanged. `POST /projects/:id/uploads` refuses a folder of files for such a project (it would replace the code and drop the bindings).
- **CLI** (`packages/cli/src/cli.ts` `appIn`, `setUpAdapter`, `deployApp`; `src/app.ts`): a folder with a Wrangler configuration, or a server framework, is an app unless an output folder was named now or on an earlier deploy. It shows "Detected Next.js (OpenNext) (Build Command: …)" and the review's notes, lists issues before anything is created or built, asks before Cloudflare creates anything ("This deploy creates the D1 database … Go ahead? [Y/n]"; `--yes` in scripts), installs and builds, opens the session, runs Wrangler (**always `npx --yes wrangler@4.148.0`**, never the project's own: setup installs the newest Wrangler into a project, and the pass-through lets through only the calls seen from the verified version; `CI=true`, the person's own `CLOUDFLARE_API_KEY`/`CLOUDFLARE_EMAIL` cleared), and reports like any deploy.
- **A folder without a Wrangler configuration** (`setUpAdapter`; a server framework: Next.js, SvelteKit, Nuxt, Remix, React Router, Angular — `serverFrameworks` in `shared/site-presets.ts`): `cloudcel` / `cloudcel deploy --prod` creates the `wrangler.jsonc` itself. It asks once ("Add them now? Wrangler changes files in this folder…"; `--yes` in scripts, which otherwise are told to pass it), installs dependencies if there is no `node_modules` (Wrangler reads the framework's version from them), then: (1) **a dry run** of `npx --yes wrangler@4.148.0 setup --yes --dry-run` with `WRANGLER_OUTPUT_FILE_PATH` set to `<config dir>/wrangler-setup.json`; `setupFramework()` reads the framework Wrangler recognised from that report, and "static" (its answer for a framework it has no adapter for, such as Remix 2, which it would deploy as the `public` folder) or no report stops with nothing changed; (2) **the setup**, `npx --yes wrangler@4.148.0 setup --yes`, with `setupEnv()`: a `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` that is good for nothing and the person's Cloudflare variables removed, because OpenNext's installer otherwise opens Wrangler's own Cloudflare sign-in to make a cache bucket (with the dead token that step fails at once and setup goes on); (3) the `cf-typegen` script when setup added one (SvelteKit's new build script checks for the generated types); then the review, build and deploy as for any app. Setup writes more than the configuration: the adapter and `wrangler` in `package.json`, `deploy`/`preview`/`cf-typegen` scripts, framework files (`open-next.config.ts`, `workers/app.ts`, the adapter in `vite.config.ts`), `.gitignore`, `.dev.vars` for Next.js.
- **Next.js versions the adapter can't run** (`worker/repository-plan.ts`, the `nextjs` block; `next.config.{ts,mjs,js}` are among the files a folder's review reads): with OpenNext up to 1.20.10, Next.js 16.4.0 fails on every request and `cacheComponents: true` (the 16.4 template's default) makes a request to 16.3.8 hang; `partialPrefetching` needs `cacheComponents`. From a computer these are issues that stop the deploy and name the fix; for a repository they are warnings, as before. OpenNext releases after 1.20.10 are not stopped: **when one appears, run a fresh `create-next-app` through `cloudcel` and move or drop the bound.** The versions come from `package-lock.json` only (pnpm, Yarn and Bun projects aren't checked). `cloudcel build` builds an app the same way; `cloudcel init` now says `cd <folder> && cloudcel` for kits other than static and Python.
- **First deploy to production** (both paths): `--prod`/`--publish` or `--target production|preview` decide; otherwise a project without a production deployment goes to production and the output says so once. A site asked for as a preview on its first deploy is created unpublished, as before.
- **Everyday commands** (`packages/cli/src/commands.ts`, each over an existing API): `dev` (the project's `dev` or `start` script, else `wrangler dev`, with `--assets <folder>` for a folder of files); `env pull [file]` and `pull` (names of secrets and build variables into `.env.local`, or `.dev.vars` beside a Wrangler configuration; secret values are never readable, existing lines stay, mode 600); `env update`; `alias` (the same as `domains`; a version must be production); `crons ls|add|rm` (the schedules route; `run` explains); `rolling-release status|start|approve|complete|abort` (the traffic and rollback routes; two versions at a time); `metrics` (the analytics route); `curl` and `httpstat` (production or `--deployment <version>`; the path is validated before it reaches a shell); `api <path>` (`--method`, `--data`); `mcp [--write]` (client settings; `.mcp.json` without a token); `agent init` (a section in `AGENTS.md`, once); `upgrade`; `telemetry` (none is sent); `signup`; `switch`/`teams` (a sign-in works in one workspace: signs in again); `git ls`, `git disconnect` (explains). Shared code moved from `cli.ts` to `src/core.ts`.
- **Presets** (`shared/site-presets.ts`, used by the CLI and by GitHub deployments): Docusaurus (`build`), VitePress and VuePress (the folder and the script their own command names: `docs:build` running `vitepress build docs` deploys `docs/.vitepress/dist`; new `Site.script`), Eleventy (`_site`), Parcel, Gridsome (`dist`), Hexo (`public`), Stencil (`www`), Ember, UmiJS (`dist`, single-page), Preact CLI (`build`, single-page); checked before or after Vite so the more specific one wins. CLI only (`packages/cli/src/site.ts` `generator`): Hugo, Jekyll (with `bundle exec` when there is a Gemfile), Zola and MkDocs, by their own files, built with the tool on the computer.
- **Dashboard and docs:** a project Wrangler deploys shows "Deploys from your computer" with how to deploy it again and no drop zone (`src/Upload.tsx`; `Deployment.localAdapter`); the API & CLI page's commands; the CLI guide (new sections and a longer Vercel table with what is different and what isn't offered), Getting started, Deploy without Git, Deploy from GitHub, AI agents, Troubleshooting (eight new messages), the contract (§3b), `packages/cli/README.md`, `README.md`; the site's features page lists "Apps with code, from your terminal" as **Beta** and says it hasn't run against a live account.
- **Security review** (one subagent, read-only, on the pass-through, the policy, the sessions and the middleware change): no critical finding, one high (a body buffered before the token check), two medium (memory after the check; a new app's Worker reachable before the deploy created it), eight low (observability destinations, upload tokens in refused answers, the scope of reused resources, sign-ins that end, rate limits, slow comment stripping, readable versions, robustness). **All fixed**, each with a test and a mutation check; `docs/qa-report.md` has one line per finding. **Recorded, not changed:** the folder-upload and R2 routes still buffer a chunked body before sign-in ("What's next" item 7); unknown session IDs aren't rate limited; a new app's upload that Cloudflare accepts and Cloudcel then fails to record leaves a Worker without a record (the next deploy refuses its name, as with folder uploads); the instant between the existence check and the upload; a single call longer than two minutes lets another deploy of the project take over; the Worker's own `services` and `workers` records pass through unreduced (no variable values in them). **A fix that was wrong at first:** expired sessions were closed without their operations; `tests/local-deploys.test.ts` ("runs one deploy of a project at a time…") now opens a second deploy after an expired one, which is how it was found.
- **Evidence:** `tests/local-deploys.test.ts` 22 (two run **the real Wrangler 4.148.0** from `node_modules` through Cloudcel over HTTP against `tests/helpers/cloudflare-wrangler.ts`: a new app with files, D1, KV, variables, a cron, a Durable Object and Workers AI; a redeploy that inherits its bindings through `versions` and `deployments`; a preview; no Cloudflare token in anything Wrangler was sent, no listing of the account; then the token's limits, one Worker of one account with 13 refused paths, reviewed resources only, bindings by name and kind only, previews kept from production, the version check, logs and previews settings, never replacing a Worker, repeating a stopped deploy, the review's local mode, consent, Free-only, roles, one deploy at a time, a deploy that uploaded nothing or half; and for the security review: sizes from declared lengths, a new app's Worker untouched before its upload, a replaced Worker, sign-ins that end, call limits, Cloudflare's upload tokens kept whatever Cloudflare answers, expired deploys closed with their operations), `tests/cli.test.ts` 58 (+13: the app flow with stand-in tools, questions and scripts, the setup offer, deploys Wrangler didn't finish, `cloudcel build`, **the real Wrangler through the CLI's own command line**, first deploy to production and `--target`, and the new commands), `tests/cli-site.test.ts` 18 (+7: presets, generators, the files sent, which Wrangler runs, its command and environment, comment stripping in one pass), `tests/browser/upload.spec.ts` +1 (the dashboard panel, axe, two widths). **168 mutations, all caught** (27 of them for the setup of a folder without a Wrangler configuration): 87 before the security review, after six assertions were tightened (28 pass-through, 12 policy, 18 sessions and review, 29 CLI and presets), and 54 for the review's fixes; the 55 server-side ones were repeated against the final code (`scratchpad/mutate.py` is not in the repository: a mutation is a guard replaced by `false` or removed, the test file run, the file restored). In the VM's dev server (workerd behind Vite) a request's declared length reached the gate intact (small JSON, 5 MB, multipart) and a chunked one got the 411. Outside the suites: `wrangler dev --assets .` served a scratch folder in the VM; `wrangler setup --dry-run` recognised a Next.js `package.json` and stopped because the packages weren't installed (which is why the CLI installs first); the built `cloudcel.js` printed its version and help.
- **Run for real in the VM, without Cloudflare (13 Oct):** fresh `create-next-app` (Next.js 16.4.0), `create-react-router` (8.4) and `sv create` (SvelteKit 3.0.1) apps through Wrangler 4.148.0's setup, Cloudcel's review, their builds, the exact deploy command as a dry run and a local run answering `/`; then the CLI itself on fresh copies (`deploy --prod --yes`, real shell and Wrangler, fake Cloudflare): React Router and SvelteKit deployed, Next.js 16.4 was stopped by the review, and the set-up Next.js app on 16.3.8 without Cache Components answered 200 locally and deployed through the pass-through. SvelteKit's runs relaxed npm's engine check (it wants Node.js 22.17; the VM and the Mac have 22.13). Not run: Nuxt, Angular, Astro. The temporary tests that did this are not in the repository (they need the network and minutes): the method is in `docs/qa-report.md`.
- **Not covered yet, by design of this pass:** Astro with server output and no adapter (the CLI asks for an output folder), TanStack Start, SolidStart, Qwik and Analog (treated as Vite sites or unknown; Wrangler's setup knows them, so adding them to the server list plus one real run each is the work), and a Worker or Hono app with no Wrangler configuration at all (nothing writes one).
- **Live since 13 Oct (the owner's runs, see "Owner's live setup"):** React Router (setup, first deploy, a preview, a second production deploy, the version list), a Worker that creates a D1 database and a KV namespace through Cloudcel and has a Durable Object and a cron schedule (two deploys; the schedule ran), Next.js 16.3.8 on OpenNext 1.20.11, and SvelteKit 3 (two production deploys, the second with the types made again before the build). Every call Wrangler made for these went through the pass-through with the OAuth connection.
- **Still not live-verified:** every other Cloudflare answer on this path. In particular that the OAuth grant is accepted for all of Wrangler's calls (D1 and KV creation use `d1.write` and `workers-kv-storage.write`, which the grant has; `versions`, `deployments`, `script-settings` and `schedules` are untried with it); the response fields the pass-through reads (`tag` and `deployment_id` on a script upload — if `deployment_id` is missing, `finish` reads the version from the Worker's deployments; `jwt` on the assets session); streaming a large file batch through the dev server; `wrangler setup` on a real Next.js app; Wrangler's behaviour when the Worker was first made by Cloudcel's own upload API (it may ask to confirm, which `CI=true` answers).

### Setup the way Vercel's CLI does it: sign-in that comes back to the CLI's page, Cloudflare connected from the terminal, builds on the person's computer, `git connect` — implemented, fixture-verified; not run live

At the owner's request (12 Oct 2026, after the three manual steps for Cloudflare's GitHub app: "i think its still complicated, check vercel cli, the github login and everything setup is super simple and happens within the cli as well. We need the same"). What Vercel's CLI does, checked first in its docs and source (vercel.com/docs/cli/login, /deploy, /build, /git, /project-linking, the "New Vercel CLI login flow" changelog of 12 Sep 2025, and `packages/cli/src/commands/login/future.ts`, `util/link/setup-and-link.ts`, `util/git/connect-git-provider.ts` in vercel/vercel): `vercel login` is an OAuth device flow that opens the browser by itself and waits; the first `vercel` asks team, link-or-name, "Connect this Git repository…? (y/N)", shows "Detected Next.js (Build Command…, Output Directory…)" and "Customize settings? [y/N]", then deploys; `vercel git connect` reads the folder's `.git` remotes. Vercel is that simple because it builds on its own servers. Cloudcel has none: on Cloudflare the remote builder is Workers Builds, which is what needs Cloudflare's GitHub app and a build token. So the CLI now builds on the person's computer and uploads the result through the direct-upload path that already runs live, and a deploy needs neither. CLI **0.3.0** (119 KB bundle).

- **Sign-in that returns to where it started** (`worker/auth.ts`, migration **0029** `oauth_states.return_to`): `GET /api/auth/:provider/start?return=cli-login:<code>|cli-setup` keeps the target with the OAuth state; the callback redirects to `/app/?connection=success|declined#cli-login?code=<code>` or `#cli-setup` instead of Connections. `returnHash()` is an allow-list (the code must match the CLI's code alphabet exactly), applied when storing and again when redirecting, so only those two in-app addresses are ever produced. Before this, a browser that wasn't signed in lost the CLI's page: hosted mode showed the sign-in page and then Connections; a local Cloudcel showed the approval page in the local workspace, which can't approve (the cause of the owner's expired codes).
- **One page for the CLI** (`src/CliSetup.tsx`, `src/cli-setup.css`; `#cli-login?code=…` and new `#cli-setup`; rendered without the dashboard around it, also before sign-in): steps **Sign in with GitHub** (the button carries `return`; shows who and which workspace, with a workspace switcher for people in several, and "Not you? Sign out"), **Approve this computer** (code, host, time, "only approve a sign-in you started yourself"; `GET /api/cli/login/:code` now returns `mine`, so after coming back from Cloudflare the person who approved sees "signed in", not "code already used"; who decided is never returned), **Connect Cloudflare** (button with `return`; account picker when the connection reaches several; the workers.dev subdomain claim; "ask an owner" for developers), then "You’re all set. Go back to your terminal." A local Cloudcel without its GitHub App says to set it up on Connections first. `CliApproval` in `src/Developers.tsx` is gone.
- **`cloudcel login`** (`packages/cli/src/cli.ts`): opens the page, waits, then says who and where (`GET /api/me` now has `workspaceName`), and whether Cloudflare is connected. In a terminal it waits while Cloudflare is connected in the same tab (no second tab), asking `GET /api/connections` every 2 s for up to 10 minutes; without a terminal it prints the `#cli-setup` address; a developer is told to ask an owner; a Cloudcel with `NATIVE_BUILDS_ENABLED` off says so. `whoami` adds the workspace and the Cloudflare account. Tokens still can't change connections: connecting stays in the browser.
- **`cloudcel` builds, then deploys** (`packages/cli/src/site.ts`, `shared/site-presets.ts`): in a project's folder (it has a `package.json`), `detect()` uses the same presets as the GitHub pipeline — the preset code moved from `worker/repository-plan.ts` to `shared/site-presets.ts` (`detectSite`, no zod; the worker still parses `package.json` with zod and passes it in), so both read a repository the same way: Vite (with `build.outDir`/`root`), Create React App, Vue CLI, static Astro, Gatsby. The package manager comes from `packageManager` or the lockfile (npm's wins next to another's). First run in a terminal, like Vercel: "Set up and deploy “…”? [Y/n]", "Found project “x”. Link to it? [Y/n]" or "Link to existing project? [y/N]" / "Existing project name?", "Project name [x]:", "Detected Vite (Build Command: npm run build, Output Directory: dist)", "Customize settings? [y/N]" (install command, build command, output directory). `--yes` takes the defaults. Then: install only when `node_modules` is missing, build (`Io.run`: a shell in the folder, its output on standard error so `--json` stays clean), and upload the output folder with the preset's single-page-app setting. Settings are saved in `.cloudcel/project.json` (`settings`; validated when read back, so a hand-edited link is detected again rather than trusted), so later deploys ask nothing. `--prebuilt`, `--build-command`, `--output`; new `cloudcel build`. Any other project with a `build` script works once its output folder is named; without a `package.json`, `--build-command` plus `--output` builds anything (Hugo…). Server frameworks (Next.js, SvelteKit, Nuxt, Remix, React Router, Angular) and projects with a Wrangler configuration are sent to `cloudcel git connect`, unless `--output` names a static export. A folder named as an argument is still uploaded as it is, and a named folder that is a project's source is refused with `cloudcel --cwd <folder>`. In a Git repository the link is added to `.gitignore` (like `.vercel`). Cloudflare is checked before the build (`cloudflareReady`): in a terminal the CLI opens `#cli-setup` and waits. A project that deploys from GitHub is refused before building; one set up for GitHub that hasn't deployed yet (the owner's `my-app`) can start from the computer after a confirmation (it then stays an upload project, which the dashboard already allowed).
- **`cloudcel git connect`** (`packages/cli/src/git.ts` reads `.git/config`, also for worktrees; nothing runs git): the terminal front end of the existing GitHub pipeline, for push deploys. It takes the `origin` remote (or `--repo owner/name`; asks between several), creates a GitHub project (runtime from `/api/repositories/detect`) or uses the linked one, then checks each thing in turn and, for whatever is missing, explains, opens the page and waits (asking again every 5 s): Cloudcel's GitHub App can see the repository (its install page), Cloudflare's GitHub app can read it (the Cloudflare dashboard, with the documented clicks), a build token exists (Connections), the account has a workers.dev subdomain (`#cli-setup`). It prints the plan and the server's notes, stops on issues, asks once ("Create the Worker … and build every push to main of …?"), starts the pipeline with the single build token (or `--build-token`), and follows `POST /api/deployments/:id/refresh` step by step to "Production: …". `--skip-checks` starts even when Cloudflare's read check says no (that check's meaning is still unconfirmed live). `cloudcel` offers it when a new project is set up in a repository's folder: "Connect ada/site so Cloudflare builds and deploys every push? [y/N]" (default no, never with `--yes`). A project already deployed from the computer is told to give the repository a project of its own (`--name`): attaching Workers Builds to an existing upload Worker isn't built.

### Connect once: Cloudflare's GitHub app and the build token on Connections, the account chosen on connect — implemented, fixture-verified; the access check run live

At the user's request (9 Oct 2026: "After connecting github and cloudflare connecting it again separately from deployments page is bad UX … connect one and everything should work"; they chose "Inside Connect" over building with GitHub Actions). What Cloudflare allows, checked first: Workers Builds takes only user API tokens ("Currently, only user tokens are supported, with account-owned token support coming soon", developers.cloudflare.com/workers/ci-cd/builds/configuration/); Cloudflare's OAuth catalog (395 scopes, read with the owner's grant) has no scope that creates user tokens, only `account-api-tokens.write` (account tokens); the Builds API has no server-generated build token; and Workers Builds clones with Cloudflare's own GitHub app, "Cloudflare Workers and Pages" (`cloudflare-workers-and-pages`), a one-time install. So two one-time steps stay, and both now live on Connections, checked by Cloudcel; Deployments never asks for setup.

- **Account chosen on connect** (`worker/auth.ts` `chooseAccount`, after `saveGrant` in the Cloudflare callback): the account a previous grant of the workspace deployed to, when the new token still reaches it (`GET /accounts/{id}`), or else the only account the token sees (`GET /accounts?per_page=2`); with several, the owner picks on Connections, where the accounts now list without a "Load" click. Never fails a sign-in. Reconnecting (as after "Update Cloudflare sign-in") no longer loses the account.
- **Cloudflare's GitHub app** (`GET /api/connections/cloudflare/github[?repository=owner/name]`, `worker/connections.ts`): for each installation of Cloudcel's GitHub App (up to 5), its first repository stands for the account; Cloudcel asks the Builds API's `GET /accounts/{id}/builds/repos/github/{owner_id}/{repo_id}/config_autofill?branch=<default>` with the OAuth token: 200 → readable, 404 → not (Cloudflare answers code 12000 "Not found"; without `branch` it answers 400 "Invalid query parameter"), anything else is an error, never "not installed". Each account gets `https://github.com/apps/cloudflare-workers-and-pages/installations/new/permissions?target_id=<account id>`. Connections → GitHub card: "Cloudflare's GitHub app" with **Allow Cloudflare on GitHub** and **Check again**, re-checked on window focus. Deployments checks the inspected repository and, when Cloudflare can't read it, shows **Let Cloudflare read it on GitHub** (advisory: it doesn't block a deploy until the 404 meaning is confirmed live). The pipeline's connect-repository step now reports Cloudflare's reason (`errorDetail`) with that hint instead of "The provider rejected this operation."
- **Build token** (`src/BuildSetup.tsx`, Connections → Cloudflare card): **Create the token in Cloudflare** opens `https://dash.cloudflare.com/profile/api-tokens?permissionGroupKeys=…&accountId=*&zoneId=all&name=Cloudcel builds` (Cloudflare's documented template link; keys `workers_scripts`, `workers_kv_storage`, `workers_r2`, `d1`, `workers_routes` edit and `account_settings`, `user_details`, `memberships` read; the dashboard ignores unknown keys, and its permission menus can look empty until the summary, per cloudflare/cloudflare-docs#29825), then paste once (the route from the previous slice). Deployments' empty state is one line: add it on Connections, or check for build tokens.
- **12 Oct 2026 follow-up (live):** the owner added the build token through Connections, so `POST /accounts/{id}/builds/tokens` with the OAuth token works (listed as "Cloudcel builds"). After the owner's GitHub step, the access check still answered 404 for every repository. Cloudflare documents installing its app through the dashboard, which links it to the account ([GitHub integration](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/ci-cd/builds/git-integration/github-integration/); the Builds API reference says the same). So the GitHub card's button is now **Connect GitHub in Cloudflare**, opening `https://dash.cloudflare.com/<account>/workers-and-pages` with those steps, and each account gets **Repository access on GitHub** (`github.com/settings/installations`, or `github.com/organizations/<org>/settings/installations`) for adding repositories later. The response is now `{connectUrl, accounts: [{login, repository, readable, repositoriesUrl}]}`. Deployments' warning carries both links. Not yet known: whether the owner's GitHub-only install happened at all, and whether the dashboard route turns the check to 200 (expected). 6 more mutations, all caught.
- **Deploying from GitHub** box on Connections (replaces the static "Before your first automatic deployment"): GitHub, Cloudflare with an account, Cloudflare reads your repositories, build token; "N steps left" or "Ready".
- **Evidence:** `tests/security.test.ts` (+4: the only account, several left to the owner, the earlier account kept, the fallback when it's out of reach), `tests/deployments.test.ts` (+6: per-account access with the exact Builds API path, default branch and OAuth token; one named repository and its validation; an account without repositories; an outage reported as an error; GitHub and an account required; the refused repository connection's reason), `tests/browser/connect.spec.ts` (4 journeys: the whole walk-through with the focus re-check, the pre-filled link's permissions and the token trimmed, axe at 1440 and 375 px; a refused token's message; several accounts listed unasked and changed; Deployments pointing to Connections with the repository check), the Deployments build-token journey removed. 24 mutations, all caught. **Live (read-only, 9 Oct 2026, through Cloudcel's API with the owner's CLI sign-in):** `GET /api/connections/cloudflare/github` answered `Xayaan` → `Xayaan/dancing-places-app` not readable, and `?repository=Xayaan/Royal-Protocol-Landing` not readable (Cloudflare's app isn't installed yet); build tokens `[]`. **Not live-verified:** that `config_autofill` answers 200 once the app is installed, the pre-filled token page, and the account choice on a real connect.

### Build tokens, sites with two lockfiles, Workers Logs for folder deploys — implemented; logs and build-token registration verified live, the GitHub deploy still pending

At the user's request (9 Oct 2026: `my-app`, their Gatsby repository `Xayaan/Royal-Protocol-Landing`, showed "Keep one package manager's lockfile; this repository has package-lock.json and yarn.lock" and "This account has no build tokens yet").

- **Two lockfiles** (`worker/repository-plan.ts` `sitePlan()`): with `package-lock.json` next to another manager's lockfile, a site is no longer refused. The plan warns, `site.install = 'npm'` (kept in `site_json`), the build command becomes `npm install --no-audit --no-fund && npm run build`, and the `start_build` step (and the branch-preview trigger) sets the trigger's build variable `SKIP_DEPENDENCY_INSTALL=1` (`skipInstall`, an idempotent `PATCH /builds/triggers/{id}/environment_variables`) before asking Cloudflare to build, so Workers Builds' own install doesn't choose a manager. `npm install` rather than `npm ci`: a second lockfile is often stale, and `npm ci` refuses a lockfile out of step with `package.json`. Other mixes (pnpm with Yarn…) stay an issue.
- **Build tokens** (`worker/connections.ts`, `POST /api/connections/cloudflare/build-tokens` `{token}`; owners only; needs `NATIVE_BUILDS_ENABLED` and a selected account): Workers Builds takes only user API tokens and OAuth can't create one, so the owner pastes one made from Cloudflare's _Edit Cloudflare Workers_ template. Cloudcel checks it with `GET /user/tokens/verify` (with the pasted token: its ID and status; only `active` passes), registers it with `POST /accounts/{id}/builds/tokens` `{build_token_name: 'Cloudcel builds', build_token_secret, cloudflare_token_id}` through the owner's OAuth connection (Workers CI Write), and stores nothing. A refusal (400/401/403) says to paste a user API token; a rate limit or an outage is reported as such; a malformed token (under 20 or over 200 characters, or anything but letters, digits, `_`, `.` and `-` once trimmed) is refused before any call with "Paste the API token exactly as Cloudflare showed it." The form first lived on Deployments; it moved to Connections the same day (newest slice).
- **Workers Logs for folder deploys** (`worker/uploads.ts`): found live, `cloudcel logs` was empty for `hello-cloudcel`. Cloudflare reports `observability: null` for a Worker made through its upload API (its dashboard turns logs on for every Worker it creates), and **a script upload (`PUT /workers/scripts/{name}`) replaces the setting: one without `observability` turned logs off again** (live: a production deploy after logs were enabled reset them; previews (`POST …/versions`), `promote` (`POST …/deployments`) and `env add` (secrets) left them). Now the first deploy's upload carries `observability: {enabled: true}`, and later production uploads carry the Worker's own setting from `GET …/script-settings` (left out when null, so logs turned off stay off); a failed read stops the deploy before uploading; previews send none. Reads of `script-settings` can lag a few seconds behind a change. Docs: Logs and analytics (with Workers Logs' free and paid allowances), the CLI guide.
- **Evidence:** `tests/deployments.test.ts` (+8: two lockfiles through to the trigger's build variable, for production and previews, and 6 for build tokens: registered through the OAuth connection with no copy anywhere in the database, refused and inactive tokens, malformed tokens before any call, a rate limit and an outage, cloud operations off and no account, owners only), `tests/uploads.test.ts` (+4 for Workers Logs; the first upload's exact metadata), `tests/browser/new-project.spec.ts` (+1: the form with axe, the password field, the button disabled while empty, a refused token's message in the form, the trimmed token sent, the new token chosen). Mutations: 9 for two lockfiles, 18 for the token route and form plus 4 after its validation message changed, and 6 for Workers Logs, all caught. **Live (9 Oct 2026, `hello-cloudcel`):** after the fix, a production deploy kept Workers Logs on (`script-settings` read 25 s later) and requests made after it appeared in `cloudcel logs` within 15 s. **Not live-verified:** registering a build token through the API and a Workers Build using it (a community report says API-registered tokens can fail at the build's environment setup; fallback: create the build token in Cloudflare's dashboard, then "check for build tokens"); `SKIP_DEPENDENCY_INSTALL` in a real build; `{enabled: true}` on a brand-new Worker (the live production deploy sent the full setting through the same field); the owner's Gatsby build on Workers Builds.
- **Owner's next step:** see the newest slice: both one-time steps are on Connections.

### First live CLI run: Workers Editor and the upload session's empty Worker found and fixed — verified live on 9 Oct 2026

On 8 Oct 2026 the owner signed the CLI in themselves (`cloudcel login`, approved in their GitHub workspace; token in `~/.config/cloudcel/config.json`, mode 600). Live results with their Cloudflare account (`Takumisolutionsmv@gmail.com's Account`, workers.dev subdomain `takumisolutionsmv`): `whoami`, `project ls`, reading the subdomain, and **listing build tokens through the Builds API with the OAuth token** worked (the account has **no build tokens** yet: GitHub deploys need one created in Cloudflare's dashboard); the Cloudflare access token **refreshed automatically** at 20:15 (expiry moved to 21:15). `cloudcel deploy` created the project `hello-cloudcel` (no Worker yet), then Cloudflare refused `POST /accounts/:id/workers/scripts/hello-cloudcel/assets-upload-session` with 403 "No access to the specified resource."

- **Cause:** Cloudflare's OAuth scope list (read with the owner's grant; 392 scopes) names `workers-scripts.edit` **Workers Editor** (the Editor role: updates and deploys existing Workers, can't create or delete them — developers.cloudflare.com/workers/authorization/workers/) and `workers-scripts.write` **Workers Scripts Write**. `inspectToken` matched candidates ["Workers Scripts Write", "Workers Scripts Edit"] against names _and IDs_ in catalog order, so the Editor scope (listed first) won.
- **Fix:** `wantedScopes` asks for `Workers Scripts Write`/`workers-scripts.write` only; candidates are tried in their own order. `GET /api/setup` returns `cloudflare.outdated` (`["Workers Scripts Write"]` when the client's scopes lack it); `POST /api/setup/cloudflare/update` `{token}` (local setup only, temporary token with OAuth Client Write) PATCHes `/accounts/:id/oauth_clients/:client` with the corrected scopes (keeping `offline_access`), or creates a new client when Cloudflare refuses (404/502), then marks every active Cloudflare grant `reconnect_required`. Connections shows **Update Cloudflare sign-in** (`CloudflareUpdate` in `src/ProviderSetup.tsx`; for `.dev.vars` setups, a hint to add `workers-scripts.write`). Migration **0028** `provider_grants.scopes` records the scopes Cloudflare reports at sign-in and refresh (`scope` in the token response). `createsWorkers()`/`cannotCreateWorkers` (`worker/auth.ts`): a new app's first upload (`POST /projects/:id/uploads`) and a GitHub deployment (`POST /projects/:id/deployments`) answer 409 with what to do, before calling Cloudflare, when the connection (or, for older grants, its client) has Workers Editor without Workers Scripts Write. Provider 403s now carry the provider's structured reason (sanitized, never a raw body) and the server log names the refused request without its query.
- **Evidence:** `tests/provider-setup.test.ts` +4 (real catalog with both scopes, no Editor fallback, candidate order, update in place and the fallback), `tests/security.test.ts` +1 (scopes recorded at the Cloudflare callback), `tests/uploads.test.ts` +1, `tests/deployments.test.ts` +2 (deploy pre-check; a 403's reason and log line), `tests/browser/connections-setup.spec.ts` +1 (update from Connections). 13 mutations, all caught. Docs: provider-setup, troubleshooting, self-hosting.
- **Live, 9 Oct 2026, after the owner's update:** **Update Cloudflare sign-in** changed the existing client's scopes in place (PATCH, no new client); the new grant records `workers-scripts.write` and the old one became `reconnect_required`. The next `cloudcel deploy` stopped with "A Worker named hello-cloudcel already exists": the refused attempt's `assets-upload-session` had left an **empty Worker** (no deployment, modules or assets; read-only check). Fix: `workerState()` (`worker/uploads.ts`) tells `none`, `empty` and `existing` apart; an empty Worker is accepted only when this project's own direct upload started it (a `direct_uploads` row for the project and name), and the deploy step refuses only `existing` (`tests/uploads.test.ts` +2, the empty Worker modelled in `tests/helpers/cloudflare-uploads.ts`; 5 mutations, all caught). Then, with `examples/hello-cloudcel`: a preview deploy (its version address served the page and `/api/hello`; production 404 while unpublished), `deploy --prod` (https://hello-cloudcel.takumisolutionsmv.workers.dev/ 200; `/api/hello` gave Cloudflare error 1042 once right after, then 200), `ls`, `inspect`, `project inspect`, `env add GREETING` and `env ls` (the app's greeting changed), a preview that kept the secret, `promote`, `rollback` (to the secret's version), `logs` (empty: Workers Logs was off, fixed in the newest slice), `domains ls` and `activity`. Not run live: `redeploy` (GitHub projects), `domains add`, `publish`/`unpublish`, `remove`.

### Sites deploy from GitHub without a Wrangler configuration — implemented, fixture-verified

At the user's request (8 Oct 2026: a plain Vite/React repository showing "Repository needs configuration" "should be done automatically like vercel does"). New adapter **`static-assets`** (`worker/repository-plan.ts`): when a repository has no `wrangler.json(c)`/TOML/programmatic config, the chosen runtime isn't python/docker/nextjs/sveltekit, and no server framework is a dependency (next, @sveltejs/kit, nuxt, @remix-run/dev, @react-router/dev, @angular/core), `staticSite()` recognises a preset like Vercel's: **Vite** (`dist`, or `build.outDir` joined to `root` when they're plain strings in `vite.config.*`; computed values or paths outside the repository are issues; single-page app), **Create React App** (`build`, SPA), **Vue CLI** (`dist`, SPA), **Astro without a server adapter or server/hybrid output** (`dist`, 404 page), **Gatsby** (`public`, 404 page), **plain HTML** (`index.html` at the root and no build script; the repository root with an `.assetsignore` for `.git`, `.github`, `node_modules`, `.wrangler`, `wrangler.jsonc`, `.assetsignore`). `sitePlan()`: build `npm run build` (a build script is required, except plain HTML), no resources, any one of npm/pnpm/Yarn/Bun lockfiles (Bun's two names count once; none → a warning, more than one manager → an issue; monorepos refused), warnings that say what is served. The trigger's deploy command writes the configuration, then deploys: `printf '%s' '{"name":…,"compatibility_date":"2026-10-07","assets":{"directory":…,"not_found_handling":"single-page-application"|"404-page"},"preview_urls":true,"previews":{}}' > wrangler.jsonc && [printf '%s\n' … >> .assetsignore &&] npx wrangler@4.148.0 deploy --no-autoconfig --name <worker>` (values validated: Worker name and a safe relative folder). Branch previews use the same written configuration with `wrangler preview` (the empty `previews` block is what Cloudflare needs, so nothing is committed; the previews block check is skipped for this adapter). Migration **0027** `deployment_jobs.site_json` stores the reviewed site for previews. Inspection now also reads `index.html` (metadata), `vite.config.{ts,js,mjs,mts,cjs}` and `astro.config.{mjs,ts,js}` at the pinned commit; `/api/repositories/detect` reads `index.html` and calls a repository of plain HTML files `static`. Deployments shows "Adapter: Static site (Vite), no Wrangler configuration needed" and "Builds with npm run build, then serves dist/ as a single-page app." Docs: Deploy from GitHub (a presets table) and Troubleshooting; the docs renderer now supports `_italic_` (whole words only, never inside code). Wrangler's own `--autoconfig` (default on in 4.148) stays off: it changes the project in ways nobody reviews.

- **Evidence:** `tests/imports-plan.test.ts` (+15: presets, Vite outDir/root, wrong runtime, server frameworks, lockfiles, monorepos, unknown builds, plain HTML detection), `tests/deploy-command.test.ts` (+5: the exact command run in a shell with the installed Wrangler 4.148 and `--dry-run` for a Vite `dist` and a plain HTML root, the `.assetsignore`, refusals, and `wrangler preview` with the written configuration against a local stand-in API, whose Preview carries the assets and SPA setting), `tests/deployments.test.ts` (+3: inspection → plan → job with `site_json` → trigger commands, branch previews with nothing to commit, plain-HTML detection), a browser journey for the Deployments plan. `npm run verify:frameworks` (full run, 8 Oct 2026): 9 adapter fixtures passed, including new `vite-react` (npm install, `vite build`, the exact command as a dry run, then `wrangler dev` answering a client-side route with the app's page) and `html` (the root served, `/.git/config` and `/wrangler.jsonc` 404); the `sveltekit-autoconfig` probe fails as before. 16 mutations, all caught (one dead check removed). **Not live-verified:** that Workers Builds installs pnpm/Yarn/Bun lockfiles (Cloudflare's docs list the managers but not how they're chosen), and the first real zero-config build.

### Vercel-style CLI (0.2.0), preview deploys, docs site, demo app — implemented; run live on 9 Oct 2026

At the user's request (8 Oct 2026: "try the cli commands… we should have same [as Vercel] so its easier for others… add it to production and get it running like vercel does… robust docs folder and also website section with docs with full guides for cli and everything"):

- **CLI** (`packages/cli/src/cli.ts`, 0.2.0; `docs/guides/cli.md`, `packages/cli/README.md`): Vercel's command names and behavior. `cloudcel [deploy] [folder]` (no command, or an existing folder, deploys) uploads a **preview** by default and `--prod` deploys to production and publishes (`--publish` kept as an alias); the first deploy in a folder asks to set up a project (or `--yes`/`--create`, named after the folder or `--name`) and links it. `promote <version|url>` (100% traffic via `/rollback`, then `/publish` for an unpublished upload app; refuses what's already production), `rollback [version|url]` (default: the most recent earlier version with `production: true`, so previews are skipped; `--to` kept), `redeploy` (GitHub projects; upload projects told to deploy again), `list|ls [project]` (age, short ID, Production/Preview/Earlier production, preview URL, message), `inspect <version|url>` (ID prefix ≥4 hex or a preview URL; ambiguous prefixes refused), `logs [--since 30m|2h|1d] [--errors] [--follow]` (oldest first, deduped while following), `open [--app]` (the dashboard project link by default), `publish|unpublish`, `init [kit] [folder] [--database]` (creates a kit project, downloads `/export`, unzips into a new folder that must be empty, links it; static kit → `cloudcel deploy public --prod`), `link [--project] [--yes]`, `project ls|add|rm|inspect` (`projects`, `create` kept), `remove|rm <name>` (says the Worker and data stay in Cloudflare; unlinks the folder), `env ls|add|rm` (Worker secrets; value from a hidden prompt or stdin, never echoed; `--build [--preview] [--plain]` for build variables; `env pull` explains why not), `domains ls|add|rm` (most specific zone that covers the hostname), `activity`, `help [command]`, `--version`, global `--cwd`, `--token`, `--url`, `-y`, `--json`. Changes ask in a terminal and need `--yes` in scripts. Dashboard API & CLI page shows install/CI commands with `--prod` and a Cloudcel ↔ Vercel command table. Not offered: `dev`, `env pull`, `teams/switch`, Vercel-only products (documented why).
- **Backend:** `POST /projects/:id/uploads/:uploadId/deploy` takes `preview: true`: for an existing Worker it uploads a version **without deploying** (`POST …/workers/scripts/:name/versions?bindings_inherit=strict`, the same multipart form as the script upload, as Wrangler 4.148's `versions upload` sends; journaled as `upload.preview`; the upload row records no URL and `published=0`; production's `live_url` untouched); for a new app it creates it unpublished as before. `preview` with `publish` → 400. Responses now always include `preview` and `productionUrl`. **Fix:** uploads with a `_worker.js` now send `keep_bindings: ["secret_text","secret_key"]` (Wrangler does), so secrets set in Manage or by sign-in & realtime survive a new upload. `GET /projects/:id/versions` adds `production` (the version appears in Cloudflare's recent deployments list). `/export` and `/config` refuse GitHub-source projects (409).
- **Dashboard:** `#projects/<id>[/<tab>]` opens a project (on load and on hash change; unknown → "That project isn’t in this workspace"; the address then returns to `#projects`); the CLI's output links there. The CLI sign-in page names the workspace the token will act in and warns when it's the local workspace. Manage → Domains loads its domains and zones with the tab (errors stay in the Custom domains panel; "Refresh domains"; "Load more zones" only when there are more), and Builds & versions loads Git builds with the tab ("Refresh builds").
- **Docs:** 15 guides in `docs/guides/` (getting started, CLI, deploy from GitHub, deploy without Git, starter kits, previews and rollbacks, environment variables, domains, databases and storage, logs and analytics, teams and tokens, AI agents, Free-only and billing, self-hosting, troubleshooting) plus `docs/README.md`. Published at `/docs/` and `/docs/<slug>/` by `site/markdown.ts` (a dependency-free renderer for the subset the guides use: headings with unique anchors, paragraphs, lists, `>` notes, fenced code in focusable regions, tables in labelled scroll regions, `guide.md#anchor` links mapped to `/docs/…`), `site/docs.ts` (order and sections) and `site/render.ts` (index, sidebar, "On this page", previous/next); main nav and footer link to Docs. `site/markup.ts` now holds `escape`/`inline` (also `#anchor` links). `public/docs` is generated and in `.prettierignore`.
- **Demo app:** `examples/hello-cloudcel/` (static page + `public/_worker.js` answering `/api/hello` with `env.GREETING`), to try the CLI. `.cloudcel/` (folder links) is git-ignored.
- **Evidence:** `tests/cli.test.ts` 15 (the CLI end to end against the real API and fake Cloudflare: previews vs `--prod`, promote of an unpublished app, rollback past previews, ls/inspect/ambiguous prefixes, link/project/rm/activity, secrets via stdin and hidden prompt, domains with zone choice, logs with since/errors/follow, init + deploy with `--cwd`, help/version/unknown/no-command); `tests/uploads.test.ts` +5; `tests/projects.test.ts` +1; `tests/docs.test.ts` 5; browser: deep links, developers page, docs (16 pages × 2 widths, axe), Domains/Builds loading, a Domains error kept in its panel. Mutation checks: 18 CLI, 7 backend, 3 deep-link and 4 Manage guards — all caught.
- **Live run (9 Oct 2026, done):** after two expired sign-in codes (`563Q-P85D`, `D4CV-RVDS`) the owner signed the CLI in themselves; the run then found the Workers Editor scope (fixed 8–9 Oct), the upload session's empty Worker (fixed) and Workers Logs being off (fixed). Previews, `--prod`, `ls`, `inspect`, `project inspect`, `env add/ls`, `promote`, `rollback`, `logs`, `domains ls` and `activity` all ran against the owner's account; see "First live CLI run" and "Owner's live setup". Version uploads with assets through the OAuth grant, preview URLs on real workers.dev and the hourly token refresh are all confirmed. Not run live: `redeploy`, `domains add/rm`, `publish`/`unpublish`, `remove`, `init`, `open --app`.

### New project: a starter kit or a GitHub repository; Deployments loads by itself — implemented, fixture-verified

At the user's request (8 Oct 2026: "choose starter kit or either github repo, both should not be available at once" and "the deployments part need me manually loading github installation and build tokens and select the repo again"):

- **Create dialog** (`src/App.tsx` `CreateProject`, `src/RepositoryPicker.tsx`): a "Start from" radio pair. _Starter kit_ shows the name, kit runtimes (plus research runtimes saved as plans), D1/R2 options and Free-only; _GitHub repository_ shows only the repository picker, name, runtime and Free-only. The picker loads the GitHub App's installations as soon as it shows, chooses the first account, lists its repositories (push/admin only, paged with "Load more"), links to the App's install page ("Choose which repositories Cloudcel can see"), and explains an account with no installations. Choosing a repository fills the name (`projectNameFrom`, unless the person typed one) and detects the runtime (`GET /api/repositories/detect?repository=owner/name` → `detectFramework` in `worker/repository-plan.ts`: Wrangler `containers` → docker, a `.py` entry or a lone `pyproject.toml` → python, then dependencies next → astro → @sveltejs/kit → react → vue → hono, assets without `main` → static, else worker; null when nothing is recognisable; files read at the default branch, a file too large to read is skipped; owners/developers only; needs push access). A slower answer for an earlier choice never overrides a later one; Create waits for detection. Without GitHub connected the GitHub path shows "Connect GitHub to choose a repository" and an "Open Connections" button. Docker (import-only) opens on the GitHub path.
- **Project source** (migration **0026** `projects.source`, `kit` | `github`, checked by `projectSchema`): without `source`, a repository means GitHub (backward compatible for the MCP server and API clients); `github` needs a repository and a runtime with an import adapter; `kit` refuses a repository. Existing projects that name a repository were backfilled to `github`. A GitHub project never offers kit export or the kit Configuration tab; its status badge reads "From GitHub". A project created from GitHub opens on Deployments; opened from the list later, it starts on Overview.
- **Deployments tab** (`src/Integrations.tsx`): before the first deployment, the saved repository is inspected automatically (once per visit); "Change repository" opens the picker; choosing a repository inspects it at once; "Check again" re-inspects after fixes; a repository Cloudcel can't read shows the error and the picker. Build tokens load by themselves when an account is selected, and a single token is chosen automatically; an empty list explains where tokens come from with a "check for build tokens" button; a failed listing says so with "Try again". The "Load GitHub installations", "Inspect and link repository" and "Load Cloudflare build tokens" buttons are gone.
