### Sign-in setup from the Connections page (local) — implemented, fixture-verified

At the user's request ("it should be easier to connect, done in the Connections page"), a local Cloudcel now creates its own GitHub App and Cloudflare OAuth client from Connections instead of hand registration and `.dev.vars` editing (`worker/provider-apps.ts`, `src/ProviderSetup.tsx`, migration 0025 `provider_apps`). `providerApp(env, provider)` resolves the app: `.dev.vars`/environment first (all three values), else the stored row (secret sealed with `TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, context `provider-app:<provider>`); without the key nothing counts as set up. Sign-in start, callback and token refresh, `/api/auth/session` and `/api/connections` (install link from the stored slug) all use it, so setup works without a restart. Routes, **local mode only** (loopback with `AUTH_MODE` not `github`; 404 when hosted; API tokens refused): `GET /api/setup`; `POST /api/setup/github` (manifest + state cookie; optional organization) → GitHub → `GET /api/setup/github/created` (exempt from the cross-site check like the OAuth callbacks; state bound to the browser, single use, one hour; conversion failures go back to Connections with a notice; the private key and webhook secret aren't stored); `POST /api/setup/cloudflare/inspect` and `/create` (temporary token with OAuth Client Write: accounts, paged scope list matched by name or ID, Account Read required, `offline_access` retried without only when Cloudflare names it; the token is never stored); `PUT /api/setup/cloudflare` (a client made by hand); `DELETE /api/setup/:provider`. The dashboard's CSP `form-action` now allows only GitHub's two app-creation paths besides `self`. `npm run dev` creates `.dev.vars` with a generated key if it's missing (`scripts/dev-env.mjs`); `npm run setup:check` treats unset providers as "set up from Connections" in local mode and still requires them when hosted. Connecting Cloudflare still requires GitHub sign-in first (the page says so). Evidence: 24 tests in `tests/provider-setup.test.ts`, 4 browser journeys (`tests/browser/connections-setup.spec.ts`: the manifest form posted to GitHub, token flow with account choice, hand-made client, missing key; axe at two widths), the real local page posting the right manifest (form submission intercepted), 23 server and 7 UI guards mutation-checked. **Live (8 Oct 2026):** the owner set up and connected both from Connections on this machine: a GitHub App `Cloudcel an44r8` and a Cloudflare OAuth client `Cloudcel local` now exist in their accounts; Cloudflare accepted the loopback redirect, offered all 13 wanted scopes (IDs like `account-settings.read`, `workers-scripts.edit`, `workers-r2.write`, `access-acct.write`, now also listed as match candidates with a regression test) and `offline_access`; both OAuth sign-ins completed (grants active, with expiry). Not yet live: API calls with the Cloudflare token (account selection), a refresh, deployments.

### Local testing round — two kit defects found and fixed

At the user's request ("let's test this locally first") Cloudcel ran locally (`npm run dev`) and the four new kits were exported from it and run in Wrangler's local runtime (offline environment, no saved login). Astro and SvelteKit worked. Two defects that dry runs can't see:

- **Next.js:** with Next.js 16.4.0 and @opennextjs/cloudflare 1.20.9 (the newest, whose peer range includes 16.4) the OpenNext build succeeds but every request fails with `Unexpected loadManifest(/.next/server/preview-props.json) call!`; Next.js 16.3.8 works. The kit and the `nextjs` fixture now use 16.3.8, and the import plan warns when `package-lock.json` locks Next.js ≥ 16.4.0 with OpenNext ≤ 1.20.9.
- **Python:** from compatibility date **2026-04-21** the runtime takes the `workers` module from the workers-py package (`enable_python_external_sdk`, read from the workerd binary; plain Wrangler starts the kit at 2026-04-20 and fails at 2026-04-21 with "No module named 'workers'", and `disable_python_external_sdk` restores the old behaviour). Cloudflare's guide now uses pywrangler for every Python Worker. The kit now has `pyproject.toml` (workers-py and workers-runtime-sdk as dev dependencies), `wrangler.jsonc` (pywrangler 1.17.7 doesn't read `wrangler.json`), uv-based scripts and README steps; the import plan sends Python Workers that declare workers-py to pywrangler (adapter `python-packages`), requires `wrangler.jsonc` there, and refuses the plain-Wrangler path from 2026-04-21 unless `disable_python_external_sdk` is set. The `python` fixture uses 2026-04-20.
- **Verification now runs the apps:** `verify:kits` serves each kit after its dry run (`wrangler dev`, or `pywrangler dev` after `uv lock`) and requires `/` and `/api/health` to answer — shown to catch the Next.js defect by putting 16.4.0 back — and `verify:frameworks` does the same for the five non-container fixtures (FastAPI answered through pywrangler). The two container fixtures built identical images, so one's cleanup could remove the other's image mid-run; they now differ.
- Evidence: 5 new plan tests and updated kit tests (478 unit tests in total), 13 guards mutation-checked; all nine kits and seven adapter fixtures pass with local runs.

### Starter kits for Astro, SvelteKit, Next.js and Python — implemented, locally verified

The four framework runtimes that were import-only now have starter kits (`worker/kits.ts`, catalog `stage: 'kit'`), each in the exact shape its import adapter expects, so a kit pushed to GitHub deploys through the same native pipeline. Configurations were captured from Wrangler 4.148's own `wrangler setup` (Astro: `astro add cloudflare`; Next.js: the OpenNext migration) run offline in a scratch folder, and SvelteKit 3's committed configuration from the verified fixture; package versions are the ones `verify:frameworks` built (Astro 7.3.6 + @astrojs/cloudflare 14.3.4; SvelteKit 3.0.1 + adapter-cloudflare 8.0.0, svelte 5.57.2, Vite 8.3.3; Next.js 16.3.8 + @opennextjs/cloudflare 1.20.9, after 16.4.0 failed at request time; Wrangler 4.148.0). Astro: `main: "@astrojs/cloudflare/entrypoints/server"`, `assets ./dist`, server output; the adapter's `SESSION` KV and `IMAGES` bindings are explained in the README. SvelteKit: adapter in the Vite plugin, `main .svelte-kit/cloudflare/_worker.js`. Next.js: `main .open-next/worker.js`, `WORKER_SELF_REFERENCE` bound to the app's own name (the adapter keeps that name), `IMAGES`, `open-next.config.ts` without an incremental cache (README says ISR isn't persisted), `predeploy` guard before `opennextjs-cloudflare deploy`. Python: a standard-library Worker (`src/entry.py`, `python_workers`) in `wrangler.jsonc`, with a `pyproject.toml` declaring workers-py and workers-runtime-sdk, run and deployed with pywrangler (`uv lock` once), and a `package.json` that pins Wrangler (see the local testing round). Every kit has a home page, `/api/health`, optional draft D1 (`DB`) and R2 (`FILES`) bindings (now allowed for these frameworks), the placeholder-ID guard, the deploy/backup workflows and a framework section in its README. `npm run verify:kits` (extended: it creates projects with Free-only off when R2 is requested, reads every file Cloudcel inspects, and runs each plan's own build command — `npx opennextjs-cloudflare build` for Next.js, none for Python) passed all nine kits: install, build, native plan, deploy dry run with `DB`/`FILES`, and the guard. Evidence also: kit tests in `tests/projects.test.ts`, the templates journey (Docker is now the import-only example), 18 generator guards mutation-checked. Dashboard and site copy now say nine starter kits. Not live-deployed.

### Sign-in for preview addresses with Cloudflare Access — implemented, fixture-verified

Manage → Domains → Who can open previews (`src/PreviewAccess.tsx`, `worker/access.ts`, migration 0024 `project_access`): preview addresses (Worker Previews and the preview address of an unpublished upload) are public by default; this puts a Cloudflare Access application in front of them, created in the customer's own Zero Trust organization. `GET /api/projects/:id/access` returns the organization (`GET /access/organizations`; any answer other than 401/403/429 means none yet, with Cloudflare's message), its login methods (`GET /access/identity_providers`), and the protection (emails, email domains, how many other rules, session length) read back from Cloudflare, or `missing` when the recorded application was deleted there. `PUT …/access` `{ emails ≤ 50, domains ≤ 10, addLoginMethod, requestId, confirm }` (lower-cased, deduplicated, at least one rule) refuses without an organization; when the organization has **no** login method it refuses unless `addLoginMethod` was confirmed, then adds Cloudflare's One-time PIN (an organization with its own login methods is never changed, so Cloudcel can't weaken someone's existing sign-in); it then creates or updates `Cloudcel previews <worker>`: `type: self_hosted`, `destinations: [{ type: "preview_worker", worker_id: <recorded Worker tag> }]` (previews only; production stays public), one inline allow policy of `email` and `email_domain` rules, `session_duration: "24h"`, `app_launcher_visible: false`. An interrupted attempt's application is adopted only with Cloudcel's exact name and exactly this Worker's `preview_worker` destination (never someone's own application or a whole-Worker one). `DELETE …/access` removes it (already gone counts as removed). Journaled `previews.protect|unprotect` with only the Worker tag and application ID — who may sign in is never stored in Cloudcel. Check Cloudflare: protection counts as applied if the application changed since the operation began; removal if it is gone. Owners and developers only, dashboard only (API tokens get 403 on writes: it decides who sees unreleased work). Protection stays when previews are turned off, because existing Previews keep their addresses. Zero Trust itself is set up by the user in Cloudflare (team name, Free plan for 50 people, and a payment method Cloudflare asks for even on Free, per its setup guide), so the section explains that step and links to it; a failure to read Access shows inside the section only. Evidence: 13 tests in `tests/access.test.ts` (with a pessimistic fake whose name filter matches parts of names), 2 browser journeys (`tests/browser/access.spec.ts`: setup guidance, consent to One-time PIN, change, removal, axe at two widths; Access errors stay in the section), 27 server and 7 UI guards mutation-checked. Site: the features page marks protected previews Built with the payment-method caveat; the Vercel page and the "not yet" list describe it with Cloudflare sources. **Not live-verified** — see `docs/provider-setup.md` (which hostnames `preview_worker` covers, whether `domain` is needed, the organization endpoint's answer without Zero Trust).

### KV and R2 storage browser — implemented, fixture-verified

Manage → Storage (`src/Storage.tsx`, `worker/storage.ts`): the KV namespaces and R2 buckets the deployed Worker binds (read from its settings; IDs and bucket names validated before they reach a request path), or with `?environment=preview` the previews' staging copies. Routes under `/api/projects/:id/storage`: `GET /storage`; KV `GET kv/:binding/keys?prefix=&cursor=` (100 a page), `GET kv/:binding/value?key=` (the first 64 KB, shown only if it is UTF-8 without control characters; the reader stops after 64 KB), `GET kv/:binding/download?key=`, `PUT kv/:binding/value` `{ key, value ≤ 1 MB of text, requestId, confirm }` (looks up the key first and sends its metadata and expiration again, because Cloudflare replaces both on every write; refuses a key expiring within a minute, which Cloudflare would reject), `DELETE kv/:binding/value`; R2 `GET r2/:binding/objects?prefix=&cursor=` (one folder at a time with `delimiter=/`; a cursor only while `is_truncated`), `GET r2/:binding/object?key=`, `PUT r2/:binding/object` (multipart `file`, `key`, `requestId`, `confirm=true`; up to 25 MB; `Content-Type` kept only if it is a plain MIME type), `DELETE r2/:binding/object`. Writes are journaled as `kv.put|kv.delete|r2.put|r2.delete:<binding>[@preview]` with only the namespace or bucket and the key as intent, never a value; R2 writes and deletes send `cf-r2-data-catalog-check: true` (as Wrangler does) and every R2 call sends the binding's `cf-r2-jurisdiction`. Keys: KV up to 512 bytes and never `.`/`..`; R2 up to 1,024 bytes, opened only if no `/`-separated part is empty, `.` or `..` (Cloudflare wants slashes literal, so dot segments would otherwise be normalised into another API path). Downloads stream through as `application/octet-stream` attachments with `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox`, `nosniff`, `no-store`, an ASCII `filename` and a UTF-8 `filename*`; provider redirects are never followed (`cloudflareBody` in `worker/providers.ts`, whose timeout covers the headers only). Body limits: 2 MB for KV writes, 25 MB + 64 KB for R2 uploads, 8 KB elsewhere. Check Cloudflare: a KV or R2 deletion is confirmed by absence; an upload by an object modified since the operation began; a KV write can't be compared (no value kept). Viewers can't browse (like the D1 browser). Evidence: 27 tests in `tests/storage.test.ts` (paging, partial UTF-8, invalid UTF-8, streamed reads, quoted and non-ASCII names, malformed bindings, EU jurisdiction, recovery), a browser journey (`tests/browser/storage.spec.ts`: edit, delete, folders, download link, upload with the 25 MB limit, previews; axe and no horizontal scroll at 1440 and 375 px), 54 server and 12 UI guards mutation-checked. Chromium fetches `<a download>` links outside Playwright's routing, so the journey checks the download URL and the server tests check the response. API shapes from Cloudflare's API reference and Wrangler 4.148's own calls (8 Oct 2026); **not live-verified** (`docs/provider-setup.md` lists the KV and R2 permissions to check).

### Teams and roles — implemented, locally verified

Migration 0023 rebuilds `memberships` with roles `owner`, `developer` and `viewer` (the old `member` becomes `viewer`), adds `workspaces.kind` (`personal`/`team`) and `workspace_invitations`. `requireIdentity(c, 'view' | 'deploy' | 'owner')` replaces the owner boolean: **owners** keep everything (connections, account selection, workers.dev subdomain, disconnecting, Free-only, webhooks, the team); **developers** create and delete projects, deploy, run every Manage operation (the journal's and the pipeline's SQL role checks accept them), and manage their own API tokens; **viewers** read. Routes (`worker/teams.ts`; session only, refused for API tokens): `POST /api/workspaces` (team owned by its creator, at most 10 owned teams, session switched), `POST /api/auth/workspace` (switch, members only), `GET /api/auth/session` (role and every workspace), `GET`/`PUT`/`DELETE /api/workspace/members[/:userId]` (owners change roles and remove; anyone leaves a team; never the last owner; nobody leaves their personal workspace, which would lock their sign-in; removal revokes the person's tokens for the workspace and moves their sessions to their personal workspace), `GET`/`POST`/`DELETE /api/workspace/invitations` (a link for one GitHub login, shown once, stored as a hash, 7 days), `GET /api/invitations/:code` and `POST …/accept` (only for that GitHub login, once, before expiry). Fixed: connecting GitHub while signed in now saves the connection in the current workspace (owners only) instead of switching the session to the personal workspace. UI: a workspace switcher in the sidebar, the breadcrumb shows the workspace, a Team page (`src/Team.tsx`: people and roles, invitation links, pending invitations, creating a team), `#invite?code=` to join, and viewers aren't offered project creation. Evidence: 13 tests in `tests/teams.test.ts` (including a developer running a journaled Cloudflare operation with the team's connection), a security test for GitHub connections inside a team, 3 browser journeys; 16 guards mutation-checked. Site: Teams rows on the Vercel and Netlify pages (sources cited), the features page, and the Enterprise tier.

### Deploy notifications (webhooks) — implemented, locally verified

Manage → Notifications (`src/Notifications.tsx`, `worker/notifications.ts`, migration 0022): one webhook per project for Slack, Discord or any HTTPS endpoint. `GET`/`PUT`/`DELETE /api/projects/:id/notifications` and `POST …/notifications/test`; dashboard only (API tokens get 403 on writes: a webhook is a standing integration that sends data out). The address must be https on a public host name (no IP literals, credentials, `localhost`/`.local`/`.internal` or single-label hosts) and is stored encrypted with only its host in clear; a `whsec_` signing secret is shown once and stored encrypted. Events: `deployment.ready`, `deployment.failed` (first build failed, homepage check failed, pipeline needs attention, a later build failed), `upload.deployed`, `health.unhealthy`, `health.healthy`. `notify()` queues only subscribed events into `webhook_deliveries` and never throws; the scheduled handler delivers up to 20 a minute with `redirect: "manual"`, a 5-second timeout, `X-Cloudcel-Event`/`-Delivery`/`-Timestamp` headers and `X-Cloudcel-Signature: sha256=HMAC(secret, "timestamp.body")`; a claim stops overlapping runs from sending twice; failures retry after 1 and 4 minutes, then fail; finished deliveries are kept a week. Payloads carry `text` (Slack) and `content` (Discord). Evidence: 9 tests plus pipeline and health tests, a browser journey, 13 guards mutation-checked. Not live-verified against real Slack or Discord endpoints.

### Build variables — implemented, fixture-verified

Manage → Secrets & variables (renamed from Runtime secrets) → Build variables (`src/BuildVariables.tsx`): Workers Builds environment variables on the production trigger, or on the branch-preview trigger when previews are on. `GET /api/projects/:id/build-variables?environment=` (secret values come back `null` from Cloudflare and are never returned), `PUT …/build-variables` `{ environment, name, value (1–5000 characters), secret }` → `PATCH /builds/triggers/:trigger/environment_variables`, `DELETE …/build-variables/:name` → `DELETE …/environment_variables/:key`; journaled as `buildvar.set|remove:<name>[@preview]` with only the name and trigger as intent, so no value reaches Cloudcel's database. Check Cloudflare: a removal is confirmed by absence; a set can't be compared (no value kept), so it asks to set it again. Apps without a Git trigger (direct uploads) are refused. The form clears a typed value as soon as it is submitted for review. Evidence: 7 tests, a browser journey, 6 guards mutation-checked. API shapes from Cloudflare's API reference (8 Oct 2026); not live-verified.

### Analytics and logs — implemented, fixture-verified

Manage → Analytics & logs (`src/Observability.tsx`, `worker/observability.ts`): `GET /api/projects/:id/analytics?range=24h|7d` queries Cloudflare's GraphQL Analytics API (`workersInvocationsAdaptive`, filtered to the app's Worker, grouped by `datetimeHour` and `status`) and returns totals (requests, errors, subrequests, a request-weighted approximate median CPU time and the highest 99th percentile), hourly buckets and outcomes; GraphQL errors (including partial data) become a 502 with Cloudflare's message. `GET /api/projects/:id/logs?minutes=60..4320[&errors=1]` queries Workers Logs (`POST /workers/observability/telemetry/query`, `view: "events"`, `dry: true`, 100 events, filtered on `$metadata.service` and optionally `$metadata.error` exists). The request and event shapes follow Cloudflare's own observability MCP server (`apps/workers-observability/src/types/workers-logs.types.ts` in `cloudflare/mcp-server-cloudflare`), since the rendered API docs don't describe events. UI: period buttons, totals, an hourly bar chart with an accessible table, outcomes in plain words, logs with period and errors-only filters. Evidence: 7 tests, a browser journey (axe, two widths), 6 guards mutation-checked. **Not live-verified:** the OAuth grant's access to Account Analytics and Workers Observability (`docs/provider-setup.md` lists both), and the `datetimeHour` dimension on this dataset.

### D1 table browser and SQL console — implemented, locally verified

Manage → Database → Data (`src/DataBrowser.tsx`), for production or the previews' staging copy: `GET /api/projects/:id/databases/:binding/tables` (tables and views from `sqlite_master`, hiding `sqlite_*` and D1's `_cf_*`), `GET …/tables/:table?offset=` (50 rows a page plus `PRAGMA table_info`; only listed tables, names always quoted), `POST …/query` `{ sql, environment }`: a single `SELECT` or `EXPLAIN` runs at once (up to 200 rows returned); anything else answers 409 unless confirmed (`requestId`, `confirm`), and then runs journaled as `d1.query:<binding>[@preview]` after a Time Travel bookmark is recorded in `d1_restore_points`, so it appears with the migrations' restore points and can be undone; Check Cloudflare explains that D1 doesn't report whether interrupted SQL ran. Wide results scroll inside a focusable region. Evidence: 5 tests on a real SQLite fake D1, a browser journey (axe, 375 px with a 14-column table), 6 guards mutation-checked. Site: the Supabase page and features page describe it; Supabase's spreadsheet-style editor and data APIs remain reasons to choose Supabase.

### Cron schedules and more of Cloudflare's free platform in imports — implemented, locally verified

- **Imports** (`inspectPlatform` in `worker/repository-plan.ts`, every adapter): `ai`, `browser`, `images`, `version_metadata` (binding name only), plain `vars`, `placement: { mode: "smart" }`, `triggers.crons` (up to five five-field expressions), and outside the container adapter SQLite-backed Durable Objects of the app's own Worker (bindings without `script_name`; migrations that create with `new_sqlite_classes`, rename or delete; key-value `new_classes` and transfers refused; each bound class needs a creating migration; deletion warns). Binding names stay unique across every kind. Queues and Workflows are still refused (account-wide names). Warnings state the free allowances.
- **Previews:** the generated `previews` block now carries these bindings (Workers AI, browser rendering, Images, version metadata, vars and the app's Durable Objects, which get a namespace per Preview); a committed block's Durable Objects may not name another Worker. Migration 0021 adds `project_previews.config_json` so the dashboard shows the same snippet the check produced.
- **Manage → Schedules:** `GET`/`PUT /api/projects/:id/schedules` (up to five distinct expressions, journaled `schedules.set`, Check Cloudflare compares Cloudflare's list). Wrangler only writes schedules when the configuration lists `triggers.crons` (verified in 4.148's `triggersDeploy`), so dashboard schedules survive Git deploys of configurations without `triggers`.
- **Evidence:** `tests/imports-plan.test.ts` (8), 5 schedule tests in `tests/operations.test.ts`, two new Wrangler runs in `tests/deploy-command.test.ts` (a production dry run with AI, browser, vars, cron, Smart Placement and a Durable Object; a Preview whose generated block gives it Workers AI and its own Durable Object namespace — Wrangler sends `durable_object_namespace` with `class_name`), a schedules browser journey; 17 guards mutation-checked (all caught after three tests were tightened). Site: the features page, Vercel's cron row and Netlify's "moving" note now describe schedules.

### One-click rollback and traffic splitting — implemented, locally verified

Manage → Builds & versions → "Versions and rollback" loads with the tab: each version shows its live share (`GET /versions` `live`), a primary "Roll back to Version N" button targets the version before the one serving 100%, any version can get all traffic ("Promote", accessible name "Send all traffic to Version N"), and "Split traffic" sends 1–99% to a version with the rest on the live one (Cloudflare's gradual deployments allow two versions). New `POST /api/projects/:id/traffic` `{ requestId, confirm, versions: [{ versionId, percentage }, { versionId, percentage }] }`: two different versions, shares 1–99 adding up to 100, both fetched under the owned Worker, `POST …/deployments?force=false` with `strategy: "percentage"`, journaled as `version.split:<id>@<share>,<id>@<share>`. Health tracking confirms the first (rolled-out) version serves its share (target `<id>@<share>`) and probes it with Cloudflare's `Cloudflare-Workers-Version-Overrides` header; "Check Cloudflare" reconciles an interrupted split. Direct-upload projects no longer show the Git build history. Evidence: 6 operations tests, a browser journey (rollback, split, axe, two widths), 8 guards mutation-checked. Not live-verified: the version override header on workers.dev and gradual deployments through the OAuth grant.

### Branch and pull request previews with Cloudflare Worker Previews — implemented, fixture-verified

Netlify's "every PR gets a preview URL", using Cloudflare's Worker Previews (open beta, 22 Sep 2026), which replace the earlier second-Worker design. Design brief and open questions: `docs/research/workers-previews.md`.

- **How it works:** previews live on the production Worker. Turning them on (`POST /api/projects/:id/previews`, journaled `previews.enable`, resumable) records a random suffix first (migration 0020, `project_previews`), creates staging copies of the app's D1/KV/R2 bindings named `<worker>-pv-<suffix>-<binding>` (consent required; Free-only refuses R2), and returns `waiting_for_config` with a snippet (`"preview_urls": true` and a `previews` block binding exactly those copies) until the repository commits it. Wrangler fails every preview build without a block and Previews inherit no bindings, so this keeps previews off production data. Once the block at the production-branch head binds exactly the copies (`previewsBlockIssues`), Cloudcel adds the trigger `Cloudcel previews <worker>` on the production Worker (`npx wrangler@4.148.0 preview --worker-name <worker>`, all branches except production) and turns on Preview addresses with the production address left as it was. Interrupted attempts adopt only resources with the recorded suffix and exactly one trigger with Cloudcel's name.
- **Routes:** `GET …/previews/plan` (read-only), `GET …/previews` (status, staging copies, snippet, Previews from Cloudflare's beta API with only https addresses, the latest deployment's commit and pull request link, recent builds from the preview trigger; `?details=0` for status only), `DELETE …/previews` (deletes only the trigger; Previews and staging copies stay, and turning on again reuses the copies), `DELETE …/previews/:previewId`. Refused for Next.js, containers, Python with packages and direct uploads (`previewRefusal`).
- **Elsewhere:** the second-Worker pipeline steps (`configure_previews`, alias URLs, `previewWorkerName`) are gone and migration 0020 disables old preview jobs; Manage → Database's "Previews" environment uses the staging database (`stagingDatabase`); preview builds of the production Worker are marked in Manage → Builds and get no production health check (`productionBuild` in `worker/health.ts`, also used by the push-build scanner); `target()` no longer takes an environment.
- **UI:** `src/Previews.tsx` in the project's deployment panel: review with staging copies and consent, the snippet with copy, "Check again and turn on", the Previews list with addresses, commits, pull request links and delete, recent preview builds, turn off and back on.
- **Evidence:** `tests/previews-plan.test.ts` (13), 12 previews tests in `tests/deployments.test.ts`, migrations tests on the staging database, and `tests/deploy-command.test.ts`, which runs the exact trigger command with the pinned Wrangler 4.148.0 against a local mock of Cloudflare's API: it names the Preview after `WORKERS_CI_BRANCH` and sends only the staging bindings (D1 as `database_id`, KV as `namespace_id`); without a block it fails before creating anything. Browser journey: review, consent, snippet, turn on, list, delete, turn off. 20 guards mutation-checked (all caught after adding the decoy-namespace test and removing a redundant check). **Not live-verified:** everything in `docs/provider-setup.md` step 11 — notably whether API-created Workers use the Previews model without the dashboard's one-way switch, whether PR comments appear for this command, `WRANGLER_CI_OVERRIDE_NAME`, the build token's permissions on Previews, the beta API's real responses, and whether Previews count toward the 100-Worker limit.

### AI agents (MCP), the `cloudcel` CLI, direct uploads with drag-and-drop, API tokens — implemented, locally verified

User request (8 Oct 2026): Netlify's ways to start — an AI agent, Git with PR previews, the terminal, drag and drop — and one-click rollback. Decisions: the user's own agent through MCP and the CLI (Cloudcel runs no model); deploys go into the user's own Cloudflare account (no anonymous hosting). HTTP contract: `docs/contracts/direct-deploy-and-tokens.md`.

- **API tokens (migration 0018; `worker/tokens.ts`):** `cc_pat_` + 32 random bytes, stored as SHA-256, shown once; created, listed and revoked by a signed-in owner (dashboard: API & CLI, `src/Developers.tsx`). A Bearer token authenticates `/api/*` and `/mcp` as its user and workspace (membership re-checked on every request; `last_used_at` written at most once a minute) and can't manage tokens, approve CLI logins, use `/api/auth/*`, change connections, or turn Free-only on or off. **CLI device login:** `POST /api/cli/login` (an 8-character code from an unambiguous alphabet, rate-limited) → approve in the dashboard (`/app/#cli-login?code=…`) → the CLI's poll receives a 90-day token named `CLI on <host>` exactly once.
- **Direct uploads (migration 0019; `worker/uploads.ts`, `shared/assets.ts`):** Cloudflare's assets upload session and asset hash (BLAKE3 as Wrangler 4.148 computes it; verified identical), files sent in base64 batches and re-hashed by the server, then a Worker with static assets — assets-only, or with a `_worker.js` module and an `ASSETS` binding; `_redirects`/`_headers`; `404.html`, single-page app or nothing for unknown paths. The first upload stays **unpublished**: its workers.dev route is off and only the version's preview address works. That address is unlisted, not access-controlled — anyone with the link can open it, and the UI, CLI and MCP say so (they say "unpublished", never "private"). Publish/unpublish routes; upload history. Upload tokens are sealed in D1 and cleared after deploy. A Git project refuses uploads; an upload project refuses Git pipelines and branch previews. Names already used in the account (Cloudcel's records or Cloudflare) are never adopted. **Dashboard (`src/Upload.tsx`):** drop or pick a folder in the project's deployment panel; hidden files, `node_modules` and `.git` are skipped; app name, single-page-app and publish options on the first upload; history with preview addresses.
- **CLI (`packages/cli`, `scripts/build-cli.mjs`):** `login` (device flow; token in `~/.config/cloudcel/config.json`, mode 0600), `logout`, `whoami`, `projects`, `create`, `deploy [folder]` (`.cloudcelignore`; uploads only what Cloudflare lacks; links the folder in `.cloudcel/project.json`; suggests `dist` when pointed at source), `publish`/`unpublish`, `versions`, `rollback`, `open`. `CLOUDCEL_URL`/`CLOUDCEL_TOKEN` for CI; https only (http for localhost). **Distribution:** the `cloudcel` name was unregistered on npm on 8 Oct 2026 and publishing is the owner's decision, so the dashboard no longer says `npx cloudcel` (that would fail, or run someone else's package if the name were taken). `npm run cli:build` packs `public/cli/cloudcel.tgz` (deterministic, laid out like `npm pack`) and every Cloudcel serves its own CLI: `npm install -g <origin>/cli/cloudcel.tgz`, or `npx --yes --package=<origin>/cli/cloudcel.tgz cloudcel …` in CI. Verified by installing the tarball into a scratch prefix and running it through `npx --package`. Both outputs are gitignored; `npm run dev` and `npm run build` build them first, and the CLI test builds before checking the binary (CI runs `npm test` before `npm run build`).
- **MCP server (`worker/mcp.ts`, `POST /mcp`, added to `run_worker_first`):** Streamable HTTP with JSON responses; MCP 2026-07-28 (stateless per-request `_meta`, header checks, `server/discover`) and the 2025 revisions (`initialize`). Bearer tokens only; a foreign `Origin` → 403; 10 MiB request limit. Eleven tools call Cloudcel's own API with the caller's token (`list_projects`, `create_project`, `get_project`, `deploy_files`, `publish`, `list_deployments`, `list_versions`, `rollback`, `list_secrets`, `set_secret`, `get_signin_info`) plus the prompt `build_and_deploy`. **Dashboard:** an "AI agents" page (`src/Agents.tsx`) with the token step and copyable settings for Claude Code (`claude mcp add --transport http …`, or `.mcp.json` with `${CLOUDCEL_TOKEN}`), Codex (`bearer_token_env_var`), Cursor (`${env:CLOUDCEL_TOKEN}`) and other clients, all reading the token from `CLOUDCEL_TOKEN`. No OAuth for MCP yet, so connectors that only sign in with OAuth (chat apps' connector settings) can't connect.
- **Rollback API:** `GET /api/projects/:id/deployment` (the deployment serving traffic) and a `live` share on each version; the CLI and MCP roll back to the version before the one serving 100%. The dashboard's one-click rollback is the next-but-one item.
- **Evidence:** `tests/tokens.test.ts` (10), `uploads.test.ts` (6), `cli.test.ts` (8: the CLI end to end against the real API and a fake Cloudflare, plus the packed tarball), `mcp.test.ts` (12: protocol and tools end to end), fake Cloudflare in `tests/helpers/cloudflare-uploads.ts`; browser journeys `developers.spec.ts` (2), `upload.spec.ts` (1), `agents.spec.ts` (1, including a real 401 from `/mcp` on the dev server). Mutation checks: 11 upload guards, 20 MCP guards, the CLI's guards — all caught. **Not live-verified:** whether Cloudflare accepts the OAuth grant for the assets upload and per-script subdomain APIs; preview addresses on real workers.dev; real agents connecting (Claude Code, Codex and Cursor settings follow their documentation as of 8 Oct 2026).

### Security review fixes for sign-in & realtime — implemented, locally verified

An independent review (8 Oct 2026) found 2 high, 3 medium and 8 low issues, several reproduced in Miniflare. All are fixed with regression tests, and every fix was mutation-checked (16 service guards, 8 control-route guards).

- **Login CSRF (high):** each sign-in request is bound to the browser that started it (`__Host-cc_browser` cookie; hash in the new `auth_request_browsers` table). The sign-in page, passkey calls and both OAuth legs refuse a request started in another browser, so an attacker can no longer finish their own GitHub/Google sign-in in a victim's browser.
- **Ownership by proof, not timing (high):** a random suffix is recorded before anything is created (migration 0017 `project_backends.resource_suffix`); the database is `<worker>-db-<suffix>` and the Worker carries `CLOUDCEL_BACKEND_OWNER = <project id>:<suffix>`. An interrupted setup adopts only resources with that proof; a same-named Worker without it is refused (and left alone on removal). Setup refuses a name another Cloudcel app in the account deploys under, and Git deploys refuse names a sign-in service uses.
- **Refresh rotation (medium):** claiming a token and storing its successor are one D1 batch that also requires no revoked token in the family (`changes()` gates the insert); a refresh token is refused once any token of its family is revoked.
- **Full-table scans (medium):** `expires_at` indexes on every expiring table (added by Install update); cleanup runs on 1 in 20 new sign-ins through those indexes.
- **Secret writes (medium) and Install update (low):** provider secrets, realtime-secret rotation and updates first require the recorded service Worker with its recorded tag; a missing Worker gives 409 instead of being recreated without secrets.
- **Sign-in off (low)** now refuses the sign-in page, passkey calls, OAuth and token issuance including refresh; issued access tokens last up to an hour. **Disabled users (low)** can't join realtime, and channels re-check members with a Durable Object alarm (every 5 minutes, only while such sockets exist; `REALTIME_RECHECK_SECONDS` shortens it in tests), closing sockets of disabled users and expired tokens. **Server messages** carry `server: true`; member messages carry `from`. **Anonymous listeners** have their own cap (500 per channel) and a per-address cap (20, hashed `CF-Connecting-IP`). **Access tokens** have `typ: at+jwt` and ID tokens are refused as bearer tokens. **Malformed input** returns 4xx (non-object JWT parts, bad percent-escapes, `Object.hasOwn` for provider names). **Logout** without a valid `id_token_hint` for the signed-in user shows a confirmation page that POSTs same-origin.
- Dashboard copy matches: what turning sign-in off does, and that disabling a user ends sessions, refresh tokens and realtime connections within 5 minutes.
- Test harness note: Miniflare does not forward a server-side close of a hibernatable WebSocket to the test client (probed separately), so the disabled-user test checks the channel's presence and delivery instead.

### Free-only mode — implemented, locally verified

User decision (8 Oct 2026): honest "free" wording plus a per-project switch, rather than claiming "100% free". `projects.free_only` (migration 0016; on by default; existing projects that already use R2 or containers start with it off). While on, Cloudcel refuses anything Cloudflare can bill for: R2 storage (project creation, inspection issues, deploy, and preview resources) and containers (Docker projects can't be created Free-only). `PUT /api/projects/:id/free-only` (owner) turns it off only with `confirm: true` and back on only while nothing billable is in use. `worker/free-only.ts` holds the rule. UI: a Free-only option in the create dialog (R2 disabled while on), a header badge, and a Billing panel with a confirmed toggle. Site: free-plan list and FAQ ("Can I make sure I never pay anything?"). 9 guards mutation-checked.

### Sign-in & realtime for customers' apps, front page repositioning, provider transport fix — implemented, locally verified

Requested by the user: sign-in (shown as "Not included" against Supabase's 50,000 monthly active users) and realtime "should be included … and built around cloudflare"; the front page should present Cloudcel as a Vercel alternative without naming it ("for legal purpose"), "from hobby to enterprise, lowest cost thanks to cloudflare"; Cloudcel is "a no-code provider that simplifies the setting up and managing part on cloudflare sides of things".

- **Service (`services/backend/`)**, deployed by Cloudcel into the customer's account as the Worker `<app>-backend` with D1 database `<app>-backend-db-<suffix>` and Durable Object class `Channel` (SQLite). It is an OpenID Connect provider: discovery, JWKS (RFC 7638 key ID), authorization code with PKCE S256 for public clients only, ES256 ID and access tokens (1 hour), rotating refresh tokens (30 days) that revoke the whole family on reuse, RFC 7009 revocation, userinfo, logout to registered addresses, and CORS only for registered app origins. The hosted sign-in page (strict CSP; fingerprinted, cacheable script and styles) offers passkeys (WebAuthn with attestation `none`, ES256/RS256, user verification required, origin/rpId/challenge checks, counter-based clone detection), GitHub (primary verified email) and Google (OpenID Connect with PKCE). Accounts link only by verified email. Realtime: WebSockets at `/realtime/channels/<name>` with hibernation; `public:` channels are open to anonymous listeners; other channels need an HS256 channel token signed by the app with the realtime secret (`channels` as exact names or `prefix*`, `publish`, `info` up to 1 KB); a sign-in access token may listen on `user:<sub>` and public channels; server publishing (`POST …/messages`) and presence (`GET …/presence`) need the realtime secret; limits of 1,000 connections and 64 KB messages per channel. Passwords are deliberately excluded: safe password hashing needs more CPU than Workers Free's 10 ms per request, and resets need an email service.
- **Bundle:** `npm run backend:build` (`scripts/build-backend.mjs`, esbuild) writes `worker/generated/backend.ts` (`backendVersion` content hash, `backendSchema`, `backendScript`); `-- --check` fails when it is stale, and `npm run build` runs it first. `worker/generated` is in `.prettierignore`.
- **Control routes (`worker/backend.ts`; migration 0015 `project_backends`):** `GET`/`POST`/`DELETE /projects/:id/backend`, `PUT …/backend/settings`, `PUT`/`DELETE …/backend/providers/:provider`, `GET …/backend/users`, `POST …/backend/users/:userId/:action` (disable ends sessions and revokes refresh tokens), `POST …/backend/realtime-secret`, `POST …/backend/update`. Writes go through `change()` (confirmation, request ID, journal). Setup: workers.dev subdomain check → D1 database (an interrupted attempt's database is adopted only by its exact suffixed name; see the security slice) → schema → settings → script upload (D1 and Durable Object bindings, migration `v1` on the first upload, a new ES256 signing key and realtime secret bound as secrets; later uploads use `keep_bindings: ["secret_text"]`) → the script's workers.dev route → the app Worker's `CLOUDCEL_AUTH_ISSUER`, `CLOUDCEL_CLIENT_ID`, `CLOUDCEL_REALTIME_URL` and `CLOUDCEL_REALTIME_SECRET` secrets. Keys and secrets never reach Cloudcel's database or journal. A Worker Cloudcel did not create, or one replaced since (tag mismatch), is never replaced or removed. Removal requires typing the Worker name and deletes the app secrets, the Worker (`?force=true`, with its Durable Object namespace) and the database; it also cleans up a stopped setup. The status route stays usable when the database can't be read (`settingsError`), so the service can still be removed.
- **UI:** Manage → Sign-in & realtime (`src/Backend.tsx`): what will be created, setup and "Continue setup", status (issuer, client ID, realtime address, version and update), integration snippets, settings (app name, return addresses, sign-in and realtime switches), GitHub and Google with the callback URL to register (the client secret leaves the form when submitted or cancelled), users (disable, enable, delete), realtime secret rotation, removal. The shared confirmation dialog now returns focus to the control that opened it, or to the current Manage tab when that control is gone; before, focus fell to the page body after every confirmed change.
- **Release-blocking fix:** `worker/providers.ts` passed `redirect: 'error'` to `fetch`; the Workers runtime rejects that value, so every GitHub and Cloudflare API call would have failed in production while Node-based tests passed. It now uses `redirect: 'manual'` and treats a redirect as a failed response; `tests/runtime.test.ts` runs the transport in workerd.
- **Front page:** "From side project to enterprise, on your own Cloudflare" (title "Cloudcel — the no-code platform for your own Cloudflare"), features including sign-in and realtime, "No infrastructure code", Hobby $0 / Growth $5 / Enterprise (your contract with Cloudflare), the free plans and why it costs less. A test fails if it names Vercel, Netlify, Supabase, Firebase, Render, Railway or Heroku. "Lowest cost" was not used (an unsupported superlative); the copy says Cloudflare's own prices with no markup. The comparison pages still name competitors: legal review recommended.
- **Site content:** the Supabase and Firebase pages show sign-in and realtime as included, with what they use of the free plan (a sign-in is about 5 to 7 Worker requests; Durable Objects allow 100,000 requests and 13,000 GB-seconds a day, outgoing WebSocket messages are free and hibernating connections cost no duration — Cloudflare's pricing page, checked 8 October 2026); Vercel's features table lists sign-in (Marketplace providers) and realtime (Vercel Functions now serve WebSockets natively, so the site does not claim Vercel lacks realtime); new FAQ entries; "not yet" lists no email/password, magic links or phone sign-in, no row-level security, and no database-change streams.
- **Evidence:** 18 service tests in workerd with a real D1 database, SQLite Durable Object and WebSockets plus a software authenticator (`tests/helpers/authenticator.ts`); 15 control-route tests; 2 browser journeys (fixtures, axe, three widths); mutation-checked. Not live-verified: see `docs/provider-setup.md` §6 "Sign-in & realtime" for the checklist and the API assumptions that only fixtures cover.

### Public site, Free plan matched to Cloudflare's, dashboard at /app/ — implemented, locally verified

Requested by the user: make the free-plan offering clear on the front page, offer the full limits of Cloudflare's free plan, and add pages comparing Cloudcel with Vercel, Supabase and others, plus "Why choose us" and why it costs less.

- **Free plan (migration 0014):** up to 100 projects (Cloudflare's Workers Free allows 100 Workers per account), no monthly cap on new pipelines (the trigger is dropped; Cloudflare's build minutes apply), one setup at a time (the existing `workspace_active_pipeline` index; Workers Free runs one build at a time). Existing free workspaces are raised to 100. `/api/plan` returns `projectLimit: 100`, `pipelineLimit: null`. The in-app Plan page and the project-limit error say so.
- **Public site:** static HTML generated by `npm run site:build` (`scripts/build-site.mjs`) from `site/content.ts` (data with an official source for every price, checked 8 October 2026) and `site/render.ts` (templates; no JSX, runs under Node's type stripping) into `public/`: `/` (front page with the Cloudcel and Cloudflare free plans, reasons, a worked example, comparison cards), `/pricing/` (free and Workers Paid limits table, four worked examples, FAQ, sources), `/why/` (seven reasons and what isn't ready yet), `/compare/` and `/compare/{vercel,netlify,supabase,firebase,render,railway,heroku}/`, and `404.html`. `public/site.css` is hand-written. Generated pages are in `.prettierignore`; `tests/site.test.ts` fails when `public/` is stale. `npm run build` regenerates the site first.
- **Dashboard moved to `/app/`:** `app/index.html` is the Vite client entry (`environments.client.build.rollupOptions.input`; a top-level `build.input` breaks the Worker build). OAuth callbacks redirect to `/app/…`. Assets use `not_found_handling: "404-page"`. Verified with `vite preview` (workerd): `/` 200, `/app` → `/app/`, `/compare/vercel` → trailing slash, unknown paths 404, `/api/*` to the Worker, CSP applied.
- **Research:** five agents read the official pricing and docs pages (Cloudflare, Vercel, Supabase, Netlify, Firebase, Render, Railway, Heroku) on 8 October 2026; facts used are cited per page. Notable: Vercel Pro's Flat Rate CDN (since 8 Sep 2026) includes 1 TB and 50 TB tiers from $20, so bandwidth is not a strong argument against Vercel; R2 bills beyond its free tier even on Cloudflare's free plan; Workers Paid has no hard spending cap (budget alerts only); Render's plans became flat-priced in 2026; Netlify pauses all projects when credits run out.
- **Claims deliberately not made:** the user suggested "100,000× cheaper"; no sourced scenario supports that, so the site shows worked examples instead ($45 vs $5.15 for a small commercial app, $65 vs $0 for five side projects, $47.50–$92.50 vs $1.35 for 1 TB of downloads, $12–$13.30 vs $5 for an always-on API). "No surprise bills" became "Predictable bills".
- **Legal review needed (raised to the user):** Cloudflare's Self-Serve Subscription Agreement (updated 12 Sep 2025) §2.2.1(a) forbids signing up for the Services on behalf of a third party or selling access; §2.2.1(h) forbids processing or collecting credit card information on a web property receiving Free Services. Cloudcel uses customers' own accounts (no sign-ups or resale), and the site's FAQ tells free-plan apps to use a hosted checkout.

### Free workers.dev addresses — implemented, locally verified

Requested by the user ("people should be able to use their github.io domain … like vercel so its cheap"). A `github.io` address cannot serve a Cloudflare-hosted app (only GitHub Pages serves it; Vercel cannot use one either). Cloudflare's free equivalent of `vercel.app` is `<worker>.<account subdomain>.workers.dev`, which Cloudcel already published but under unreadable generated names.

- `POST /projects/:id/deployments` accepts `workerName` (`appName` in `worker/repository-plan.ts`: one DNS label, 1–63 lowercase letters/numbers/hyphens). The UI defaults it to the project name, so the app lives at `https://<project>.<subdomain>.workers.dev`. Without `workerName` the API still generates `cc-<project>-<id12>`. Adapters that keep their configured name (Next.js, containers) refuse a different `workerName`. `bucketNameIssues` rejects names whose `<name>-<binding>` R2 buckets exceed 63 characters. Per-account uniqueness and the queued-step "Worker already exists" check mean a chosen name is never adopted; Wrangler 4.148 always creates new `<name>-<binding>` resources (never adopts), so a taken resource name fails the build instead.
- Workers Builds cannot register a workers.dev subdomain (Wrangler only auto-registers when it detects an AI agent), so the deploy route refuses accounts without one. `workersSubdomain` (`worker/health.ts`) reads it, treating Cloudflare error 10007 as "none" whatever the HTTP status (`providerJSON`/`cloudflare` option `missingCodes`); `probeHomepage` and preview URLs use it.
- `GET /api/connections/cloudflare/subdomain` returns `{subdomain}` (null when none). `POST /api/connections/cloudflare/subdomain` `{subdomain, confirm:true}` (native switch, real session) registers one only when the account has none (`PUT /accounts/{id}/workers/subdomain`); it never changes an existing subdomain, which would move every Worker's address.
- UI: deployment step "3. Choose the app's free address" (editable app name with the live URL, fixed name for Next.js/containers, claim form with confirmation when the account has no subdomain; Deploy stays disabled for an invalid name or a missing subdomain), the free address in Manage → Domains ("custom domain is optional"), and a starter-kit README note. Cloudflare treats workers.dev as a free site for personal and hobby projects; the UI says to use a custom domain for business-critical apps.
- Evidence: 14 backend tests and 2 browser journeys plus updated deployment/container journeys; every guard mutation-checked. Unverified live: the subdomain endpoints' real responses and OAuth scope.

### Container Workers import adapter — implemented, fixture-verified

Runtime `docker` (catalog stage `import`) → adapter `container` in `worker/repository-plan.ts`.

- Accepts root Wrangler JSON/JSONC with one to five `containers`, `durable_objects.bindings` (name + class_name only, classes of this Worker), `migrations` or `exports` that only create, rename, or delete classes, and `upload_source_maps`, plus the usual Workers/Assets keys and draft D1/R2/KV bindings. Two scheduling policies: default (`image`, `max_instances`, `instance_type` named or custom, `image_build_context`, `image_vars`, rollout settings, `observability`, `name`) and Cloudflare's beta Durable Object-managed policy (`scheduling_policy: durable_object`, `images.<name>.dockerfile|image`) used by Cloudflare's current containers template.
- Refused: SSH/authorized keys/placement/affinities/unsafe settings (unsupported keys are listed), Durable Object bindings with `script_name`/`environment`, `transferred_classes` and exports in a transfer state (they move another Worker's data), unbound container classes, Dockerfile paths outside the repository, registry images without host and tag, and a repository with containers imported under another runtime (it is pointed to Docker / custom runtime).
- `inspectRepository` reads each Dockerfile named in the configuration (metadata only) at the pinned commit (`containerDockerfiles`).
- The plan returns `containers` (`className`, `policy`, `images`, `instanceType`, `maxInstances`; Wrangler defaults `lite`/20 shown; null for the managed policy) and warnings (Workers Paid billing, Docker builds in Workers Builds and the build token's Containers permission, registry pulls, beta policy, Durable Object migrations, first-deploy provisioning).
- **Deploys under the configured Worker name**: Wrangler names container applications `<config name>-<class>` from the configuration even when `--name` differs (verified with the real CLI: a generated name breaks this). Name collisions are prevented by the per-account name uniqueness and the queued-step "Worker already exists" check; Wrangler itself refuses to reuse a container application bound to another Durable Object namespace.
- `POST /projects/:id/deployments` requires `confirmContainers: true` when containers are listed. Deploy command: the usual pinned `npx wrangler@4.148.0 deploy … --name <configured>`; no build command unless the repository has a build script.
- Health check: a first container deployment can take minutes to provision, so for adapter `container` a failing homepage keeps the job in `health_check` for up to 15 minutes (`health_since`, migration 0013), then `needs_attention` with a container-specific message. Recovery resets the window.
- Branch previews are refused (`previewRefusal`): Cloudflare creates no preview URLs for Workers with Durable Objects, and `versions upload` deploys no container images.
- UI: "Containers to deploy" list with a separate billing consent ("Deploy these containers as <name> …"), a startup hint while the first container starts, and a Manage → Builds & versions note that promoting a version changes Worker code only (default-policy containers keep their latest image).
- Evidence: 19 backend tests, 2 browser journeys, fixtures `container` and `container-managed` in `scripts/verify-frameworks.mjs` (they need a running Docker daemon; Wrangler 4.148 builds images even with `--dry-run` and prints `Untagged: <image>:wrangler-…` after removing them). Unverified live: Docker builds and pushes in Workers Builds, the build-token permission, provisioning time, rollouts, and Workers Paid detection (Cloudcel cannot detect the plan; a Free account's build fails).

### Python Workers import adapters — implemented, fixture-verified

- Adapter `python` (no packages): `.py` entry point, `python_workers` flag, and (since the local testing round of 8 Oct 2026) a compatibility date before 2026-04-21 or `disable_python_external_sdk`; npm files optional. Deploy with the pinned Wrangler command.
- Adapter `python-packages` (dependencies in `pyproject.toml` `[project]`, or workers-py declared; needs `wrangler.jsonc`): requires `uv.lock`, `workers-py` in a dev dependency group, and `package.json` + `package-lock.json` with `wrangler` (pywrangler runs `npx --yes wrangler`, so the repository must pin it; versions other than 4.148.0 get a warning). Deploy command: `pipx run --spec uv==0.12.23 uv run pywrangler deploy --no-autoconfig --x-provision --x-auto-create --name <worker>`. No build command. Warning: only Pyodide-compatible packages work.
- Build image facts used (Cloudflare docs): Python 3.13, pip, pipx 1.7.1, no uv; Node 24 default.
- Evidence: Python tests in `tests/deployments.test.ts`; fixtures `python` (package-free with D1) and `python-fastapi` (`uv lock`, then the exact pywrangler command with `--dry-run`, which used Wrangler 4.148.0). `verify-frameworks.mjs` isolates `PIPX_HOME`, `PIPX_BIN_DIR`, and `UV_CACHE_DIR`.

### Isolated branch previews — implemented, locally verified

- `worker/previews.ts` (registered under `/api`): `GET /projects/:id/previews/plan` (read-only, resources at the production-branch head), `POST /projects/:id/previews` (owner, real session, consent, deployed production with a trigger, resource consent for separate preview resources, reuses production's build token), `GET /projects/:id/previews` (job plus preview builds with alias URLs), `DELETE /projects/:id/previews` (`{confirm:true}`; deletes only the preview trigger; status `disabled`).
- Migration 0012 adds `deployment_jobs.environment` (`production`/`preview`). A preview job creates Worker `cc-<project>-pv-<id12>` and trigger `Cloudcel previews <worker>` for all branches; its first build deploys the production commit so Wrangler creates separate, empty resources; step `configure_previews` then PATCHes the trigger to `npx wrangler@4.148.0 versions upload … --name <preview worker>` and excludes the production branch. Alias URLs follow Wrangler 4.148's algorithm (`previewAlias`, sanitized branch, 63-character label, 4-hex hash suffix): `https://<alias>-<worker>.<subdomain>.workers.dev/`.
- Manage operations stay on production (`target()` filters by environment); Manage → Database has an environment picker for preview databases (journal kinds get an `@preview` suffix). Previews are refused for Next.js (self-reference) and containers.
- UI: "Branch previews" section in Deployments (review plan, consents, enable, load preview builds, disable with confirmation).

### Database backups and point-in-time restore — implemented, locally verified

- `POST /projects/:id/databases/:binding/export` `{requestId, confirm, bookmark?, environment?}` runs one polling step of D1's export API per call (`output_format: polling`; Cloudflare cancels exports that are not polled, and the database cannot serve queries while one runs). Returns `{status:'active', bookmark}` or `{status:'complete', filename, url}`; the one-hour signed URL is returned once and **never stored**; a `d1.export:<binding>` journal row records the filename and bookmark.
- `POST /projects/:id/databases/:binding/restore` `{requestId, confirm, timestamp, environment?}`: Time Travel restore to a time within the past 30 days (7 days on Workers Free), journaled as `d1.restore:<binding>` with Cloudflare's undo bookmark.
- UI: Manage → Database → Backups (export with confirmation and polling to a download link; restore to a chosen time with confirmation).
- Starter kits with a database include `docs/backup.workflow.yml` (manual GitHub Actions export with a commented schedule, `npx wrangler d1 export … --remote`, 30-day artifact) and a README section. Cloudcel does not schedule exports itself because each export blocks the database.

### Recovery for Manage operations and push-build health — implemented, locally verified

- Migration 0010 adds `project_operations.intent_json` (operation parameters, never secret values; recorded by `change()` through `options.intent`) and `reconciled_at`.
- `worker/operation-recovery.ts`: `POST /projects/:id/operations/:operationId/reconcile` for `needs_attention` operations or `running` ones older than 120 seconds. Read-only checks: secret set (absent → `not_applied`; present → stays `needs_attention` because values are never revealed), secret removal, domain attach/detach, version promotion, redeploy (a build for the recorded trigger and commit created after the operation), D1 migrations (compare `d1_migrations`), D1 restore (cannot be verified). Confirmed promotions/redeploys start their health check. `not_applied` means safe to submit again. UI: "Check Cloudflare" in Operation history.
- Migration 0011 adds `observed_builds`, `build_scans`, and an index on `operation_health(project_id, target)`. `registerBuilds`/`scheduledBuildScans` in `worker/health.ts`: every five minutes per deployed production project (and when the build list loads) Cloudcel reads recent builds; the first scan is a baseline; later builds that are new, less than an hour old, and not already tracked get a health check `push:<build_uuid>`. The cron runs scans before health checks.

### Next.js import adapter — implemented, fixture-verified

Adapter `nextjs`: requires `next` and `@opennextjs/cloudflare`, a build script, root Wrangler JSON/JSONC with `.open-next` output, `services` limited to `WORKER_SELF_REFERENCE` → the configured `name`, an optional `images` binding, and a committed `open-next.config.ts` without `incrementalCache`/`tagCache`/`queue`/`cachePurge` (comments ignored). Build command `npx opennextjs-cloudflare build`. Deploys under the configured name (a generated `--name` would leave `WORKER_SELF_REFERENCE` pointing at another Worker). Migration 0009 rebuilds `deployment_jobs` with `UNIQUE(account_id, worker_name)`. vinext is not used.

### Reviewed D1 migrations — implemented, locally verified

`worker/migrations.ts`: `GET /projects/:id/migrations?binding=DB[&environment=preview]` lists pending `<migrations_dir>/*.sql` in Wrangler 4.148 order with SQL and SHA-256 (read-only); `POST /projects/:id/migrations` applies exactly the reviewed commit/files after a Time Travel bookmark is saved (`d1_restore_points`, migration 0008), Wrangler-compatible `d1_migrations` records, stop at first failure with D1's error; `POST /projects/:id/migrations/restore` restores a recorded point and journals the undo bookmark. `providerJSON`/`cloudflare` accept `{errorDetail, timeout}` (only D1 calls surface Cloudflare's message, bounded and sanitized). Unverified: whether D1's `/query` rolls back a failed multi-statement migration completely; the restore point is the guaranteed recovery path.

### Health checks after redeploys, promotions, and pushes — implemented, locally verified

`worker/health.ts`: `probeHomepage` (workers.dev homepage, no redirects, body not read; also used by the initial deployment), `trackHealth`, `advanceHealth`, `scheduledHealthChecks`. Migration 0007 adds `operation_health`. Checks at most every 20 seconds; a promotion first confirms `result.deployments[0]` serves the version; HTTP 2xx → `healthy`, third failed probe → `unhealthy` (no automatic rollback); `unknown` after 45 minutes or when project/account/Worker identity changes. Apps that disable workers.dev, require auth, or redirect the homepage report as unhealthy.

### Initial deployment recovery — implemented, locally verified

`worker/recovery.ts`: `POST /api/deployments/:id/reconcile` `{confirm:true}` for `needs_attention` jobs (owner, real session, same Cloudflare account and repository). Interrupted steps: `intent_create_worker` (adopt only a Worker created no earlier than 5 minutes before the job), `intent_connect_repository` (upsert → repeat), `intent_create_trigger` (adopt the single `Cloudcel <worker>` trigger; several → stop), `intent_start_build` (adopt the build for the trigger and commit), `intent_configure_previews` (repeat the idempotent PATCH), `finished` → repeat binding review and health check. Writes `recovery_note`, restarts the 45-minute window (`monitor_started_at`, migration 0006), resets the container startup window, and advances once. The cron never resumes a job on its own.

### Framework imports and resource consent — implemented, fixture-verified

Draft D1/R2/KV bindings (binding name only) are listed at inspection; deployment requires resource consent for the exact inspected commit; Wrangler creates them in the native build; Cloudcel records observed IDs and publishes no URL if a reviewed binding is missing, a provider ID is malformed, or an unreviewed D1/R2/KV/Queue/Dispatch/Vectorize/Hyperdrive binding appears. Astro and SvelteKit adapters use committed configuration. The deploy command originally passed `--yes`, which `wrangler deploy` 4.148 rejects; `tests/deploy-command.test.ts` now runs the exact commands against the installed Wrangler.

Verified Wrangler 4.148.0 facts (from `node_modules/wrangler/wrangler-dist/cli.js` and runs):

- `deploy` has no `--yes`; `--x-provision`/`--x-auto-create` are hidden global aliases, default true; `--no-autoconfig` is valid.
- Workers Builds expects the configuration name to match the connected Worker and sets `WRANGLER_CI_OVERRIDE_NAME`/`WRANGLER_CI_MATCH_TAG`; `--name` wins, so Cloudcel's `--name` matches and avoids Cloudflare's rename pull request.
- Auto-created resources are named `<script name>-<binding lowercased, _→->`; CI runs never write IDs back; later deploys inherit bindings from the Worker's settings.
- R2 bucket names must match `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{1,61}[a-z0-9]$`; generated Worker names are shortened when R2 bindings exist.
- Astro's build adds `SESSION` (KV) and `IMAGES` bindings. SvelteKit 3 configures the adapter in the Vite plugin; automatic setup leaves no adapter. Next.js setup uses OpenNext and binds `WORKER_SELF_REFERENCE` to the configured name.
- Containers: default container application name is `<config name>-<class>` (lowercased), independent of `--name`; Dockerfile images are built (platform linux/amd64) even with `--dry-run`; `versions upload` deploys no images; Wrangler refuses to reuse an application bound to a different Durable Object namespace.

**Credential safety:** a saved Wrangler config file exists at `~/Library/Preferences/.wrangler/config/default.toml`; it has never been read. All verification uses `scripts/offline-env.mjs` (temporary `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, invalid placeholder `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`). Keep that for any future probe.

## User intent and working rules

The user wants a Vercel-style platform using customers' own Cloudflare accounts: connect GitHub, deploy automatically, manage infrastructure cheaply, and offer a Free plan. The full product scope includes React, full-stack SaaS, Next.js, JavaScript, Python, containers, WordPress, and as much of Cloudflare's stack as practical. Do not silently narrow the product. Supabase-style backend capabilities were discussed; they are not implemented merely because D1 bindings exist.

Standing instructions: "Go ahead, ensure its fully complete this time before stopping" and "go ahead with the next items, ensure nothing is left before you stop." Finish authorized local work fully and verify it before stopping; be candid about fixture-versus-live evidence. Later requests (8–12 Oct) are listed one by one in "Session record"; the most recent: "connect one and everything should work" (setup lives on Connections, never on deploy pages), "Save all we did, and every detail and everything you told me into handoff and including from memory as well" (12 Oct), and "check vercel cli, the github login and everything setup is super simple and happens within the cli as well. We need the same" (12 Oct, the newest slice). See also "Working preferences".

- Open-source question (unanswered decision): recommendation given — one codebase (open-core only if paid hosted features appear), no separate fork; before publishing remove/rewrite internal agent docs (`HANDOFF.md`, `AGENTS.md`), scrub local paths in `docs/framework-verification*` and `.claude/launch.json`, add LICENSE (AGPL-3.0 or Apache-2.0, the user's choice), SECURITY.md, and self-hosting docs; publish after one successful live deployment. Publishing is the user's action.
- "Is it a complete, cheap Vercel and Supabase alternative?" Answer given: not yet — a locally verified alpha with no live deployment; costs are low by design (customers pay Cloudflare directly) but not capped. Since then sign-in (passkeys, GitHub, Google) and realtime channels were added; there is still no data API, row-level security, email/password sign-in, or Postgres (D1 is SQLite). Keep docs and UI equally candid.
- Earlier request: include sign-in and realtime "built around cloudflare"; present the front page as a Vercel alternative without naming it, "from hobby to enterprise"; Cloudcel is a no-code provider for setting up and managing the Cloudflare side.
- Latest request (8 Oct 2026): "Ensure we have all features of vercel and supabase and netlify and we are 100% free alternative to them", plus Netlify's start options (AI agent, Git with PR previews, terminal deploy, drag and drop, one-click rollback). Decisions the user made when asked: (1) truthful "free" wording plus a Free-only mode, no "100% free" claim; (2) AI building through the user's own agent via a Cloudcel MCP server and CLI (Cloudcel runs no model); (3) CLI and drag-and-drop deploys go into the user's own Cloudflare account (no anonymous hosting in Cloudcel's account). Then: "dont create so many sub agents and continue yourself" — do the work directly and sequentially.
- Use relevant skills (ECC plugin, superpowers) when they apply; read a skill before applying it.
- Do not send external messages or publish anything. Live cloud changes so far are limited to the owner-authorized demo (`hello-cloudcel`) and the owner's own actions (see "Owner's live setup"); ask before anything else in their account. Do not commit or push unless asked (all files are untracked; no commit exists).
- No provider secrets in chat, browser responses, logs, exports, or committed files.
- Credentials/domain/ownership cannot be invented. Keep making independent local progress while waiting for configuration.

## Workspace and starting commands

Root: `/Users/zayaan_1/Downloads/Cloudcel` (macOS, zsh). Git is initialized, but **all project files are untracked** and no commit exists. Do not reset or clean the checkout. From the owner's Parallels Ubuntu VM the same folder is `/media/psf/Home/Downloads/Cloudcel`: never run `npm ci` or `npm install` there (its `node_modules` holds the Mac's binaries, which the owner's dev server uses), and never open `.wrangler/state` or `.dev.vars` from the VM. Work in the VM like this: edit in the shared folder; `rsync -a --delete --exclude node_modules --exclude .wrangler --exclude dist --exclude test-results --exclude .git --exclude .dev.vars /media/psf/Home/Downloads/Cloudcel/ ~/cloudcel-verify/`; run the commands below in `~/cloudcel-verify` with `PATH=$HOME/.local/cloudcel-toolchain/node/bin:$PATH` (browser tests with `CI=1`, which uses Playwright's Chromium); copy generated outputs back (`public/`, `packages/cli/dist/`), and after `prettier --write` in the copy, copy those files back too. git is installed in the VM since 13 Oct (2.43), but nothing is committed. Mutation checks rewrite files in the copy: don't rsync into it while one runs.

```sh
cd /Users/zayaan_1/Downloads/Cloudcel
npm run db:migrate
npm run dev
# in another terminal
npm run check
npm test
npm run build
npm run test:browser
npm run setup:check
npm run verify:frameworks        # credential-free dry runs, ~5 minutes; container fixtures need Docker running
npm run verify:kits              # needs npm run dev running

npm run cli:build                # bundle the CLI and pack public/cli/cloudcel.tgz (dev and build run it)
```

Node 22.13 and npm 10.9 were used; dependencies are installed. Docker 29.4.0 (OrbStack) is available locally. Playwright uses installed Google Chrome locally, Chromium in CI. Local URLs: public site `http://127.0.0.1:5173/`, dashboard `http://127.0.0.1:5173/app/`, docs `http://127.0.0.1:5173/docs/` (use `127.0.0.1`, never `localhost`). Since 12 Oct the owner starts the server in their own terminal (`npm ci && npm run db:migrate && npm run dev`); earlier it ran as the agent's background task and stopped whenever the session or app restarted. Playwright reuses a running server or starts its own. `npm run verify:frameworks -- <fixture>` rewrites `docs/framework-verification.json` with only that fixture; finish with a full run.

Local D1 persists in `.wrangler/state`; retain it (it holds the owner's live connections). Migrations **0001–0031 are applied locally on the Mac (0029–0031 by the owner on 13 Oct 2026)** (local only). The VM's copy has its own local database and no connections. Tests apply every migration to in-memory SQLite. Never rewrite an applied migration; add a new one.

## Architecture and files

React 19 / TypeScript / Vite 8 dashboard, Hono API on Cloudflare Workers, D1 metadata. Wrangler 4.148.0 and the Cloudflare Vite plugin. Zod, fflate, jsonc-parser. A pinned sharp 0.35.5 override resolves a tooling advisory.

- `src/App.tsx`: navigation, project creation/details, starter configuration/export, catalog/guide (copy derives counts from the catalog).
- `src/Integrations.tsx`: sign-in, connections, repository inspection/deployment (resource and container consent), recovery, branch previews, plan page.
- `src/Operations.tsx`: Manage panel (resources, secrets, domains, builds & versions with health, operation history with Check Cloudflare, Database tab with migrations, restore points, backups, and environment picker, and the Sign-in & realtime tab). Exports `ConfirmOperation` (returns focus to its opener).
- `src/Backend.tsx`: Manage → Sign-in & realtime. `src/Previews.tsx`: branch previews. `src/Upload.tsx`: drag-and-drop deploys. `src/Developers.tsx`: API & CLI (tokens, install commands, Vercel command table). `src/CliSetup.tsx` (+ `cli-setup.css`): the pages the CLI opens, `#cli-login?code=…` and `#cli-setup`, rendered without the dashboard (GitHub sign-in, approving the CLI, connecting Cloudflare). `src/Agents.tsx`: AI agents. `src/BuildSetup.tsx`: Connections' Cloudflare GitHub-app row and build-token row (pre-filled Cloudflare token link). `src/RepositoryPicker.tsx`: GitHub repository picker. `src/ProviderSetup.tsx`: local GitHub App / Cloudflare OAuth client setup and **Update Cloudflare sign-in**. `src/Team.tsx`, `src/Storage.tsx`, `src/DataBrowser.tsx`, `src/Observability.tsx`, `src/BuildVariables.tsx`, `src/Notifications.tsx`, `src/PreviewAccess.tsx`: the Team page and Manage tabs of those names.
- `worker/tokens.ts` (API tokens, CLI device login), `worker/uploads.ts` (direct uploads, publish/unpublish), `worker/local-deploys.ts` (apps deployed from a computer: the review and the sessions), `worker/wrangler-api.ts` (Wrangler's calls during such a deploy, checked and passed on to Cloudflare), `worker/wrangler-policy.ts` (what an upload may contain), `shared/local-deploy.ts` (the files a review reads, the pinned Wrangler version), `worker/mcp.ts` (MCP server), `shared/assets.ts` (asset hashing, paths, limits, content types).
- `packages/cli/` (the `shipcel` CLI, `cloudcel` until 23 Oct 2026, 0.4.0: `src/core.ts` what every command is built from (`Io`, arguments, the saved sign-in, the API client), `src/cli.ts` the deploy flows and dispatch with injected I/O, `src/app.ts` apps with code (the files a review reads, which Wrangler runs and how), `src/commands.ts` everyday commands (dev, env pull, crons, rolling-release, metrics, curl, api, mcp, agent, upgrade), `src/site.ts` what a project builds (`detect`, package manager, output folder), `src/git.ts` Git remotes from `.git/config`, `src/main.ts` process wiring including `Io.run`, the shell a build runs in), bundled and packed by `scripts/build-cli.mjs`.
- `shared/site-presets.ts`: the framework presets for sites without a Wrangler configuration (`detectSite`, `parseManifest`, `siteFiles`, `lockfiles`, `serverFrameworks`), read by both `worker/repository-plan.ts` and the CLI. No dependencies; imported with its `.ts` extension so Node's type stripping (the verify scripts) resolves it.
