## Native pipeline behavior

`POST /projects/:id/repository` checks the user's write permission and App access, pins a commit, reads package/config/lock files (plus `open-next.config.ts`, `pyproject.toml`/`uv.lock`, or Dockerfiles for those runtimes) at that commit, and returns the plan: adapter, build command, resources, containers, Worker name (for adapters that keep it), warnings, issues. Existing resource IDs/names, routes, environments, and TOML/programmatic configuration are rejected; containers only under Docker / custom runtime.

`POST /projects/:id/deployments` requires a real session, active grants and selected account, the native switch, a build token of the selected account, a UUID idempotency key, charge consent, `inspectedCommit` equal to the branch head, `confirmResources` when resources are listed, and `confirmContainers` when containers are listed. One production pipeline per project. Worker names: the owner's chosen app name (UI default: the project name), the configured name for Next.js and containers, or a generated `cc-<project>-<id12>` when the API receives none; unique per account and never adopted. The account must have a workers.dev subdomain (claimable from Cloudcel).

Steps: queued → placeholder Worker (503) → repository connection → production trigger → pinned initial build → build outcome → binding review (record reviewed IDs; stop on missing/unreviewed) → homepage health check (containers: up to 15 minutes) → deployed with URL. Preview jobs add `configure_previews` after their first build. Failed or ambiguous provider mutations stop with `needs_attention` and are never retried automatically; owners resume through recovery. Leases prevent duplicate execution; membership, project, repository access, grants, and account are rechecked. Monitoring stops after 45 minutes.

Disconnect removes local ciphertext and stops pending Cloudcel jobs; apps and native triggers remain. Upstream grant revocation and transparent key rotation are unimplemented.

## Project operations

Routes under `/api/projects/:id`: `operations` (history with health), `operations/:operationId/health`, `operations/:operationId/reconcile`, `cloud` (bindings, values stripped), `secrets` (names/set/remove, no value storage or echo), `versions` (with each version's live share), `deployment` (the deployment serving traffic), `rollback` (owned-version promotion, `force=false`) and `traffic` (a two-version split), `builds` and `redeploy` (commit-pinned, renewed GitHub authorization), `domains`/`zones` (unused-hostname preflight, attach/detach for the recorded Worker), `migrations`, `migrations/restore`, `databases/:binding/export`, `databases/:binding/restore`. Shared target checks require a workspace owner, real session, matching account grant, and the recorded Worker name **and immutable tag**; writes need the native switch and no running initial setup. Mutations are journaled first; one running operation per project (SQL); request IDs replay recorded results; uncertain results become `needs_attention` and can be checked with Check Cloudflare.

Production follow-up: verify DTOs and OAuth scopes against real responses; disconnect cannot cancel an in-flight provider request; domain preflight cannot exclude a simultaneous dashboard change; redacted build logs are not implemented.

## Missing external configuration and pending user answer

Locally (9 Oct 2026), `npm run setup:check` reports the required configuration present: GitHub and Cloudflare were set up from Connections (stored encrypted in local D1, not in `.dev.vars`), and native resource creation is on in the ignored `.dev.vars`. A hosted Cloudcel still needs its own `APP_ORIGIN`, encryption key and provider apps. The Wrangler D1 ID is an all-zero local placeholder.

Pending from the owner (29 Oct 2026):

- **The Cloudflare OAuth client's permissions** (Hyperdrive Write, record 112): the 29 Oct `--cloudflare-permissions` run ended "No OAuth client in the accounts that token sees sends people back to https://shipcel.com/api/auth/cloudflare/callback; nothing changed", so the token has to be made on the account that owns the `shipcel` Worker, with OAuth Client Read and Write; then Connections → connect Cloudflare again.
- **A sign-in by email with the owner's own inbox** (record 112; the send to a sink address was accepted by Cloudflare's Email Service).
- **A push from the Mac** of `6ddb269` and the commits after it (record 115); record 113 itself is already live.

Resolved since 22 Oct: the product's name (Shipcel, record 73), hosting (Cloudflare; shipcel.com on Workers Paid), the Cloudflare sign-in app and the GitHub App under the owner's accounts (Connections work live, record 99), the Bittensor light client (stopped after its diagnostic, record 89; the owner said to skip it).

For a hosted Cloudcel, the Cloudflare sign-in app must also become public: permanent, and it needs the client URL's domain verified with a DNS TXT record.

Cloudflare's Builds docs require user-scoped API access with a separate build-token UUID plus the customer's one-time Cloudflare GitHub App installation. Listing build tokens worked live with the OAuth grant (8 Oct 2026); creating triggers and starting builds with it is **unverified** until the first GitHub deployment. If OAuth is not accepted for those, implement a supported integration path rather than declaring it working.

## Remaining scope

- **The roadmap's items 4–11** (record 107; the order and the slices are in "Current checkpoint" above): Next.js previews and fork pull-request previews; a container kit and a service catalog on Containers; WordPress (external MySQL through Hyperdrive, durable uploads, plugins and themes, cron, mail, updates, restore tests; never a fake "Deploy WordPress" action); GitLab, Bitbucket and Gitea; scheduled backups to R2; Telegram and email notifications and shared variables; row-level security, data APIs and change streams; the CLI on npm (the name was unregistered on 8 Oct 2026) and a Coolify comparison page.
- **Owner-side, open:** the Cloudflare permission for Hyperdrive, a real emailed sign-in, a real MCP connector (above).
- **Next.js previews are blocked upstream:** OpenNext's `WORKER_SELF_REFERENCE` is a service binding, which a Preview would point at production; Cloudflare's answer is `ctx.exports`, which OpenNext 1.20.14 does not use (record 111). Until it does, Shipcel refuses them with the reason.
- **CLI follow-ups, not built:** attaching Workers Builds to a Worker first deployed from the computer; from the computer, Python Workers with packages, `wrangler.toml`, containers and OpenNext caches; static exports of Next.js, SvelteKit, Nuxt and Angular.
- **Previews:** cleanup of Previews whose branch is gone (Cloudflare evicts the least recently deployed at 100 per Worker on Free); previews from forks (roadmap 4).
- **Not started, lower priority:** build-time variables, redacted build logs, build cancellation; Queues, Workflows, AI and other Cloudflare products; upstream OAuth revocation, key rotation and an audit trail; user export and import for sign-in; paid billing (Shipcel is free as a launch offer; every price on the site is Cloudflare's).
- **Design:** a fresh critique after the roadmap's next slices; the hallmark stamps on the stylesheets record the last polish.
